Libsyn Help Guide

The official Libsyn help documentation, for both Libsyn 5 and Libsyn Classic

2026-08-02

Libsyn Help Guide

This guide gathers the official Libsyn help documentation, as it stood on 2 August 2026.

Libsyn publishes two help centres side by side, because it runs two generations of its platform. Libsyn 5 is the current one; Libsyn Classic is the older one, still supported and still documented. Their instructions are not interchangeable, so every category here names the platform it belongs to. Following a Classic procedure in Libsyn 5 would send you looking for buttons that are not there.

How this was gathered, and why that is worth saying. Libsyn’s rendered help pages refuse automated reading – every one of them answered with a refusal. But Libsyn’s own publishing system offers the same articles through a listing, and that listing answered without complaint. The text here is Libsyn’s, complete, taken from Libsyn: title, section and body exactly as published. All 229 articles arrived with their text.

The arrangement is Libsyn’s own. The categories are the section names Libsyn files its articles under, with the platform added at the front. Sections holding a single article have been folded into Miscellaneous, since a category of one is not a category.

Cross-references between articles have been resolved into links within this document, by Libsyn’s own article identifiers, so following a “see also” never leaves the file.

Illustrations do not survive the conversion. Libsyn’s articles lean on screenshots for some steps, and where a step says to click an icon, the picture of that icon is not here. The wording of each step is unchanged.

Libsyn 5: Connecting Your Show to Podcast Destinations

About the Advanced Options for a Destination Feed

To get your show to appear in a destination feed, you need to connect it to the destination through your Libsyn account. See Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to a Destination Feed.

When setting up your feed, you can click Show More located to the right of the Advanced Options header to expand the Advanced Options section. You can then choose from the following fields:

  • Title Override:

    Enter a show title in this field only if you want to display a different show title for this destination than what displays in the Title field in your Podcast Settings.

    • See About Your Podcast Settings.

  • Author Override:

    Enter an author name in this field only if you want to display a different author for this destination than what displays in the Author field in your Podcast Settings.   

    • See About Your Podcast Settings.

  • Description Override:

    Enter a show description in this field only if you want to display a different show description for this destination than what displays in the Description field in your Podcast Settings. 

    • See About Your Podcast Settings.

  • Extra RSS Tags:

    Enter any additional channel-level tags you want to include for this destination.

    • This is a power user-only feature that has the potential to break your feed. Only enter valid RSS tags as directed, and only as needed.

  • Use Show Image:

    Select this check box to use your show artwork instead of your episode artwork for episodes published to this destination.

    • This check box does not display for the Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed destination, because Apple does not currently support using the show artwork at the episode level.

  • Use Plain Text Description:

    Select this check box to remove all formatting from the episode descriptions for this destination.

    • HTML tags will be stripped and  and  tags will be converted into line breaks.
    • This helps ensure your RSS feed is fully valid and is typically only used if you are encountering specific issues and errors with your feed.

Claim Your Show in Spotify

You can manage your Spotify listing and get additional metrics on Spotify’s Spotify for Creators site if you claim your show in Spotify directly.

Note

Spotify only allows you to claim your show in Spotify for Creators if it is distributed via RSS feed. If you enable Spotify Video without claiming your show in Spotify for Creators, you cannot access analytics or other features in Spotify for Creators.

To claim your show in Spotify for Creators and enable Spotify Video in Libsyn: 

  1. Publish an audio episode to your Libsyn feed.
  2. Claim your Spotify show. 
  3. Enroll in Spotify Video after your show appears in your Spotify for Creators dashboard. See Enroll in Spotify Video with Libsyn.

 

To claim your show in Spotify for Creators:

  1. Click the icon in the top right corner of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations to display the Destinations page.  

  3. In the row for Spotify, click Manage to display the Spotify Destination Settings page.  

  4. Under FeedURL, click the icon to copy your Spotify-specific feed URL to your clipboard.

     

  5. As you follow the steps in the Spotify Help Center article Claiming your podcast in Spotify for Creators, paste the URL you just copied into the relevant field on Spotify’s application.    

Note

To learn more about your RSS feed, see Understand Your RSS Feed.

Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to a Destination Feed

To get your show to appear in a destination feed, you need to connect it to the destination through your Libsyn account.  

Note

Before you can connect to a destination feed, you must do the following:

  • Enter all the required information entered in your show settings. See About Your Podcast Settings.
  • Publish at least one episode. See Publish an Episode.

To connect your show to a destination:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations to display the Destinations page.  

  3. The following destinations are connected by default, but you can customize them by clicking the Manage button:

    • Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed: See Customize Your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed.
    • Spotify: See Customize Your Spotify Feed.
    • Website: See Set Up Your Libsyn Podcast Website.

    If your podcast isn’t already available on Apple Podcasts and/or Spotify, and you want it to be, you will still need to manually submit your show to those destinations.  

  4. Click Connect in the row for a destination to add it.  

  5. Complete the information for the new destination.

    • For WordPress, see Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to WordPress.

    • For Blogger OnPublish, see Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to Blogger.

    • For iHeartRadio, Deezer, Amazon Music/Audible, Gaana, Player FM, JioSaavn, or Boomplay, complete the following:

      • Feed URL: By default, your Feed URL ends in the name of the destination, but you can change this until you save for the first time. After you have saved your destination, the URL changes to a link. Click the icon to copy it to your clipboard.
      • Country of Origin: If this field displays, select the country where you produce your podcast.
      • Category: If this field displays, select the category you want to use for your episodes on this destination.
      • Advanced Options: Click Show More to expand. See About the Advanced Options for a Destination Feed.
      • Approve: Select this check box to confirm that you agree to the terms of service.
      • Terms of Service : Click this link to review the terms of service.

       

  6. Click Submit.

After the destination has approved your show, it will appear as a publication destination when you publish a new episode.

Customize Your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed

When you set up your show in Libsyn, all the information you enter becomes part of your main RSS feed. This feed is called the Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed, and it’s used by directories like Apple Podcasts to list your show in their app and publish your episodes.

Note

To learn more about RSS feeds, see Understand Your RSS Feed.

However, the information you enter when you set up your show is also used in the other publication destinations that you set up in Libsyn, such as Spotify, Deezer, iHeart Radio, and more. Customizing your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed gives you the option to change some of the information in your RSS feed without affecting any of the other destinations that are using the information you entered when you set up your show.

For example, if you want a directory like Apple Podcasts to display a different show description than the one you entered in show settings, you can update the Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed. Doing so affects Apple Podcasts (and any other shows that use the Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed), but not the rest of your publication destinations. 

If you have more than one show, remember to select the right one before you make changes. Then, to update your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations.  

  3. In the Destinations table, click Manage in the row for Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed.

  4.  You can click the icon to copy your Feed URL to your clipboard.

    Note

    You can also copy your Feed URL to your clipboard by clicking in the row for Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed in the Destinations table.

  5. Click Show More located to the right of Advanced Options to enter any advanced options for this destination. See About the Advanced Options for a Destination Feed.  

  6. Click Submit to save your changes.

Customize Your Spotify Feed

Millions of people listen to podcasts on Spotify, making it a great place to grow your show’s audience. When you set up your Libsyn account, a Spotify feed is automatically created for you, but you can customize it.

Note

To use your Spotify RSS feed, you must have the following information entered in your show settings: Title, Description, Author, System Language, Category One, and Show Artwork. For information on updating your show settings, see About Your Podcast Settings.

To customize your Spotify feed:

  1. Click theicon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations.  

  3. In the Destinations table, click Manage in the row for Spotify to display the Spotify Configuration page.

    Note

    If you are on the Advanced Plan, or higher, you have the option to activate Spotify video distribution by selecting the Enable Spotify Video checkbox at the top of the page. See Enroll in Spotify Video with Libsyn.

     

  4. You can click the icon to copy your Feed URL to your clipboard.  

  5. Review the Country of Origin field and, if necessary, update it to the country where you produce your podcast.  

  6. Click Show More located to the right of Advanced Options to enter any advanced options for this destination. See About the Advanced Options for a Destination Feed.

    Important

    If you add information in the RSS Feed Overrides section, Spotify uses that information to replace the information in your podcast settings. If you ever change your title, authors, or description in podcast settings, those changes will not show up in Spotify as long as there is information in the RSS Feed Override fields.  

     

  7. Review the Terms and Conditions, and select the Approve checkbox to confirm that you agree to Spotify’s terms of service.  

  8. Click Submit.

Note

You can manage your Spotify listing and get additional metrics from Spotify for Creators if you claim your show in Spotify directly. For help, see Claim Your Show in Spotify.

Edit or Delete Your Show’s Connection to a Destination Feed

To edit or delete your show’s connection to a destination:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations to display the Destinations page.  

  3. Click Manage in the row for the destination you want to change.

     

  4. Make your changes on the edit screen.

    • To edit your connection to this destination, change any selectable field and then click Submit. For details about the fields, see Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to a Destination Feed.

    • To delete your connection to this destination, clickand then click Yes to confirm.

      Notes

      • Your show and your previous episode list may still be visible at that destination until they remove your show listing, although your audio may not play. To reduce this timeline, please contact the destination directly to request manual removal on their end.
      • For instructions on reconnecting the destination, see Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to a Destination Feed.

Enroll in Spotify Video with Libsyn

Libsyn supports video distribution to Spotify, allowing you to upload and publish video episodes directly through your Libsyn account. 

Once you’re enrolled in Spotify Video with Libsyn:

  • Your show will be distributed to Spotify through an API instead of through an RSS feed. 
  • Your RSS feed continues to distribute your audio episodes to other podcast destinations as normal.

 

NoteSpotify Video is available on the Advanced Plan and higher. You must be the show owner to enroll. Your show must also meet Spotify’s brand safety check to be eligible for video distribution.

To enroll in Spotify Video with Libsyn:

  1. Click the icon at the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations.  

  3. In the Destinations table, if you see the icon in the row for Spotify, click Manage to display the Spotify Configuration page.  

  4. On the Spotify Configuration page, select the Enable Spotify Video check box:

     

  5. Click Submit.

 

About Your Enrollment

Once enabled, your Spotify Configuration page will update to show that Spotify Video is enabled for your show. 

Once Spotify Video is enabled:

  • You can begin publishing video episodes through Libsyn. See Publishing to Spotify Video.
  • You can view streaming data for your episodes in Spotify. See View Spotify Statistics in Libsyn.

Now that your show is enabled for Spotify Video, you’re eligible to apply for monetization through the Spotify Partner Program. Click Enroll Now to apply.

Find the URL to Your RSS Feed

RSS is an acronym for “really simple syndication,” and your RSS feed allows people to access your show information on any platform that you plug your feed into. For example, Spotify and Apple Podcasts use your Libsyn RSS feed to play your episodes. Without your RSS feed, no one could access your show. See Understand Your RSS Feed.

To find and copy the URL for your main RSS feed:

  1. Sign in to Libsyn and — if you’re not already there — click Dashboard at the top of your Libsyn account to display the Dashboard page. 

  2. Scroll down to the Quick Links section. 

  3. Click the icon next to Apple Podcasts / Libsyn ClassicFeed to copy your Feed URL to your clipboard.

Submit Your Show to Apple Podcasts

Publishing your episodes to Apple Podcasts exposes your show to a large global audience, with potential listeners in countries all over the world. For your episodes to appear in Apple Podcasts, you must submit your show to Apple Podcasts for approval.

Plan the Timing of Your Submission

Libsyn recommends that you begin the submission process a full month before the date you want to launch your show on Apple Podcasts. The reason is that, after you submit, Apple can take up to 24 hours to approve your show. Then, it can take another week after that before your show becomes fully searchable in their store. Also, if you are publishing to any apps that pull your information from Apple Podcasts, it could take a full month for those apps to get access to your show from Apple.

It’s also important to consider that, if your show gets rejected by Apple for any reason, submitting a month ahead of your launch date gives you enough time to address issues and resubmit.

Prepare for Submission

Before you submit, set up your show in Libsyn so that your RSS feed works properly. Apple uses the RSS feed generated by Libsyn to make your episodes available to your audience on the app.

Note

You will use your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed to connect to Apple Podcasts. Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed is your main RSS feed. If you want any information in that feed to be different than what you entered in your show settings, you can customize your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed.

After you set up your show, make sure to publish your first episodes. You need to publish at least one episode (either a trailer or a full episode) but it is a good idea to provide a few. It’s necessary to provide some content because Apple reviews it to make sure it meets their content guidelines.

When your show is set and you have at least one episode published, you’re ready to submit to Apple Podcasts.

Submit Your Show

To submit your show to Apple:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations.  

  3. In the Destinations table, click Manage in the row for Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed to display its settings page.

     

  4. Go to Advanced Options and click Show More to enter any advanced options for this destination. See About the Advanced Options for a Destination Feed.  

  5. In the Add Your Show to Apple Podcasts section, select one of the following options:

    • Submit Show:

      Select this option to submit your show to Apple Podcasts without using your own Apple ID.

      • Your feed URL will be submitted using Libsyn’s Apple ID.
      • You will be able to manage your feed completely through Libsyn, but you will not have access to analytics in Apple Podcasts Connect.
      • You won’t be able to offer subscription content through Apple Podcasts Subscriptions while this option is selected. 
      • Only submit here if your podcast is not listed in Apple Podcasts. Submitting more than once may result in duplicate listings.
      • You can reset this if you later change your mind and want to submit your show manually so you can have subscriber content. See Set Up Apple Podcasts Subscriptions.

    • Link to Existing Show:

      Select this option to submit your show to Apple Podcasts using your own Apple ID. 

      • Your individual feed will be in Apple Podcasts Connect, and you will have access to analytics there.
      • You will need your own Apple ID to submit subscriber audio to Apple Podcasts through Libsyn. See Set Up Apple Podcasts Subscriptions.

      To proceed:

      1. Click Copy Feed URL to copy your feed URL to your clipboard.
      2. Click Apple Podcasts Connect to navigate to the Apple Podcasts Connect website.
      3. On the Apple Podcasts Connect website, create an account and submit your feed.

     

  6. Click Submit to finalize your submission.

Once your show has been approved, Apple Podcasts will publish your episodes from your Libsyn feed when you publish your episodes. See Publish a Subscriber Episode to Apple Podcasts.  

Note

If you already have a show on Apple Podcasts but it’s not in your Apple Podcasts Connect account, you can claim your existing show by generating a token in your Apple Podcasts Connect account and then entering the token in the Apple Ownership Token field (also known as Claim Your Show in Apple Podcasts) in your Libsyn Podcast Settings.

Understand Your RSS Feed

“RSS” is an acronym for “really simple syndication.” Your show’s RSS feed is what makes it a podcast and differentiates it from other kinds of episodic shows, like a TV or a radio show. Unlike TV or radio shows, all podcasts are syndicated to an RSS feed.

For example, The Late Show with Stephen Colbert or Jimmy Kimmel Live! are episodic shows comprised of audio and video, just like some podcast shows that use both audio and video. However, Stephen Colbert and Jimmy Kimmel’s shows are not podcasts because they are not syndicated to an RSS feed.

Your RSS feed is an XML file that contains all the information about your show as well as all your episodes, including URLs to the place where your episodes are hosted, descriptions, artwork, and much more. Podcast directories, like Apple Podcasts, require a connection to your RSS feed so they can pull your information and make your episodes available to your listeners.

How Do I Set up and Manage My RSS Feed?

Libsyn creates your RSS feed based on the information and media you provide when you set up your show. That information becomes part of your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed, which is the main RSS feed that you’ll use to connect to places like Apple Podcasts. The information you enter when you set up your show is also used for all of the connections you set up in Libsyn, like connections to Spotify, Deezer, Facebook, and other destinations.

Even though your connections all draw initially from the information in your podcast settings, Libsyn offers plenty of flexibility if you want some information, like your show description, to show up differently in different directories. For example, if you want your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed to use a different description than the one you entered when setting up your show, you can customize it. This action is useful if you want your show’s description to be different in Apple Podcasts (and the other places that use the Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed) than in the rest of the podcast directories where you want to publish your episodes.

If necessary, you can also customize certain information for the publication destinations you set up in Libsyn, including places like Spotify, iHeartRadio, Deezer, and more. All you have to do is update the fields you want to change when you set those connections up, and Libsyn displays the new information only in those destinations. The fields where you specify different information for specific destinations are generally called RSS Feed Overrides.

Important

If you ever enter information in a destination’s RSS Feed Override fields, it is important to remember that you did so if you ever want to change your show title, authors, or show description in the future. Keep in mind that, if you ever change any of that information in your podcast settings, those changes will not show up in any destinations where there is information in the RSS Feed Override fields because these fields are designed to replace the information in your podcast settings.

Once you’ve set up your show settings and you’ve connected your show to directories, webpages, and social media, you’re all set. All of your RSS feeds, including your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed and the connections to destinations in Libsyn, are automatically updated with new episode information whenever you publish a new episode. Places like Apple Podcasts pull information from the updated feed, and your new shows will appear in all of the places you’ve set your show up to appear in.

Note

To find the link to your RSS feed, see Find the URL to Your RSS Feed.

Libsyn 5: Connecting Your Show to Social Media Sites

Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to Facebook

With its billions of users worldwide, publishing your episodes to Facebook is a great way to promote your show. Before you can publish to Facebook, you must connect your show to Facebook in Libsyn.

Note

Facebook only supports the publication of podcast episodes to a page, not to personal profiles or groups. You can refer to Facebook’s documentation about the difference between a page, a profile, and a group to get more information about Facebook pages and how to set one up.

To connect your show to Facebook:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Social.

  3. Click Add under the Facebook logo to open Facebook OnPublish. (OnPublish is a Libsyn publishing feature that publishes your episodes directly to Facebook.)

  4. Click Sign in with Facebook to open the Facebook page where you can connect your Libsyn account.

  5. Complete one of the two following actions:

    • a.: If you’re already logged in to Facebook in the browser you’re using, a window appears asking if you want to continue with that account. Click Continue as… to use the account you’re logged in to or click Log into another account to switch to a different account.
    • b.: If you’re not logged in to Facebook, a Log Into Facebook page opens. Enter your email and password and click Log In. Then, click Continue as… to use your account.

     

  6. In the What Pages do you want to use with Libsyn? window, select all the Facebook pages you want to publish episodes to and then click Next.

    Note

    Only pages appear in the list, not personal profiles or groups. If you don’t have a page, you can click Create a Page to create one and then return to this process.

     

  7. In the What is Libsyn allowed to do? window, make sure all the options are switched to Yes to ensure Libsyn has the access it needs to publish your episodes to your Facebook page.

  8. Click Done.

  9. In the You’ve now linked Libsyn to Facebook window, click OK. You now return to the Facebook OnPublish form in Libsyn.

  10. Select the check mark for the pages in the Pages section to which you want to publish episodes.

  11. In the Publish Link field, select what kind of link you want to use to share your episode with your listeners in your Facebook post. Link options include the following:

    • Directory Link: Select this option to use a link that points to your episode as it exists in the Libsyn podcast directory.
    • Episode Permalink: Select this option to use the link that you specify as the episode permalink when you publish your episode. For more information about an episode’s permalink, see About Your Episode Details.
    • Download URL: Select this option to let users download your episode to their devices.

     

  12. Click Save.

    Note

    You must use the Social Destinations calendar to publish to Facebook if you are on the Advanced Plan, or higher. See Schedule an Episode to Appear on Social Media Using the Social Destinations Calendar.

Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to LinkedIn

Using Libsyn, you can set up your show to automatically publish new episodes with a link to your LinkedIn page. 

To connect your show to LinkedIn:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Social.

  3. Click Add under the LinkedIn logo to open LinkedIn OnPublish. (OnPublish is a Libsyn publishing feature that publishes your episodes directly to LinkedIn.)

  4. In the Connect field, click the Sign In button to open the LinkedIn page where you can connect your Libsyn account.

  5. Sign in to LinkedIn with the account you wish to use. You now return to the LinkedIn OnPublish form in Libsyn.

  6. In the Post to Personal Profile field, select Yes or No.

  7. In the Publish Link field, select what kind of link you want to use to share your episode with your listeners in your LinkedIn post. Link options include the following:

    • Directory Link: Select this option to use a link that points to your episode as it exists in the Libsyn podcast directory.
    • Episode Permalink: Select this option to use the link that you specify as the episode permalink when you publish your episode. For more information about an episode’s permalink, see About Your Episode Details.
    • Download URL: Select this option to let users download your episode to their devices.

     

  8. Click Save.

    Note

    You must use the Social Destinations calendar to publish to LinkedIn if you are on the Advanced Plan, or higher. See Schedule an Episode to Appear on Social Media Using the Social Destinations Calendar.

Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to YouTube

Libsyn can automatically publish your audio or video files to YouTube. In the case of a video file, the file is sent straight to YouTube. In the case of an audio file, Libsyn will use your artwork combined with your audio file to generate a video and push that out to YouTube.

Note

Before you begin, ensure you have verified the YouTube account to which you want us to publish so they will accept videos longer than 15 minutes. For more details visit YouTube’s help page on the topic. You can verify your channel by making sure you are using YouTube as that channel user, then access their verify page.

 

To connect your Libsyn account to your YouTube account:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Social.

  3. Click Add under the YouTube logo.

  4. Click Sign In to open the Google page where you can connect your Libsyn account.

  5. On the Libsyn wants to access your Google Account page, click Allow to link your account and approve all the permissions Libsyn needs to publish your episodes to your YouTube account. After you authorize Libsyn to publish to YouTube, you will return to the YouTube form in Libsyn.

    • The name of your YouTube account now displays under the Connect field, and some additional fields display in the form.

  6. In the YouTube Category field, select the category you want to use for your episodes on YouTube.

  7. Optionally, you can change the following fields.

    • Convert Audio to Video:

      Leave this set to Yes if you want Libsyn to convert your audio files to .MP4 video files and upload them to YouTube. Otherwise, only your video episodes will be uploaded.

      Video file formats that both Libsyn and YouTube accept:

      • .MP4
      • .AVI
      • .FLV
      • .MOV
      • .WMV

    • Copy Episode Keywords: Leave this set to Yes unless you don’t want to copy any keywords associated with the episode to YouTube.

    • Set Premium Episodes to Private on YouTube: Leave this set to Yes unless you want all users to be able to access your premium episodes on YouTube.

    • Set Visibility for YouTube:

      • Leave this set to Public if you want anyone to be able to view or share your videos and if you want them to be posted on your YouTube channel and show up in search results. 
      • Select Private if you want your videos to be visible only to you and the people associated with the email addresses you enter on YouTube.
      • Select Unlisted if you want your videos to be seen and shared by anyone with the link but you do not want them to be posted on your YouTube channel or show up in search results.

      For more details, see YouTube’s Help. 

    • Add Artwork:

      You can upload a playlist image here.

      • This image will be used as the playlist thumbnail if you publish your episodes to a YouTube playlist.
      • If there is no image here, your show artwork will be used as the playlist thumbnail if you publish your episodes to a YouTube playlist.

      The image requirements are:

      • 1400 by 1400 px (minimum)
      • 3000 by 3000 px (maximum)
      • Must be square
      • Under 2MB
      • Use sRGB color space
      • JPG or PNG files only

    • Default Playlist:

      Displays the playlist(s) that you have created on YouTube. Select a playlist if you want to automatically publish your episodes to this playlist on YouTube.

      • This playlist will automatically be set as a Podcast Playlist on YouTube, and the episodes in this playlist will automatically be submitted to YouTube Music.
      • If you do not select a playlist, your episodes will automatically publish to your YouTube channel without a playlist.
      • You can always make changes to your playlists from within your YouTube account.

  8. Click Save.

    The Social Settings page now displays “YouTube” followed by a check mark to indicate that your YouTube account is connected.

    Note

    If the check mark is ever replaced by a Warning icon, there is a problem with the connection from Libsyn to your YouTube account. Click to navigate to the YouTube Social Settings page. If an error displays to inform you that the Auth token has expired, click Sign In to reconnect your account and approve all the permissions, and then click Save to save your settings. If adjustments need to be made to the permissions selected, please reach out to for assistance.

Edit or Delete Your Show’s Connection to a Social Media Site

To edit or delete your show’s connection to a social media site such as Facebook, YouTube, or LinkedIn.

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Social to display the Social Settings page.  

  3. Click on the social media connection you want to edit or delete.

     

  4. Make your changes on the edit screen.

    • To edit your connection to this social media site, change any selectable field and then click Save. 

    • To delete your connection to this social media site, click and then click Yes to confirm. 

      Notes

      • Your future content will no longer be sent to this site. To remove your previous content, please visit the site directly.
      • For instructions on reconnecting the social media site, see Connecting Your Show to Social Media Sites.

Libsyn 5: Connecting Your Show to Websites

Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to Blogger

You can connect your show to Blogger, one of the world’s leading content management platforms. 

To do so:

  1. Click theicon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Destinations.

  3. In the Add New table, in the row for Blogger OnPublish, click Add to open the Blogger OnPublish page. (OnPublish is a Libsyn publishing feature that publishes your episodes directly to Blogger.)

  4. Click Sign in with Blogger.

  5. Choose your Google Account.

  6. Click Allow, so Libsyn can access your Google Account. The Blogger OnPublish page redisplays:

  7. Complete the following fields:

    • Link Text: Enter the name you want for the link that listeners can use to download your episode. For example, you can enter Check out this episode!
    • Labels: Choose genre labels for your podcast. For example, your podcast might focus on Crime, History, or Culture.
    • Publish Link: Use this field to specify which link you want to use to give your listeners access to your episode. Select either Directory Link, Episode Permalink, or Download URL.

     

  8. Click Save to save your changes. (By clicking Save you are also agreeing to the terms of service and Google’s Privacy Policy.)

    Blogger OnPublish displays in your list of destinations on the Destinations Settings page with a status of Connected. 

    Note

    If the Connected status ever changes to Update, there is a problem with the connection from Libsyn to your Blogger account. Click Edit to navigate to the Blogger OnPublish Destination Settings page. If an error displays to inform you that the Auth token has expired, click Sign in to reconnect your account and approve all the permissions, and then click Save to save your settings. If adjustments need to be made to the permissions selected, please reach out to  for assistance.

Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to WordPress

To publish your show’s episodes to WordPress, you need to connect your show to your WordPress account. After you do so, episodes you publish from Libsyn will automatically appear as blog posts in your WordPress website, letting you easily advertise and distribute your content to a wider audience.

Note that you can only publish to WordPress if you have a self-hosted WordPress site. However, because of the way certain web hosts operate, some WordPress websites won’t allow integration with Libsyn. If you’re unsure about whether your WordPress site works with Libsyn, contact our Support team.

 

Prepare Your WordPress Site to Connect to Libsyn

For your WordPress site to correctly accept posts from Libsyn, you need to ensure your web host uses XML-RPC, and you need to install the iFrame plugin.

What is XML-RPC?

XML-RPC is a common protocol that many blogging software products use to correctly exchange information with other services. This protocol is necessary to make sure that the information Libsyn sends when you publish an episode appears correctly on your WordPress site.

Some web hosts don’t allow XML-RPC for security reasons. In those cases, WordPress can’t connect to Libsyn. If you’re not sure whether your web host uses XML-RPC, check with our Support team.

The iFrame Plugin

iFrame is a WordPress plugin that ensures that the media player that plays your podcast episodes will work correctly in your WordPress posts. To install the iFrame plugin in WordPress:

  1. Log in to your WordPress administrative dashboard.

  2. In the pane on the left, click Plugins and then select Add New.

  3. In the Search plugins field in the upper, right-hand corner of the page, enter iFrame and press Enter on your keyboard. The iFrame plugin appears in the search results.

  4. Click Install Now. When the download is complete, the Install Now button changes to Activate.

  5. Click Activate.

Connect Your Show to WordPress

After you’ve confirmed that your web host uses XML-RPC, and you’ve installed the WordPress plugin, you can connect your Libsyn account to your WordPress account. To do so, log in to your Libsyn account and follow the steps below. 

Note

If you have more than one show, make sure you select the show you want to connect to WordPress before you start.

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Destinations.

  3. In the Add New table, in the row for WordPress, click Add to display the WordPress form.

  4. Enter the information for each field to connect your account and to specify how you want episodes to appear in WordPress. For more information about each field, see the Set up the Information for Your WordPress Connection section below.

  5. Click Save.

  6. If you have more than one WordPress site, repeat these steps to connect to your other  sites.

 

Congratulations! Your WordPress account is now connected to Libsyn, and the episodes you publish in Libsyn will automatically appear as blog posts in your WordPress site.

 

Set up the Information for Your WordPress Connection

The WordPress form asks for information to connect your Libsyn account to your WordPress account. The following sections explain the information you should enter for each field.

  • Username

    Enter the username you use to log in to your WordPress account.

  • Password

    Enter the password you use to log in to your WordPress account.

  • Blog Web Address

    Enter the final URL to your site in this field. In other words, do not enter the URL to your WordPress administrative dashboard or to a specific category or page on your site.

    The final URL is the URL that appears in your browser’s address bar when you navigate to your site. For example, if entering myawesomeshow.com in your browser’s address bar takes you to 0, then enter the latter in this field. Make sure the address appears exactly as it does in the address bar. For example, take note of small details, like whether the address appears as http or https.

  • Connect

    The Test Connection button in this section tests whether the login information you entered enables Libsyn to connect to your WordPress account.

    If the test says that the connection failed, check to make sure that you entered your username and password correctly. If they are correct and the test still fails, save the information and reach out to our Support team to help you figure out why the system is unable to connect to your website.

  • Link Text

    Enter the text that you want to appear in the link that listeners can use to download your episode. For example, you can enter Download this episode!

  • Categories

    Enter a category from your site that you want to use to house the posts that contain your podcast episodes.

    WordPress organizes posts by categories. Your connection to Libsyn creates posts whenever you publish an episode. Linking your posts to one of the categories you’ve defined on your site makes it easier for your listeners to find your episodes.

  • Publish As Draft

    This setting is automatically set so that any episodes you publish from Libsyn appear as drafts in your WordPress account. After you publish in Libsyn, you can log in to WordPress, review the post, and publish it at your convenience. This setting is useful because it lets you make changes to your posts before you publish them in WordPress.

    If you’d rather have episodes that you publish in Libsyn bypass the draft stage and appear automatically as finished posts for your listeners to see immediately, deselect this check box. 

  • Allow Comments

    This setting is automatically set so that your listeners can post comments in the blog post that contains your episode.

    If you’d rather not let listeners post comments, deselect this check box.

  • Enable Trackbacks

    This setting is automatically set to allow trackbacks. If you’d rather not allow them, deselect this check box.

  • Publish Link

    Use this field to specify which link you want to use to give your listeners access to your episode.

Edit Your Legacy Website

When you sign up to host a podcast with Libsyn, you get access to set up a website where all of your episodes will appear. See Set up Your Libsyn Podcast Website.

If you created a Podcast Page in the Libsyn 4 interface, you can edit it through the Libsyn 5 interface.

To do so:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

     

  2. Click Website.  

  3. Click Edit Legacy Website to display the Settings table for your Libsyn Podcast Page.

    Note

    As you make changes to your page, you can refresh the page in a browser to see them. To get the URL for your Podcast Page, click Copy Website URL in the upper-right of the Settings table and paste the URL into your browser.

     

  4. Set up the look of your page, including the color scheme, where you want your episode players to appear, and more. For more information about how to do so, see the Customize the Look of Your Libsyn Podcast Page section below.  

  5. Set up the widgets you want on your page, such as Facebook or Twitter widgets that show a list of your posts and tweets. The widgets you set up appear on the right side of the Podcast Page. For more information about widgets, see the Set up Widgets on Your Libsyn Podcast Page section below.  

  6. Create About, Contact, or Blog pages to include more information about yourself and your podcast on your Podcast Page. For more information about enabling these pages, see the Add About, Contact, or Blog Pages to Your Libsyn Podcast Page section below.

 

Customize the Look of Your Libsyn Podcast Page

You have several options for customizing the look of your Libsyn Podcast Page. You can add some content to the page, implement a custom color scheme, and more. To get started, click Edit in the Theme Settings row of the Items table.

The sections below explain how to make changes to get your Podcast Page to appear just like you want it.

Upload a Header Image or a Favicon Image

You can upload a header image or a favicon image (or both) to use on your page. A header image is a long image that appears along the top of the page. The favicon image is a smaller image, like a logo, that represents your show and page. Note that there are some requirements and recommendations for these images.

  • Header Image Requirements and Recommendations: Favicon Image Requirements
    • The image can only be a JPG or PNG file.
    • We recommend that you upload a high resolution image.
    • We recommend image dimensions of 1920 x 480 px.
    :
    • The image must be a PNG file.
    • The image must measure exactly 300 x 300 px.

There are two quick ways to upload your images.

  • a.: Click the Click or Drag New Artwork button in the Header Image or in the Favicon Image section, as appropriate. In the window that opens, navigate to the file you want to upload and then double-click it.
  • b.: Find the file you want on your computer, and then click and drag it to the Click or Drag New Media Artwork button in the Header Image section or in the Favicon Image section, as appropriate. When you let go of the mouse button, the upload begins.
Select Your Color Scheme

You can change the theme, font, and link colors of your page. To do so, click any of the swatches that appear next to Theme Color, Font Color, or Episode Link Color to open a window where you can select your colors.

In the window that appears, there are four ways to select a new color.

  • a.: Select one of the options at the bottom of the table by clicking it.
  • b.: Enter the hex code of the color you want in the Hex field.
  • c.: Enter the RBG numbers for the color you want in the respective R, B, and G fields.
  • d.: Move the color slider to change the color range that appears in the box at the top of the window, and then select the color you want in the box. The white circle in the box shows the color you have selected.
Customize the Rest of Your Page Layout Options

The rest of the settings fields give you more options for customizing your page. The table below provides an overview of what the fields do and some guidance for entering information in each one. 

  • Display Title: This option is automatically set to show the title of your podcast on your page. If you don’t want the title of your podcast to appear at the top of your page, select the Hide check box.
  • Producer Message: Enter the message that you want to appear at the top of your page. You can use the ribbon at the top of the box to customize the text by changing the size, bolding text, or more. You can also click the icon on the right side of the ribbon to see more options for customizing text.
  • Media Player Position: This field determines the position of your episode player with relation to the episode summary. If you select Above, you player appears to the left of the summary if you don’t use widgets, or it appears above the summary if you do use them. If you select Below, the player appears to the right of the summary if you don’t use widgets, or it appears below the summary if you do use them. For more information about widgets, see the Set up Widgets on Your Libsyn Podcast Page section below.
  • Allow Comments: This option is automatically set to allow your listeners to leave comments on your page. If you don’t want to allow comments, deselect the check box.
  • Right To Left Language Support: If your podcast page will be in a language that reads from right to left (such as Arabic or Hebrew, for example), select this check box to change the configuration of the page so your language appears correctly.
  • Google Analytics Tracking ID: Enter your Google Analytics tracking ID to use Google Analytics with your Podcast Page.
  • Google Verification ID: Enter your Google verification ID to verify that you use the site. The ID gives you access to master tools.
  • Public Contact Email: Optionally, enter the email where your listeners can contact you. If you prefer not to list an email publicly, leave the field blank.

 

Save Your Options

When you’re done entering your customization options, click Save at the bottom of the page.

Set up Widgets on Your Libsyn Podcast Page

You have the option to include several kinds of widgets on your Podcast Page. The widgets appear along the right side of the page, as show in the image below.

To add widgets, click Edit in the Widgets row of the Items table.

  • Enable Archives Widget: Select Yes in this menu to add a widget that lists all of your episodes by year.
  • Enable Categories Widget: Select Yes in this menu to add a widget that lists all of the categories you have organized your episodes by. Listeners can click a category in the widget to browse episodes that are only in that category. For more information about setting up a category for your episodes, see the “Category” section in The Ins and Outs of Your Episode Details.
  • Enable Custom HTML Widget: Select Yes in this menu to add a widget that you can customize. For example, you can create a widget that has links to your app in the App Store. After you select Yes, enter the content you want to appear in the widget in the text box that appears. Note that you can only use HTML or CSS to format your text.
  • Enable Facebook Widget: To create a widget that shows your Facebook posts, select Yes in this menu and then enter your Facebook URL in the field that appears.
  • Enable Login Widget: Select Yes in this menu to add a widget that listeners can use to log in to MyLibsyn.
  • Enable Twitter Widget: To create a widget that shows your Twitter posts, select Yes in this menu and then enter your Twitter URL in the field that appears.

When you’re done setting up your widgets, click Save at the bottom of the Widgets section.

Add About, Contact, or Blog Pages to Your Libsyn Podcast Page

You can add pages to your Libsyn Podcast Page to give your listeners more information about yourself. For example, you can add any of the following:

  • About page: tell your audience about yourself and what your podcast is about.
  • Contact page: use this page to invite listeners to contact you. You don’t have to enter your email address or contact info in your message to your listeners; they will be able to use the Contact page itself to send you messages. The page includes fields that a listener can use to enter their name, email, and a message they can send you through the Podcast Page. Messages go to the email you entered as your contact email when you set up your podcast.
  • Blog page: set up this page to automatically include any text posts you create. Posts with media can’t be included here.

The links for the pages you add appear at the top of your Podcast Page, as shown in the image below.

To add a page:

  1. Click Edit in the Pages section of the Settings table in the row that corresponds to the page you want to add.
  2. Click the Not Active toggle to activate the page. When you click the toggle, it changes from Not Active to Active.
  3. In the Content field for the About or Contact pages, enter the information you want to include on those pages. Your listeners will see the information you enter when they view the page.
  4. Click Save.

Set Up Your Libsyn Podcast Website

When you sign up to host a podcast with Libsyn, you get access to set up a Libsyn Podcast Website where all of your episodes will appear. You can send your listeners to this site to listen to your show and to get more information about you. The site is customizable.

To set up your Libsyn Podcast Website:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn 5 account to display the Settings page.   

  2. Click Website to display the Website Settings page.  

  3. Customize your site layout options.

    • Producer Message:

      Enter a custom message that you want to appear at the top of your site.

      • If you leave this blank, your show description will be used. See About Your Podcast Settings.

    • Theme Color : Click the swatch to open the Color Selector, where you can select your theme color. See Use the Color Selector.

    • Font Color: Click the swatch to open the Color Selector, where you can select your font color. See Use the Color Selector.

    • Google Font: Select the font for your page.

    • Google Analytics Tracking ID: Enter your Google Analytics tracking ID to use Google Analytics with your Podcast Website.

    • Google Verification ID: Enter your Google verification ID to verify that you use the site. The ID gives you access to master tools.

    • No Follow / No Index: Select this toggle if you don’t want search engines to index your podcast page in search and to follow the links.

    • Website URL:

      Select one of the following options:

      • Default Libsyn URL - Select to use the default Libsyn URL, formatted as 0 number]/site. By default, it ends with “site,” but you can edit this before saving your site for the first time.

        • After you save for the first time, the URL displays below the Website URL field. Click on the URL to view the current version of your site, or click the next to the URL to copy the URL to your clipboard. 

           

      • Personalized Libsyn Domain - Select to create a personalized URL. In the box that displays, enter your text to create a URL formatted as https://[yourtext].libsyn.com. 

         

      • Use my own Domain - Select to enter a full domain name that you have already registered with an accredited domain registrar. There is a $2/month charge for each domain name you add. See Use Your Own Domain Name for Your Libsyn Podcast Website.

    • URL: After you save for the first time, the URL displays as a clickable link below the Website URL field. Click on the URL to view the current version of your site, or click to copy the URL to your clipboard.

    • Pages: See Editing the Pages Section below.

    • Edit Legacy Website: This button displays if you have the Libsyn 4 Podcast Page added as a destination for your show. Click to edit. See Edit Your Legacy Website.

    • Add Custom Favicon:

      The favicon image is a smaller image, like a logo, that represents your show and page. It should be a PNG file that measures 300 x 300 pixels.

      To upload a image in the Favicon Image section, do one of the following:

      • Click Add Custom Favicon. In the window that opens, navigate to the file you want to upload and then double-click it.
      • Find the file you want on your computer, and then click and drag it into the Add Custom Favicon area. When you let go of the mouse button, the upload begins.

      Click Remove to remove the favicon. (You will be able to click Undo Remove to reverse this.)

    Your Libsyn Podcast Website automatically includes the following:

    • Links page: Visitors to your site can click Links at the top of your Libsyn Podcast Website to view a page of all the links you have entered in the External Media Links section of your podcast settings. These can include links to listen to your show on different destinations such as Apple Podcasts, Spotify, or YouTube. They can also include social links such as your Facebook or Instagram page. See About Your Podcast Settings.
    • Social buttons: A button that links to your RSS feed automatically displays underneath your show artwork. Additional buttons display there for some popular services if you have entered URLs for them in the External Media Links section of your podcast settings. See About Your Podcast Settings.

Editing the Pages Section

You can add pages to your Libsyn Podcast Website to give your listeners more information about you. The links for the pages you add appear at the top of your Podcast Website, as shown in the image below.

You can edit the following options in the Pages section:

  • About: Select Enabled to include an About page to tell your audience about yourself and what your podcast is about.
  • Custom: Enter custom text for your About page. You can use the ribbon at the top of the box to customize the text by changing the size, bolding text, or more. You can also click the icon on the right side of the ribbon to see more options for customizing text.
  • Blog: Select Enabled to include a Blog page. The page is automatically generated to include only text posts (no posts with media will be included on this page).
  • Contact: Select Enabled to include a Contact page. Use this page to invite listeners to contact you. You don’t have to enter your email address or contact info in your message to your listeners; they will be able to use the Contact page itself to send you messages. The page includes fields that a listener can use to enter their name, email, and a message they can send you through your Libsyn Podcast Website. Messages go to the email you entered as your contact email when you set up your podcast.
  • Public Contact Email: Enter the email address where you want to receive the messages sent through the form on the Contact page. This email address will not be publicly displayed.
  • Custom: Enter custom text for your Contact page. You can use the ribbon at the top of the box to customize the text by changing the size, bolding text, or more. You can also click the icon on the right side of the ribbon to see more options for customizing text.

Use Your Own Domain Name for Your Libsyn Podcast Website

If you already have a website, you probably already own a domain and are likely using that root domain with that website. For example, you might have a blog titled “My Awesome Blog” which can be found using your domain: 0

If you have your podcast and don’t already have a website, you can purchase a domain from an accredited domain registrar and add that root domain to your Libsyn show. This will allow you to use that custom domain with your Libsyn RSS feed and Libsyn Podcast Website.

If you are already using a root domain on a website, you cannot use that domain with your Libsyn show, but you can use a subdomain, such as 0.

There is a $2 per month fee for hosting a custom root domain or subdomain with Libsyn.

Important

  • The custom domain will not mask your media file URLs.
  • If you have any questions, reach out to our support team before making any DNS changes.

 

To add a custom root domain or subdomain to your Libsyn account: 

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Website to display the Website Settings page. See Set Up Your Libsyn Podcast Website. 

  3. In the Website URL field, select Use my own Domain.  

  4. In the box that displays below the Website URL field, enter your root domain or subdomain name. Do not include http/s or www in your entry.  

  5. Click Add.  

  6. The root domain or subdomain name displays below the Website URL field. 

    • Click Delete to delete a domain. You will be asked to confirm.

    • Click Make Primary to set a domain as your default URL for the L5 site and RSS feeds. (Only displays if you have more than one custom domain listed.)

    • Click to copy the link to your clipboard after a domain name has been added.

       

  7. Repeat Steps 4 and 5 to add multiple custom domains. There is a $2/month charge for each domain name you add. Upon saving, you will be immediately billed for the remainder of the current month, and then monthly on the first of each month.  

  8. Complete any required fields in the Website Settings page.  

  9. Click Save.

After a Success message displays at the top of the page, a message displays beneath the URL(s) informing you that your custom domain was added and instructing you how to point your root domain(s) or subdomain(s) to Libsyn.  

SSL Certificate Assignment

  • Root domain: An SSL certificate is automatically assigned by the system. This process may take up to 7 days to complete.
  • Subdomain: Requires manual submission of an SSL certificate file, private key, and CA bundle before SSL can be activated. If you need assistance with this process, please contact support@libsyn.com.

Pointing Your Root Domain to Libsyn

To point a root domain to Libsyn, you must update the nameservers. Nameservers are like the post office of the internet. The nameserver keeps a record of where your domain is, much like a post office knows your house address in relationship to a physical location. When a website visitor types your website address into a browser, their computer connects to the internet and asks the question, Where is this domain? Your name server will respond back saying The domain is right over here; let me load it for you. For your website to work, the best configuration is to point your nameservers at your domain registrar to Libsyn.  

For a root domain, your nameservers should be the following:

  • ns1.libsyn.com
  • ns2.libsyn.com
  • ns3.libsyn.com
  • ns4.libsyn.com

Important

Nameserver updates take anywhere from 24-48 hours to take full effect. During this time, some visitors will see the change, while others may not. If you are migrating from an old website over to Libsyn, keep your old site online to prevent downtime during the switch.

 

Pointing Your Subdomain to Libsyn

The best way to point to a root domain is to update your nameservers, as explained earlier. This points the entire domain, so you can control that domain from one place.

When pointing to a subdomain, however, you are only pointing a single piece of the domain to Libsyn. Your root domain will remain where it is. In that case, instead of updating nameservers, you must use a specific custom DNS record to point that subdomain to Libsyn. You can do this with either a CNAME record (recommended) or an A Name record.  

CNAME Record (RECOMMENDED OPTION) Add a CNAME record for your subdomain pointing to web-proxy.libsyn.com.

Notes

  • A CNAME record is a canonical name. It specifies an alias for that domain. So, when a visitor enters your subdomain into a web browser, and their computer asks the internet, Where does this subdomain live?, the nameservers assigned to the root domain will say, Oh I don’t have that; it’s over there, go there and you will find it, and the visitor’s computer will go there, find it, and load it.
  • The CNAME record is created wherever your DNS is hosted. Typically this is your web host, but it can also be your domain registrar.
  • CNAME records take an hour or so to fully take effect.

A Record You can point to either of these IP addresses:

  • 204.16.247.189

  • 204.16.247.190Notes

  • An A record, or Address record, points a domain name (in this case a subdomain) to a specific IP address.

  • Libsyn recommends against using an A record for the purpose of pointing a root or subdomain to Libsyn, unless other options are simply unavailable. The reason for this is that the IP address the A record points to can change, and if it does, you may not be aware of the change, which can cause downtime.

  • Adding an A record is similar to adding a CNAME record in that it is done at your DNS host (which is typically your web host or your domain registrar).

 

Mail and Other Records

If you are pointing your domain to Libsyn’s name servers and want to use email or other specialized services requiring updates to custom DNS records, please reach out to our support team. They can put those records in place for you.

Libsyn 5: Including Ads in Your Podcast

Enroll in Automatic Ads

With Libsyn’s Automatic Podcast Ads, you can enable ads to be inserted in real-time for your podcast listeners. (You must be the show owner to enroll.)

To apply: 

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

     

  2. Click Advertising to display the Advertising Settings page. (If you are not the show owner, click Email Show Owner to send them more information about enrolling.)

     

  3. Complete the following:

    • PayPal Email Address: Enter the PayPal email address for the PayPal account where you would like to receive payment. If your show is already enrolled in PodRoll with Libsyn, this PayPal Email Address field is prefilled for you.

    • Select the Programmatic Ad Categories You Would Like Excluded.:

      Select one of the following groups of  ads that you do not want to  appear in your podcast. (Fewer restrictions mean more earning potential, but you can choose to exclude certain ad categories based on what fits your audience.)

      • A. Open | Exclude ads that contain Religion & Spirituality, Firearms, and Illegal Substances  
      • B. Middle of the Road | Exclude ads that contain Religion & Spirituality, Firearms, Illegal Substances, Alcohol, Law, and Government & Politics  
      • C. Cautious  | All of the above plus Arts and Entertainment, Business, Family/Parenting, Health/Fitness, Food/Drink, News, and Personal Finance

    • I consent to being enrolled in the Automatic Podcast Ads program.:

      Select the check box to give us permission to enroll your show in the Libsyn Automatic Podcast Ads program.

      If you are approved, ads may appear in your content immediately, and revenue will be paid via your configured PayPal email address.

     

  4. Click Apply.

You’ll receive a response by email within 5 business days. 

Once approved, your account will be auto-enabled with two pre-roll and two post-roll ad slots, which you can customize at any time. Your content will be converted to MP3 format (for ad compatibility), and you will be able to access earnings and ad slot tools.

Enroll in PodRoll with Libsyn

Libsyn has partnered with the podcast technology company PodRoll to help you sell space in your own episode listings to promote another show — no matter how many monthly downloads you have, and regardless of whether you’re enrolled in Automatic Podcast Ads. (You must be the show owner to enroll.)

When you’re enrolled in PodRoll with Libsyn, PodRoll dynamically inserts a suggested show as the second-most recent entry in your episode listing on Apple Podcasts, Spotify, and Overcast. It is labeled as a Bonus episode in your feed, and the episode title starts with You Might Also Like.

Here’s how that looks on Apple Podcasts’ mobile app:

 

Note

PodRoll is currently only accepting podcasts in English.

To apply for PodRoll with Libsyn: 

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click PodRoll to display the PodRoll Enrollment page. (If you are not the show owner, click Email Show Owner to send them more information about enrolling.)

  3. Complete the following:

    • PayPal Email Address: Enter the PayPal email address for the PayPal account where you would like to receive payment.
    • Show Category Exclusion: You can select up to six show categories to exclude from your podcast based on what fits your audience.

     

  4. Click Apply.  

About Your Enrollment

  • You’ll receive an email notification when your PodRoll enrollment has been activated. (This can take up to 24 hours.)
  • You must publish a new episode after activation before episode suggestions will appear in your feed on Apple Podcasts, Spotify, and Overcast.

About Your Suggested Episodes

  • Campaigns are automatically inserted for the categories you did not exclude. You can update your PodRoll category exclusions at any time by updating your PodRoll settings.
  • All visitors see the same promoted episode in your episode listings.
  • Promoted episodes may change or be removed automatically based on PodRoll campaign demand and alignment with your preferences.
  • When you publish new episodes, you will unlock access to new PodRoll campaigns.
  • Promoted episodes will only appear on Apple Podcasts, Spotify, and Overcast. All promoted placements are temporary and will not remain in your feed permanently.

About Your Payments

  • A PodRoll impression is counted when a dynamically inserted episode is played for at least one minute.
  • Shows with 50,000 downloads in a given month earn a 60% revenue share of the revenue allocated to Libsyn.
  • Shows with fewer than 50,000 downloads in a given month earn $15 per 1,000 PodRoll impressions.
  • After you have earned at least $20, payments are sent to your PayPal account within 90 days of the end of the month when the downloads were recorded.
  • To view your show’s PodRoll revenue, click Advertising at the top of your Libsyn account and click PodRoll. See View Revenue for Your Show.

Enroll in the Spotify Partner Program with Libsyn

When enrolled in Spotify Video with Libsyn, creators can also enroll in the Spotify Partner Program, enabling monetization through Spotify’s ecosystem, including video revenue and ads. Participation is optional and separate from standard Spotify distribution.

When enrolled, your show can access Spotify’s monetization features while you continue to publish and manage your content from your Libsyn account.  

Before You Begin

Before your show can be approved for the Spotify Partner Program:

  • You must have at least three video episodes published, each with at least one ad marker. Video episodes support pre-roll and post-roll ad slots. Mid-roll ad slots are not currently supported for video. See Understanding Automatic Ad Types.
  • If you are not already enrolled in Automatic Ads, you will be prompted to complete the enrollment form during setup. See Including Ads in Your Podcast.

You can still submit your application before meeting these requirements. Your enrollment will remain in a Pending state until your show meets the approval criteria or is reviewed by Spotify.  

To enroll in the Spotify Partner Program:

  1. Click the icon at the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.   

  2. Click Spotify Partner Program to display the Spotify Video Advertising Settings page.  

  3. Complete the following:

    • Select your content category Select the category that best represents your show’s content.

    • Exclude content categories Select any categories you do not want your ads associated with.  

  4. If you are not already enrolled in Automatic Ads, complete the following:

    • PayPal Email Address Enter the PayPal email address where you would like to receive payments.

    • Exclude content categories  Select any categories to exclude for Automatic Ads placement.

    • Consent to Automatic Ads Check the box to confirm enrollment in the Automatic Podcast Ads program and agree to receive payments via your configured PayPal account.  

  5. Click Apply.  

About Your Enrollment

After submitting your application, your enrollment status will be shown as Pending while Spotify reviews your show. The review process typically takes up to 7 days, although it may take longer in some cases.

Once approved:

  • Your show will be eligible for Spotify monetization features
  • Ads may begin serving in your episodes delivered to Spotify
  • You can manage your settings from your Spotify page in Libsyn

If your application is not approved, you may need to review Spotify’s Partner Program Monetization Policies before reapplying.  

About Ads in the Spotify Partner Program

Ads served through the Spotify Partner Program are currently:

  • Automatically inserted into your episodes delivered to Spotify
  • Limited to pre-roll and post-roll placements

Ad availability and behavior are managed by Spotify and may change over time.  

About Your Payments

Payments are sent to your PayPal account within 90 days of the end of the month in which eligible ad activity was recorded.

Spotify pays advertising revenue to Libsyn, and Libsyn includes those earnings in your payout according to this payment schedule.

You can view your Spotify Partner Program earnings by signing in to advertising.libsyn.com. Every show enrolled in Automatic Ads has an advertising account created automatically. After signing in, select your Organization in the upper-right corner, then click Payouts to view your:

  • Eligible impressions
  • Earnings
  • Payment history
  • Pending payouts

Keep in mind that impressions do not directly correspond to a payout amount. Earnings are based on eligible ad playback, and some impressions may result in partial earnings depending on listener behavior, such as skipping an advertisement before it has played long enough to qualify. Because of this, projected earnings cannot be determined from impression counts alone.

Include Ads in Your Libsyn Podcast

You can use Libsyn Ads to enable ads on your podcast.

  • Host-read ads: You can apply to our podcast advertising marketplace if you have 20,000 downloads per new episode within the first 30 days. When an advertiser orders an ad buy from you, you have the first right to approve or deny their ad request. Apply here by clicking Add Your Podcast. 

  • Automatic ads: Automatic ads allow anyone to monetize their Libsyn podcast by dynamically inserting ads. You can choose where you want ads to run — pre-roll, mid-roll, or post-roll. Automatic ads are not a permanent part of your podcast, and because they are inserted in real-time, you can also monetize your back catalog. To apply for Libsyn’s Automatic Podcast Ads through your Libsyn 5 account, see Enroll in Automatic Ads.

Manage Automatic Ad Settings for an Episode

If your show has automatic ads enabled, then you can manage your ads at the episode level.  

Note

To manage your pre-roll and post-roll settings at the show level, see Manage Pre-Roll and Post-Roll Ad Settings for Your Show.

To manage the automatic ad settings for an episode: 

  1.  Add or edit an episode.  

  2. Click Advertising  

  3. Click the toggle next to the Advertising header to turn on advertising for this episode. You will see options to include mid-roll, pre-roll, and post-roll ads.

    Notes

    • Ad Slot tools work best in a Chrome browser on a Mac or PC.
    • Any ad blocking plugins must be turned off.
    • Tools work with CBR (Constant Bit Rate) files; we do not support VBR (Variable Bit Rate) files.

The following options are available: 

  • Slot Picker:

    After you have uploaded or selected an MP3 audio file for your episode, you can use the waveform to visually select the location of mid-roll ads.

    • Click on the waveform to place a marker.
    • Click to zoom in on the waveform.
    • Click to zoom out from the waveform.
    • Click to play the audio. Audio will begin playing from the marker. 

  • Time: When you are visually selecting the location for a mid-roll ad, the time you have selected displays in a time box below the waveform. You can also use this box to manually type in the time to set the position of the mid-roll ad marker.

  • 1 Ad / 2 Ads / 3 Ads / 4 Ads / 5 Ads: Use this dropdown to select whether you want 1, 2, 3, 4, or 5 ads inserted into the selected mid-roll point of your episode audio.

  • Add Slot:

    Click the Add Slot button to insert the selected mid-roll ad slot into your episode.

    • A mid-roll section then displays under the Post-Roll section, and is labeled as Slot 1, Slot 2, etc.
    • The mid-roll ad section includes the selected time location for the ad slot and the number of ads it contains (1, 2, 3, 4, or 5). You can edit these.
    • Click Delete to remove the mid-roll ad slot.

  • Pre-Roll: Displays whether you have selected 1, 2, 3, 4, or 5 ads to play at the beginning of your episode audio. If you have not set this at the show level, you can change the number of pre-roll ads or turn them off for this episode.

  • Post-Roll: Displays whether you have selected 1, 2, 3, 4, or 5 ads to play at the end of your episode audio. If you have not set this at the show level, you can change the number of post-roll ads or turn them off for this episode.

 

After you publish your episode, the Episodes List displays the number of automatic ads in the episode. Mouse over a number to see whether it is the pre-roll, mid-roll, or post-roll ad count. (The mid-roll count is the total number of mid-roll ads across all your configured mid-roll slots.)
