With Libsyn’s Automatic Podcast Ads, you can enable ads to be inserted in real-time for your podcast listeners. See Enroll in Automatic Ads.

After you are enrolled, you can manage your pre-roll and post-roll ad settings at the show level. To do so: 

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

     

  2. Click Advertising to display the Advertising Settings page.

     

  3. Select your settings.

    • Pre-Roll Ads:
      • Select one of the following:
        • All Episodes On - Turn on pre-roll ads for all your episodes 
        • All Episodes Off - Turn off pre-roll ads for all your episodes
        • Set by Episode - Set pre-roll ads on an episode-by-episode basis
      • If the Pre-Roll Ad Count field displays, select a number between 1 and 5 to indicate how many pre-roll ads you would like on all your episodes.
      • If the Apply pre-roll settings to all past episodes check box displays, you can select it to apply the above settings to all your past episodes.
    • Post-Roll Ads:
      • Select one of the following:
        • All Episodes On - Turn on post-roll ads for all your episodes
        • All Episodes Off - Turn off post-roll ads for all your episodes
        • Set by Episode - Set post-roll ads on an episode-by-episode basis
      • If the Post-Roll Ad Count field displays, select a number between 1 and 5 to indicate how many post-roll ads you would like on all your episodes.
      • If the Apply post-roll settings to all past episodes check box displays, you can select it to apply the above settings to all your past episodes.

     

  4. Click Save.

    The settings will apply to your future episodes. They will also be applied to your past episodes if you selected that option.  

Note

These settings only appear on this page if your show has automatic ads enabled. To enroll, see Enroll in Auto Ads. To manage your automatic ad settings for an individual episode, see Manage Automatic Ad Settings for an Episode.

Maximize Revenue with Libsyn Automatic Ads

Libsyn Automatic Ads are designed to help podcasters monetize their content with minimal setup while still maintaining flexibility and control over the listener experience.

Because every podcast is different, there is no single ad strategy that works for every show. Episode length, audience behavior, and ad placement all play a role in overall earnings. However, strategically configuring your ad slots can significantly increase the number of available monetization opportunities across your catalog.

In many cases, podcasters can substantially increase their revenue potential simply by making full use of available ad placements.   

Recommended Ad Placement Formula

One of the most common questions podcasters ask is:

“How many ads should I place in my episodes?”

When Automatic Ads are enabled, Libsyn will automatically configure your show with default ad placements:

  • 2 pre-roll slots  
  • 2 post-roll slots  

While this provides a starting point, we recommend adjusting your ad strategy based on episode length to maximize available inventory and revenue potential.  

Recommended Automatic Ad Configuration

  • Length of Episode: Pre-Rolls Mid-Rolls Post-Rolls
  • Less than 30 minutes: 1 2 2
  • 30 - 60 minutes: 2 4 3
  • 60 - 90 minutes: 2 6 3
  • Greater than 90 minutes: 2 8 3

Breaks

Consider breaks in between topics in your recordings or in long pauses so that you can take an ad break. For ad breaks, we recommend 2 ads per break. If you take 4 ad breaks in a one-and-a-half-hour recording, you can fit all of the mid-rolls you would like in the recording without worrying about overwhelming your audience.

Note

Each Automatic Ad slot is 30 seconds in length and can be skipped by the listener. Skipped ads are still counted as impressions and remain eligible for payout.

Why Mid-Roll Ads Matter

While pre-roll and post-roll ads are important monetization tools, mid-roll placements are typically the most valuable ad inventory available in podcast advertising.

Mid-roll ads perform well because they are inserted naturally throughout the listening experience rather than clustering all ads at the beginning or end of an episode.

Benefits of mid-roll placement include:

  • More monetization opportunities throughout the episode  
  • Better listener retention compared to excessive pre-rolls  
  • Higher advertiser demand  
  • Increased earning potential per episode

 

In addition, pre-roll and mid-roll placements are considered premium inventory and generally receive higher advertiser demand than post-roll.

Because of this, payouts for pre-roll and mid-roll placements are typically higher than post-roll placements.

That said, we recommend avoiding excessive pre-roll usage, as too many ads at the start of an episode may negatively impact the listener experience.  

Understanding the Revenue Impact

Using the recommended ad placement strategy can dramatically increase the number of monetization opportunities available within each episode.

For example, a 60-minute episode with only 2 pre-rolls and 2 post-rolls is using only a portion of its available ad inventory.

Adding the recommended 4 mid-roll slots significantly increases the number of opportunities for ads to be served throughout the episode.

Similarly, a 90-minute episode that only uses pre-roll and post-roll placements may be missing the majority of its monetization potential.

Long-form content naturally creates more opportunities for mid-roll insertion, which is why longer episodes benefit most from a properly configured Automatic Ads strategy.  

Best Practices for Automatic Ads

To maximize revenue potential while maintaining a positive listener experience, we recommend:

  • Spacing mid-roll ads naturally throughout the episode  
  • Avoiding excessive clustering of ads at the beginning  
  • Matching ad density to episode length  
  • Reviewing episode structure when placing manual cue points  
  • Monitoring listener feedback and retention trends  

Need Help Managing Automatic Ads?

If you have questions about configuring Automatic Ads or optimizing your monetization strategy, our support team is happy to help.  

Contact us at:

Understanding Automatic Ad Types

If your show has automatic ads enabled, you can insert pre-, mid-, and post-roll ads into your episodes. This article describes each of these ad types.

Note

Depending on your ad enrollment level, you can pre-record a host-read ad, or dynamically insert an existing ad into an episode. See Include Ads in Your Libsyn Podcast. 

 

Pre-Roll Ads

Pre-roll ads are placed at the very start of an episode. These ads run before the main content of an episode. 

They are considered to be effective in catching a listener’s attention since they are the first thing that plays when a listener starts a new episode. Pre-roll ads are typically 15 to 30 seconds in length, and you can choose to manage pre-roll ads for all of your episodes at once, or set them on an episode-by-episode basis.

 

Mid-Roll Ads

Mid-roll ads are longer ads that you can place in the “middle” of your podcast episode. Think of mid-roll ads as traditional ad breaks when it comes to television; more than one ad slot can be added to an episode. Also, they don’t have to be placed at the halfway point of your episode.

To manage mid-roll ad settings for an individual episode, see Manage Automatic Ad Settings for an Episode.

 

Post-Roll Ads

Post-roll ads play after a podcast episode has concluded. They can be effective for reinforcing messages and converting committed listeners into customers, even though their placement makes them less likely to be listened to.

Like pre-roll ads, these ads are usually 15 to 30 seconds long and they can be managed on an episode-level or show-level basis.

View Revenue for Your Show

If your show has Automatic Podcast Ads and/or PodRoll enabled, you can view your show’s ad revenue in your Libsyn account.

To view, click Advertising at the top of your Libsyn account to display the Advertising page:

The Automatic Ads and PodRoll tables contains the following information:

  • Month

    Displays the months your show generated revenue.

    • Data will not display until 30 days after the end of the month.
    • The table will not display months with no advertising. 

  • Revenue: Displays the amount of revenue for that month. 

  • Impressions: Displays the number of impressions your show had that month. 

    • For Automatic Ads, “Impressions” refers to the number of times listeners downloaded ads on your podcast.
    • For PodRoll, “Impressions” refers to the number of times listeners played at least a minute of a dynamically inserted episode on your feed.

    Impressions are one of the factors considered when calculating your revenue.

  • Estimated Payout Date:

    Displays the estimated payment date for your revenue.

  • Paid:

    Displays the current stage of payment for your revenue. 

    •  - Payment is currently pending
    •   - Payment is complete

Libsyn 5: Managing Your Account Settings

Change Your Account Settings

To change your account settings:

  1. Click the icon and select Account Settings to open the Account Settings form.  

  2. You can edit the following fields:

    • First Name: You can edit your first name.

    • Last Name: You can edit your last name.

    • Display Name : You can edit the name that is displayed on the Manage Users page. See View Users for Your Show.

    • Timezone: You can change your timezone here. Your time zone settings will affect the dates and times episodes are actually published. Note: The time zone setting does not affect statistics or storage rotation, which are handled on UTC time.

    • Default Podcast: You can select which of your shows you would like to be your default podcast when you log in.

    • System Language: Select whether you want to view the site in English or Spanish.

    • Email: You can edit your email address. If you change the email on file, that will change your primary billing email address as well as the email address you use to log in with. An email will be sent to your new and old email addresses to verify the change.

    • Billing CC Emails (One Email Per Line): Enter any additional email addresses that should receive copies of all your billing emails. Place each email on its own line.

    • Opt into email updates and newsletters from Libsyn.com: Select this check box if you want to receive news and marketing information from Libsyn.

    • Terms of Service: Click to read the Terms of Service.

    • Privacy Preferences: Click to open the Privacy Preference Center, where you can choose not to allow some types of cookies. Click on the different category headings to find out more and change our default settings.

    • Password: Click to expand to view password options. See Reset Your Password. When changing your password, you must create a password between 8 and 75 characters that contains at least one upper case letter, one lower case letter, and one number.

    • FTP Settings:

      Click to expand.  

      • You can create or change your FTP password in this section. See Create or Change an FTP Password.
      • You can also see your show IDs. See How to Find Your Show ID.

     

  3. Click Save.

Change Your System Language

You can change your system language in your account settings. To do so:

  1. Click the  icon in the upper-right and select Account Settings to open the Account Settings form.

  2. Click the System Language field and select whether you want to view the site in English or Spanish.

  3. Click Save.

Create or Change Your FTP Password

Before you can use any FTP software, you need to create an FTP password in your Libsyn account. The password you create enables you to to connect to your Libsyn account through your FTP software.

Note

Your FTP password and your Libsyn account password are separate passwords that access different functionality. For security, the password you create for FTP should be different than your Libsyn account password.

After you create your FTP password, you might have to wait a few minutes before it becomes active.

To set up or change your FTP password:

  1. Sign in to your Libsyn account.  

  2. Click the icon and select Account Settings to open the Account Settings form.  

  3. On the right side of the form, click FTP Settings to expand the FTP Settings section.  

  4. Enter your new password in the New Password field, and then enter it again in the Retype Password field to confirm it.

    Tip

    To see the characters you entered for your password, click the icon to the right of the field.

  5. Click Save. Your FTP password is set and you can now log out of Libsyn and work on your FTP software.

    Note

    It might take a few minutes for your password to become active. If you try to connect to your Libsyn account using your FTP software, but the connection fails, try again in a few minutes.

How to Find Your Show ID

Your show ID (formerly known as your show slug) is needed for your FTP settings. See Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episodes.

To find your show ID:

  1. Click the icon and select Account Settings to open the Account Settings form.  

  2. Click FTP Settings to expand the FTP Settings section.

    A list of your shows displays beneath the password fields. 

    • If you have more than three shows, click Show More to further expand the list.
    • Your show ID appears in bold text in parentheses after your show name.
    • You can  click the icon to copy your show ID to your clipboard.

Move Your Podcast from Libsyn to a New Host

We’d hate to see you leave Libsyn, but if you decide to move on, you can easily close your podcast and move to your new host. There are several steps.

Move Your Content to Your New Host

After you close your podcast with Libsyn, you won’t have access to your content. That’s why it’s important to make sure your episodes, artwork, and everything else you want to keep has moved to your new location before you close your podcast.

Different hosts use different processes for moving content, so you should contact your new host to find out how to move your content from Libsyn to its new location.

Note

When you set up your show, you may have chosen to have Libsyn submit your show to Apple Podcasts, in which case your feed URL is using Libsyn’s Apple ID. If so, please contact Apple Podcasts Support to transfer the show ownership to your own Apple ID. Do not resubmit your show to Apple Podcasts because that will create a duplicate of your show in Apple’s system.

 

Point Directories from Libsyn to Your New Feed

Directories where you publish your episodes, like Apple Podcasts, get your information by pulling it from your RSS feed. These directories will continue to look for your Libsyn feed, so you have to update them to go to your new feed instead. To do so, you have to set up a redirect in Libsyn. See Add Feed and Website Redirects for Your Show.

Important

Note that not all directories automatically update to your new feed with a redirect — you might need to manually update some directories for them to start going to your new feed. Check with your new host for assistance with any directories that don’t automatically update.

 

Close Your Show with Libsyn

After you have made sure that your content has moved to your new host and your redirect to your new feed is in place, you can safely close your show with Libsyn. See Close Your Podcast.

Once you close your show, your content will no longer appear in Libsyn after the last calendar day of the month, but the redirect you set up will still be active. That means that directories looking for your Libsyn feed will still get redirected to your new one, but your content — such as episodes or artwork — will no longer be available in your Libsyn account.

Getting Additional Help

Add a Payment Method

To add a payment method:

  1. Log in to your Dashboard on Libsyn 5.  

  2. Click the icon and select Payment Methods to open the Payment Methods page.  

  3. Select the payment method that you would like to use in the Add New Payment Method section at the bottom of the page:

    • Credit Card:

      Select if you would like to enter a credit card for this payment method.

      • Enter your 16-digit credit card number. (Libsyn accepts Visa, MasterCard, American Express, Discover, JCB, UnionPay, and UCB.) 
      • Enter the expiration date in MM/YY format.
      • Enter the CVV number found on the back of your card.

    • PayPal: Select if you would like to connect a PayPal account for this payment method. You will be prompted to validate your PayPal information when you save the form.

    • Bank Account (U.S. Only):

      Select if you would like to enter a U.S. bank account for this payment method.

      • Name on Account - Type your name as it appears on your bank account.
      • Account Type - Select Checking or Savings
      • Routing Number - Enter the routing number for your bank.
      • Account Number - Enter your bank account number.
      • Account Number Confirmation - Type your bank account number again to confirm it.

     

  4. Complete the following:

    • Make This My ‘Primary’ Payment Method: Select this check box if you want this to be your default payment method.

    • Make This My ‘Backup’ Payment Method: Select this check box if you want this to be your backup payment method. If your primary payment method fails to process a charge, your backup payment method will then be used to attempt to process the charge.

    • First Name: You must enter the first name associated with the payment method.

    • Last Name: You must enter the last name associated with the payment method.

    • Company: Optionally, you can enter your company name.

    • Billing Location Lookup: Start typing your address, and select the correct address from the list that displays. This will autocomplete the address fields below.

    • Billing Address: You must enter the billing street address associated with the payment method.

    • Suite/Apt: Enter a suite or apartment number if applicable.

    • City: You must enter the billing city.

    • State: You must enter the billing state if applicable.

    • Postal Code: You must enter a billing postal code. If you do not have a billing postal code, enter 00000 (five zeros).

    • Country: You must select the billing country.

    • VAT Number: (Field displays when you select an applicable country.) You can enter a Value-Added Tax number, if you have one.

    • Payment Method Nickname: You must enter a nickname for the payment method you entered.

    • Cloudflare Verification Box:

      Libsyn uses Cloudflare Turnstile as a verification tool.

      • Select the Verify you are human check box if it displays.
      • After Cloudflare has finished verifying that you are a real web visitor and not a bot, the Cloudflare verification box displays:  Success! 

     

  5. Click Save.

Close Your Podcast

We’d hate to see you leave Libsyn, but if you decide to do so, you can close your podcast to cancel its monthly subscription charge. If you are simply taking a break — whether for seasonality or a temporary change in your circumstances — you can choose to pause your account once per year by following the prompts below.

Tip

If you are struggling with how to publish in Libsyn check out our video playlist for podcasters or join one of our upcoming Live Q&As with our support team.

To pause or close your podcast:

  1. Log in to your Dashboard on Libsyn 5.  
  2. Click the Show selector in the upper-left of your Libsyn 5 account and select the show you want to manage.  
  3. Click the icon in the upper-right and select Billing to open the Billing and Subscriptions page.  
  4. Click Cancel Subscription in the Your Subscription section.  
  5. Follow the on-screen prompts and choose one of the following options:
    • Pause Show to temporarily pause your podcast.
    • Cancel Plan to permanently close your podcast.  

If you pause your podcast: Pausing your podcast temporarily disables the show for up to three months. During this time, no actions can be taken on the show, including uploading new content. You may return at any time during the pause period to resume the show.

At the end of the three-month pause period, the show will automatically resume and regular billing will restart using the payment method on file unless the show is resumed or closed before the pause period expires.

If you close your podcast: Closing your podcast stops any future billing for the show. The show will be taken offline after the billing period ends. Once the show is fully closed, your content may be removed from Libsyn’s servers. You may also be prompted to complete a short survey to share feedback.

 

Things to Think About Before Closing Your Account

Closing your account is a big step. To ensure a smooth transition, consider the following:   

Your Podcast Media
  • After closing your account, your episodes will disappear from podcast apps unless you’ve migrated your feed to another host. Transfers can take anywhere from days to weeks; please check with your new host for their specific timelines.
  • For quick removal, please contact the podcast app directly to request a content takedown.
  • Download and save your final episode files and consider backing up show notes and metadata from your podcast website.
  • If you embedded the Libsyn Podcast Player on your site it will also stop working with the closure of your account and podcast. Please take steps to address this issue as we can not automatically remove them.
  • If you are moving your podcast to a new host, we recommend you upload your content to your new host and add a redirect that points to your new RSS feed before closing your podcast with Libsyn.
  • You may also want to export your show stats and invoices for your own records.  
Payments & Earnings

If you haven’t activated Automatic Podcast Ads or PodRoll, these could help you earn passive income on older episodes if you want to keep your show available but are no longer publishing new content. If they are activated, the following payout information applies:

  • Any pending payouts from Automatic Podcast Ads or PodRoll will still be processed and sent to your chosen payment method if you are owed money after closing your account. This includes any earnings from the month your account is closed.
  • After account closure, you will not be able to log in to address payment issues or view your payment history. Ensure your payment information is up to date before closing your account.
  • If your balance is under $20 at the time of account closure, that amount will not be paid out.  
Your Listeners
  • Consider releasing a short “goodbye” episode to inform your audience, provide closure, and maintain your connection with them.  
Custom Domains
  • If you set up a custom domain with Libsyn, you will not be able to access or manage it once your account is closed. Make changes with your registrar before closing.  
Multiple Podcasts
  • You must delete each podcast separately since billing is tied to each show.  

We’re sorry to see you go. At Libsyn, we’re always working to improve our services and would love to welcome you back in the future. If you return within a year, rejoining and picking up where you left off is easy — just contact our support team and we’ll help you get started again.

Edit a Payment Method

To edit a payment method:

  1. Log in to your Dashboard on Libsyn 5.  
  2. Click the icon and select Payment Methods to open the Payment Methods page.   
  3. In the Saved payment method section, click the icon for an existing payment method.  
  4. On the expanded form, update fields as needed. See Add a Payment Method.
    • If you see the text, “Default,” then this is already marked as your primary payment method. You can change that by marking a different payment method as your primary.
    • You cannot delete a payment method if it is marked as your primary.  
  5. Click Save.

    • Plus Plan: $20/ month - Basic Stats + 324 MB of Monthly Storage
    • Advanced Plan: $25/ month - Advanced Stats + 540 MB of Monthly Storage
    • Advanced 650 Plan: $35/ month - Advanced Stats + 650 MB of Monthly Storage
    • Advanced 800 Plan: $45/ month - Advanced Stats + 800 MB of Monthly Storage
    • Advanced 1000 Plan: $55/ month - Advanced Stats + 1000 MB of Monthly Storage
    • Advanced 1500 Plan: $75/ month - Advanced Stats + 1500 MB of Monthly Storage
    • Max Plan: $150/ month - Advanced Stats + 3000 MB of Monthly Storage

    For more details on the plans, see About the Plan Levels.

    Note: When upgrading your plan you will be proactively billed the difference from your current plan to the new plan for the rest of the billing cycle. When downgrading your plan, you will be billed your current plan until the end of your current billing cycle and the new plan will start on the next billing cycle.

    You can also pause and cancel your subscription from this section. See Close Your Podcast.

  • Billing Issues And Closed Shows: You can manage a closed, paused, or overdue show’s billing and invoices. See Reopen Your Closed Podcast.

Reopen Your Closed Podcast

To reopen your closed podcast:

  1. Log in to your Dashboard on Libsyn 5.

  2. In the Billing Issues and Closed Shows table, click and select Reactivate in the row of the closed show that you want to reactivate.   

    Note

    If you want to reactivate a show on an account that also has an active show, you will have to go to the Billing and Subscription page to find the Billing Issues And Closed Shows table.

     

  3. Select a subscription plan. (See About the Plan Levels.)  

  4. Select or add a payment method. (See Add a Payment Method.)

Once this is complete, your show should be reactivated in our system.

Customize and Share the Libsyn Media Player for a Specific Episode

When you publish an episode, it becomes available on the podcast directories and social media sites where you’ve submitted your show. But once the episode is published, you can also embed a media player on a web page, blog post, or anywhere else that you want to share it. You can customize the look of the episode player by changing colors, size, and more.

Notes

  • You can also customize and embed a Libsyn player that plays multiple episodes from your show. For more information about the podcast player, see Customize and Share the Libsyn Media Player for Your Podcast.
  • If you want to use a third-party player, see Use a Third-Party Media Player.

To customize and share your episode player:

  1. Click Episodes in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Episodes page.   

  2. Find the episode for which you want to customize a player. For help, see View Your Episodes List.  

  3. Click the episode title to display the episode details.  

  4. Click </> Embed  

  5. Click Get Embed Player to display the Link/Embed window.  

  6. Customize the player by updating the information in the player fields. For more information about your options, see the Customize Your Episode Player section below.  

  7. At the bottom of the window, click Embed. The URL to embed the player is now copied to the clipboard.

    Note

    The Embed button creates a link that uses an iFrame to embed the player. If you are embedding the player somewhere that doesn’t support iFrames, you can use the Embedly button to get an oEmbed link instead. You can also click View Embedly to see what it will look like.

     

  8. Paste the embed link you copied where you want the player to appear, such as on a web page or a blog post.

Customize Your Episode Player

The Link/Embed window gives you many options for customizing the look and function of your episode player. As you change the settings, the preview updates automatically to show you what your player will look like when you embed it. 

If you change the height of the player, you can see the exact measurement in pixels in the embed code beneath the player. The measurement is useful so you can decide whether the player will fit where you want to embed it.

 

This table provides some guidance on your options for customizing the player:

  • Assign Stats Tracking Code: You can create a custom stats tracking code for this episode player. (For example, if you’re using a player on your website for a certain episode, and someone else is helping you promote that same episode by embedding another player on their website as well, you can create two different stats codes for each player to see how many downloads are coming from each one.) See Get More Detailed Stats with a Custom Tracking Code.

  • Player Size:

    You can choose different sizes for your player depending on your preference. However, you must choose Extra Large if your podcast includes video.

    When you select Extra Large, the following field displays:

    • Video - Enter a number of pixels for the height of the video.

  • Player Color: Click the box to select the background color you want for your player. See Use the Color Selector.

  • Font Color: Click the box to select the font color you want for your player. See Use the Color Selector.

  • Accessibility:

    This note displays the contrast ratio between the selected Player Color and Font Color.

    According to the World Wide Web Consortium (W3C), the standard minimum ratio for text should be 4.5:1 or higher to provide enough contrast between text and its background so that it can be read by people with moderately low vision. For details, see the W3C’s Web Content Accessibility Guidelines.) 

  • Display Art: Select to show your episode artwork on the left side of the player.

  • Display Title: Select to have the title of your podcast appear above the episode title.

  • Follow: Select to include a FOLLOW link in the upper-right of the player. When users click the link, a window displays with options for following your show on the different social media sites where you are active.

  • Share: Select to include a SHARE link in the upper-right of the player. When users click the link, a window displays with options for sharing your episode on social media.

  • Display Media Download: Select to include a link in the player that your listeners can use to download the episode.

  • Start Time: You can enter the time at which you would like the episode to start in the player, and click Apply.

Customize and Share the Libsyn Media Player for Your Podcast

You can set up a media player for your podcast that lists all of your episodes or a subset of them by category. When you set up the player, you can customize its size, color, which links to make available for your listeners, and more. When you’re done customizing the player, you can embed it on a webpage, blog post, or anywhere you want to make it available to your listeners.

To customize and share your podcast player:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

     

  2. Click Player.

    Note

    Clicking Player might take you to a Player Select Settings table or directly to the Player Settings page. If the Player Select Settings table opens, click Edit in the row that contains the player you want to customize to open the Player Settings page. If you are already at the Player Settings page with all of the customizable fields, continue to step 3.

     

  3. Customize the podcast player by updating the information in the player fields. For more information about your options, see the Customize Your Podcast Player section below.  

  4. At the bottom of the page, click Save to save your changes.  

  5. Copy the code that displays under the player, or click Copy Embed in the upper-right of the page. The code for embedding the player is now copied to the clipboard.   

  6. Paste the code you copied where you want the player to appear, such as on a web page or a blog post.

Customize Your Podcast Player

The Player Settings page gives you many options for customizing the look and function of your podcast player. As you change the settings, the preview at the bottom of the page updates automatically to show you what your player will look like when you embed it. 

If you change the height of the player, you can see the exact measurement in pixels in the embed code beneath the player. The measurement is useful so you can decide whether the player will fit where you want to embed it.

 

This table provides some guidance on your options for customizing the player:

  • Player Size:

    You can choose Large or Extra Large depending on your preference. However, you must choose Extra Large if your podcast includes video.

    When you select Extra Large, the following field displays:

    • Video - Enter a number of pixels for the height of the video.

  • Player Color: Click the box to select the background color you want for your player. See Use the Color Selector.

  • Font Color: Click the box to select the font color you want for your player. See Use the Color Selector.

  • Accessibility:

    This note displays the contrast ratio between the selected Player Color and Font Color.

    According to the World Wide Web Consortium (W3C), the standard minimum ratio for text should be 4.5:1 or higher to provide enough contrast between text and its background so that it can be read by people with moderately low vision. For details, see the W3C’s Web Content Accessibility Guidelines.) 

  • Display Art: Select to show your podcast artwork on the left side of the player. If valid episode artwork is uploaded for the episode being played, the episode artwork will display. Otherwise, the show-level artwork will be shown.

  • Display Title: Select to have the title of your podcast appear above the episode title that’s currently playing.

  • Follow: Select to include a FOLLOW link in the upper-right of the player. When users click the link, a window displays with options for following your show on the different social media sites where you are active.

  • Share: Select to include a SHARE link in the upper-right of the player. When users click the link, a window displays with options for sharing your show on social media.

  • Display Media Download: Select to include a link in the player that your listeners can use to download the episode they’re listening to in your player.

  • Category:

    You can choose any category that you created when you published your episodes. The player will only play the episodes in the category you select. If you want to include all of your episodes, select No Category.

    For more information on categories, review the “Category” section in About Your Episode Details.

  • Playlist:

    In this section, you can expand or shorten the height of the playlist to make more or fewer episodes visible at a time. You have the following options:

    • Hide Playlist - Listeners will have to listen to episodes one at a time, or click through them by using the arrow icons in the player.
    • Show Playlist - Select to show a playlist of your episodes on the bottom of the player that your listeners can scroll through. Listeners can then select which episode they want to listen to. When you select this Show Playlist, the following fields display:
      • Playlist Height - Enter a number of pixels for the height of the playlist.
      • Episodes - Enter the number of episodes you want visible at all times. For example, if you enter 3, there will always be enough room for your listeners to view three episodes in the playlist as they scroll through it. The number of pixels in the Playlist Height field automatically updates to accommodate your choice.
      • Playback Direction - Select Last to First to make your most recent episode appear at the top of the playlist and your oldest episode appear last. To make your oldest episode first and your most recent episode last in the playlist, select First to Last.

Delete an Active File to Free up Your Monthly Storage Space

If you uploaded a file to Libsyn that’s cutting into your monthly storage quota, you can delete it to free up space if you need more this month. For example, if you want to publish more episodes than usual this month, but you’ve almost hit your monthly limit, you can delete an active file that you don’t need so you can free up some space for your new content.

Important

If you don’t want to delete any files, there is an alternative for freeing up space in your monthly quota: you can archive episodes early. For more information about early archiving, see Free up Monthly Storage Space with Early Archiving. For more information about how your storage works and the difference between active and archived files, see Understand How Your Monthly Storage Works.

To delete files:

  1. Open the File Manager page. See Manage Your Files.

  2. Click the icon at the top of the Active Files section.

  3. Select one or more active files to delete. You can filter the list using the icon to search or icon to filter. The icon can be used to display only Active content. See Understand How Your Monthly Storage Works.

    Note

    The items with a status of Active are the ones that are actively counting toward your monthly storage quota. Deleting a file with a status of Archived won’t free up any space. 

     To select a single file, click the  in the row for the active file and select Delete from the menu**.

    **To select multiple files:

    • a.: Click the button.
    • b.: Select the check box for one or more active files. (The check box in the header selects or deselects all the check boxes at once.).
    • c.: Click the Delete button.

  4. In the window that opens, click Delete to confirm.

    The file is moved to the Files to Be Deleted section of the File Manager page. You can recover a deleted file within 90 days. See Recover a Deleted File.

    Note

    You cannot preview, play, or download a deleted media file. If you delete a media file connected to a scheduled or published episode, then the episode is changed to a draft. See Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish It Later.

Free up Monthly Storage Space with Early Archiving

Early archiving is a feature that lets you archive your content before the end of the month to free up space in case you need to exceed your monthly storage quota. For example, if your plan lets you upload 540 MB per month, but you’re publishing more episodes than usual and you’re pushing against your limit, then you can pay a one-time fee to archive some of your content early to free up space and publish more for just this month without having to upgrade to a higher plan. (It’s sort of like paying an overage fee on your cell phone plan for one month instead of upgrading your plan for every month.)

Early archiving costs 5 cents per MB of content, but the minimum fee for the service is $5.

To archive your active content early:

  1. Open the File Manager page and locate the active file(s) you want to archive. See Manage Your Files.

    Note

    You should only archive files with a status of Active, because those are the files from the current month that are actively counting toward your monthly storage quota. (These statuses are used only to track your uploads and storage. There is no difference between archived and active files in terms of availability or accessibility for your listeners.)

  2. Select the file(s) to be archived.

    • To select a single file, click the in the row for the file and select Archive from the menu.

       

    • To select multiple files, click the icon at the top of the table, select the checkbox for the active file(s) you want to archive, and click the Archive button.

  3. In the window that opens, click Agree To Charge to confirm the charge.

    The episodes you selected are now archived, and you have more space to publish this month.

Manage the Episode Information in Your Media Files

Media files, such as MP3 audio files, hold information about the file’s content in containers called ID3 tags. ID3 tags form part of all MP3 files, and they contain information such as the episode title, artist or author name, description, artwork, and more.

When listeners download a podcast episode to listen to offline, media players show them the information in the ID3 tags. Some media players pull information from ID3 tags even when connected to the internet. That’s why it is highly recommended you enter ID3 tag information. Even though entering that information isn’t required when you publish in your Libsyn account, it’s important to do so because it ensures that all your listeners get a consistent experience, regardless of how they listen to your episodes.

Note

Although you don’t need to have information in your ID3 tags when you upload an episode in your Libsyn account, you do need to have information in your ID3 tags if you are publishing using FTP. For more information about how to publish using FTP, see Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episodes.

There are two main ways to update ID3 tags in your media files: download an ID3 editor and use it to edit the ID3 tags in your MP3 files before you publish, or use the fields in Libsyn when publishing an episode.

 

Use an ID3 Editor to Enter Information in Your Media File

There are many ID3 editors that you can download to edit the ID3 tags in your media files. The editor you choose depends on your preferences.

Notes

  • Your editor should use ID3v2 tags. For more information, see the IAB’s Podcast Measurement Compliance page.
  • Using an editor to write information in the ID3 tags is important if you are publishing using FTP. For more information, see Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episodes.

After you download and install the tool of your choice, you can use it to update the ID3 tags with information about the episode contained in the file, including information such as the title of the episode, the name of your show, a description of your episode, episode artwork, and more.

Sometimes, the names of  ID3 tags are geared to the music industry. For example, ID3 tags might include a field called Album. Libsyn uses that field to store the name of your show. For a general overview of how ID3 tag names correspond to Libsyn fields, see the table below.

  • ID3 Tag Name

    Corresponding Information or Libsyn Field

  • Artist: Corresponds to the Author field.

  • Album: Refers to the name of your show.

  • Comment: Truncated version of the Description field. You can enter your full description here. Players and other programs will truncate it to offer a short version of the episode description.

  • Lyrics: Full version of the Description field.

  • Artwork: Contains the episode’s artwork. If you include episode artwork, it overrides the artwork you set for your show. (We recommend that you limit the ID3 size for art to 300 KB with a maximum of 800 x 800 pixels.)

Import ID3 Tag Information to Libsyn

When you publish an episode and upload your media file in Libsyn, you can import the information you entered in the ID3 tags to Libsyn. Libsyn then uses that information to publish to the destinations you’ve connected your show to, such as your RSS feed, podcast directories, or your website.

To import the information in the ID3 tags, upload your episode and then click the Populate Form with ID3 Data button in the Add Episode form. The ID3 information you entered appears in the corresponding Libsyn fields.

Note

If you began entering information in the fields in the Add Episode form before you clicked the Populate Form with ID3 Data button, the information in your ID3 tags replaces the information you entered in Libsyn. However, the information you entered is not lost! To retrieve what you wrote, click Undo.

If necessary, you can review the information from your ID3 tags. If you want to update anything, just edit the information in the fields in the Add Episode form. Then, select the Update ID3 Tags checkbox to export your updates to your ID3 tags.

 

Update ID3 Tags When Publishing in Libsyn

If you don’t want to edit your ID3 tags before you upload a media file to Libsyn, you can write all of the information about the episode in Libsyn and then use that information to populate the ID3 tags.

When you’re done writing the information about your episode in Libsyn, select the Update ID3 Tags checkbox at the bottom of the Episode Details tab. Libsyn exports all of the information you entered about your episode into the corresponding ID3 tags in your media file. Now, when listeners download your file to listen to offline, it will contain the same information in the ID3 files that you entered in Libsyn.

After you select a file for deletion, it is moved to the Files to Be Deleted section of your File Manager page, where you can recover it within 90 days.

Note

You cannot preview, play, or download a deleted media file. If you delete a media file connected to a scheduled or published episode, then the episode is changed to a draft. See Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish It Later.

 

To recover files you have marked for deletion within the past 90 days:

  1. Open the File Manager page. See Manage Your Files.

  2. In the Files To Be Deleted table, select the deleted file(s) to recover.

    • To select a single file, click the in the row for the file and select Recover from the menu**.

      **

    • To select multiple files, click the icon at the top of the table, select the checkbox for the file(s) you want to recover, and click the Recover button.

  3. In the window that opens, click Recover to confirm.

    The file is moved back to the Active Files table.

    Note

    Restoring a file does not restore its connection to an episode. To reconnect it to an episode, click the Media Library button in the Add Episode form. See Upload Your New Episode.

Understand How Your Monthly Storage Works

Every Libsyn plan comes with a monthly upload quota. For example, if you sign up for an account with 540 MB of monthly storage, you can upload 540 MB of new content every month. There is no limit on how much content you can store at Libsyn at any time, regardless of your account level — all the content that you’ve uploaded in the past will always be available on Libsyn as long as you maintain your account. The only limit is on how much new content you can upload each month.

 

How Does Libsyn Keep Track of Your Monthly Storage?

To keep track of your monthly storage quota, Libsyn labels your content with a status of either Active or Archived. For example, every media file you upload to your account this month is labeled as Active because those are the files that are actively counting toward your storage quota this month. When the month is over, those files become labeled as Archived, which means that they no longer count against your storage quota. Your active files are archived at midnight, GMT, on the first of every month. After those files get archived, your full monthly quota becomes available again for the new month.

Important

In terms of availability and accessibility for your listeners, there is no difference between archived and active content. Libsyn uses these statuses only to help track your monthly quota.

There is also an Exempt status. Files with that status don’t count against your monthly storage.

 

Check and Manage Your Storage

The Monthly Storage section on the Dashboard displays the following:

  • The percentage of your storage you’ve used this month.
  • How many megabytes you’ve used this month and how many remain.
  • The time until your quota resets.

Click Manage to open the File Manager page. See Manage Your Files.

Do You Need to Free up Some Space This Month?

There are two main ways to free up space if you’re running out: you can delete an active file so it no longer takes up space, or you can archive a file early.

If you delete a file, it is no longer available on Libsyn. This is a good solution if you no longer need a file and don’t mind getting rid of it. For more information about how to delete a file, see Delete an Active File to Free up Your Monthly Storage Space.

On the other hand, archiving a file early is helpful if you need to keep all your files but need a little extra storage just for this month. For a one-time fee, the episodes you archive early will move from an Active status to an Archived status right away, and they will stop taking up room in your storage quota for the month. For more information about how to do so, see Free up Monthly Storage Space with Early Archiving.

Use a Third-Party Media Player

Libsyn offers a customizable media player for your podcast as well as customizable media players for specific episodes. If you have a third-party player you wish to use on a website, you are welcome to do so. Most third-party players will pull from an RSS feed or from your download URL for the media file.

When using or configuring a third-party player, we recommend following these guidelines for IAB compliance:

  • Do not autoplay content or preload media.

  • Use ID3v2 tags.

  • ID3v2 tag size for artwork should be limited to 300 KB and a maximum of 800 x 800 pixels.

  • For a full download, ask for the entire file at once. For a progressive download, ask for the file in slices (byte ranges).

  • Use header information (located at the start of the podcast) to prevent a full download when not needed.

  • Do not modify the enclosure URL when requesting media; don’t add extra parameters.

  • Do not cache podcast episodes. Always download the latest episode from the enclosure URL for every app user wanting to listen.

  • Use the GUID (as opposed to episode URL, title, publication date, etc.) to identify new episodes in the RSS feed that should be automatically downloaded to a user’s device. 

  • Use an “automatic download unsubscribe” behavior (for example, stop auto downloads after 5 episodes of non-listens).

  • Do not automatically download all episodes of a podcast by default. 

If you have questions regarding using your third-party player, we recommend reaching out to the developer of that player.

Use the Color Selector

You can use the Color Selector to pick a specific color for a theme, background, or font.

To open the Color Selector, click the color swatch below one of the following fields:

  • Podcast Settings - Directory Theme Color

  • Website Settings - Theme Color or Font Color

  • Player Settings - Player Color or Font Color

  • Player Settings for an Episode - Player Color or Font Color

After the Color Selector displays, you have several options for selecting a color:

  • Color Picker

    Click in the box or drag the circle around it to select a color.

  • Slider

    Push the slider to the right or left to change the selected color.

  • Hex Color Code

    Enter the 6-digit hex code to select a color.

  • Color Contrast

    Displays the contrast ratio between the selected font and background colors. (See Note below.)

  • Swatches:

    Select a swatch to choose that color.

  • Brand Default:

    (Displays only on the Player Settings page.) Click to use the Theme Color and Font Color from your Website Settings page. 

  • Auto:

    Click to use the color that the system identifies as the main color element in your podcast artwork.

NoteColor Contrast - According to the World Wide Web Consortium (W3C), the standard minimum ratio for text should be 4.5:1 or higher to provide enough contrast between text and its background so that it can be read by people with moderately low vision. For details, see the W3C’s Web Content Accessibility Guidelines.

Libsyn 5: Managing Your Libsyn Pro Episodes

About the QC Process for LibsynPro Episodes

LibsynPro includes features for quality control, which allows a user to submit an episode for review before it is published.

For the purposes of this discussion, we will refer to the user who creates the episode as the Creator, and the user who approves the episode as the Editor.

Note

The ability to approve an episode depends on having the Release Episodes permission. See About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions.

The QC process is as follows:

  1. A Creator adds an episode and clicks Publish. See Publish an Episode.

  2. The episode’s status changes to QC Awaiting instead of Published or Scheduled. See Publish a Pro Episode.  

  3. An Editor clicks View on an episode with a status of QC Awaiting. See View an Episodes List for Your Pro Network.

  4.  The Episode Details display with a QC banner at the top, and the Editor can review the episode:

  5. The Editor clicks either Decline or Approve in the banner, and then clicks Yes to confirm.

    • When an episode is approved, it is immediately published.
    • When an episode is declined, the status changes to QC Declined, which is similar to the Draft status. See Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish It Later.
      • The Creator (or any other user on the network who has the Edit Episodes permission) can then make changes to the episode and click the Publish button again to resubmit the episode to the QC process.

Publish a Pro Episode

When adding or editing an episode for a show on a LibsynPro network, the following additional restrictions may apply:

  • When anyone who doesn’t have the Release Episodes permission clicks the Publish button, the episode’s status changes to QC Awaiting (instead of Published or Scheduled). For more details, see About the QC Process for LibsynPro Episodes.
  • A pro network can be restricted to only allow media uploads that are .mp3 audio files. For help with this, email Support at support@libsyn.com.

View an Episodes List for Your Pro Network

To view the Episodes List for a show on your LibsynPro network:

  1. Open your pro network admin dashboard. See View Your Pro Network Admin Dashboard.  
  2. Click the Shows tab.  
  3. On the Shows page, click View in the row for the show you want to view. See View the Shows for Your Pro Network.

The Episodes page displays for the selected show.

A pro network Episodes page includes the following:

  • Total Episodes: Displays the total number of episodes for the selected show in your pro network.

  • Search: You can search for an episode of the selected show by entering text in the Search bar.

    • Draft
    • Published (also includes Scheduled)
    • QC Awaiting (episodes awaiting QC from an editor)
    • QC Declined (episodes declined during QC by an editor)
    • Audio (episodes with audio files attached)
    • Video (episodes with video files attached)
    • No Media (episodes with no audio or video media files attached)

  • Episode Artwork: The episode artwork displays in the first column.

  • Title: The episode title displays next to the episode artwork.

  • Date: Displays the date that the episode was published.

  • Status:

    Displays one of the following statuses:

    • Published
    • Draft
    • Scheduled
    • QC Awaiting
    • QC Declined

  • Ad Slots: Displays the number of automatic ads in the episode, if applicable. See Manage Automatic Ad Settings for an Episode.

  • View: Click to view the Episode Details. See View Your Pro Episode Details.

View Your Pro Episode Details

To view the Episode Details for an episode on your LibsynPro network:

  1. Open the Episodes page for a show on your network. See View an Episodes List for Your Pro Network.

  2. Search for an episode to view. You can use the icon to search or the icon to filter the episodes by publication status. 

  3. Click View in the row for an episode. The Episode Details display. They are the same as the details for a non-pro episode, with the following exceptions:

    • Quick Links do not display.
    • The Status field displays QC Awaiting or QC Declined, if applicable.
    • If the Status is QC Awaiting, a QC banner displays. See About the QC Process for LibsynPro Episodes.

Libsyn 5: Managing Your LibsynPro Settings

Change Your Pro Network Payment Method

If you have the right level of access for a LibsynPro network, you can update the payment method for the network.  

Note

You must have the Admin Role permission Modify Billing to access this feature. See About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions.   

To change your payment method for a pro network:

  1. Open the admin dashboard for your pro network. See View Your Pro Network Admin Dashboard.
  2. Click the icon in the top of your account to display the Settings page.  
  3. Click Billing to open the Billing page. If prompted, select the Update Billing Method checkbox.  
  4. In the Choose a way to pay area, select one of the following:

    • Credit Card:

      Select if you would like to enter a credit card for this payment method.

      • Enter your 16-digit credit card number. (Libsyn accepts Visa, MasterCard, American Express, Discover, JCB, UnionPay, and UCB.)
      • Enter the expiration date in MM/YY format.
      • Enter the CVV number found on the back of your card.
      • Enter your name and address. (For details on the fields, see Add a Payment Method.)

    • PayPal: Select if you would like to connect a PayPal account for this payment method. Click PayPal Checkout to be prompted to validate your PayPal information.

  5. Cloudflare Verification Box - Libsyn uses Cloudflare Turnstile as a verification tool.
    • Select the Verify you are human check box if it displays.
    • After Cloudflare has finished verifying that you are a real web visitor and not a bot, the Cloudflare verification box displays: Success!

 

  1. Click Save.

Manage Single Sign-On for Your Pro Network

Single sign-on (SSO) is an authentication service that allows users to log in through their network or identity provider (IdP) with one set of credentials and have access to multiple applications.

If you have access to manage the Single Sign-On settings for a LibsynPro network, you can set up SSO to allow your network users to be automatically signed in to their LibsynPro account through your IdP.

Note

To request access to manage the Single Sign-On settings for your network, email Support at support@libsyn.com.

To manage the Single Sign-On settings for your network:

  1. Open the admin dashboard for your pro network. See View Your Pro Network Admin Dashboard.

  2. Click the icon in the top of your account to display the Settings page.

  3. Click Single Sign-On to display the Single Sign-On Settings page.

    Note

    • The left side of the page displays the configurations you must select in Libsyn.
    • The right side of the page displays the information you must copy into fields on your idP’s application.

     

  4. In the Identity Provider field, select the name of your single sign-on identity provider (idP).  

  5. Copy the following information into your idP’s application:

    • Identity Provider Settings (SAML 2.0):

      This section displays the required URLs for the particular IdP you selected in Step 4. (SAML 2.0 stands for Security Assertion Markup Language 2.0, an XML-based protocol that uses security tokens to pass information between an idP and a service provider.)

      1. Copy a URL (you can click the to copy a link to your clipboard).
      2. Paste the URL into the settings field with the corresponding label on your IdP’s application. 

    • Attributes:

      Attributes help the system identify a user and redirect them to log in through their IdP. If a user has previously logged in with SSO, the system matches their email address to an existing user profile (see Add or Edit Users for Your Pro Network) and automatically logs them in. If the system cannot match an email address to an existing user profile, it creates a new user profile using the email address and the first and last name. It also grants this new user the default permissions you select in the Newly Created User Permissions section.

      To enable this automatic user profile creation, enter the following on your IdP’s application:

      • For the user.firstName value, enter FirstName as the ID or name.
      • For the user.lastName value, enter LastName as the ID or name.
      • For the user.email value, enter Email as the ID or name.

  6. On your IdP’s application, follow the prompts to generate a metadata URL, and copy it to your clipboard.

  7. On the left side of Libsyn’s Single Sign-On Settings page, complete the following:

    • Metadata URL :

      Copy and paste the metadata URL you generated on your IdP’s application in Step 6. The metadata URL is used to match Libsyn’s data with your IdP’s data.

    • Newly Created User Permissions
      Select the check boxes for the permissions that you want to be granted to newly created users by default. The available permissions are:
      • Create Episodes
      • Edit Episodes
      • Release Episodes
      • Create Shows
      • Edit Shows
      • View Stats

     

  8. Click Save to save your changes**.

    After you have successfully saved your SSO settings, an Active** badge displays at the top of your Single Sign-On Settings page. Your users will no longer have to sign in through the Libsyn sign-on page.

Manage Your Network Settings

If you have the right level of access for a LibsynPro network, you can view and update the settings for the network. 

 

Note

You must have the Admin Role permission Modify Network to access this feature. See About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions.   

To modify settings for a pro network:

  1. Open the admin dashboard for your pro network. See View Your Pro Network Admin Dashboard.  

  2. Click the icon in the top of your account to display the Settings page.  

  3. Click Network Settings to display the Network Settings page.  

  4. The following options display:

    • Network Name: You can edit the name of your network.

    • Network Slug:

      Displays a short identifier for the network that is used to generate the URL where users access the network in Libsyn.

      For example, if the network slug is testslug, the user logs in at 0.

      Once set, a network slug can only be changed by contacting support@libsyn.com.

    • Website URL: You can add or edit your website URL. This is published at the channel level of your network’s feed to point your feed’s audience toward your webpage.

    • Update Artwork:

      You can add or edit network artwork, which displays in your Network / Show switcher to help identify your network in Libsyn’s system.

      • Click Add Artwork to add your art. The file requirements are the same as they are for your show artwork. See Upload Your Show’s Artwork.
      • Click Remove to remove your network artwork. (You will be able to click Undo Remove to reverse this.) 

    • Hide Libsyn Logo on Player:

      Select the checkbox to hide the Libsyn logo that appears in the top-right corner of all embed players for the shows in your network. You can also upload a custom logo to be used in its place. A custom logo must be a 24px by 24px PNG file. To upload your logo, do one of the following:

      1. Click the Add Player Logo area. In the window that opens, navigate to the file you want to upload and then double-click it.
      2. Find the file you want on your computer, and then click and drag it to the Add Player Logo area. When you let go of the mouse button, the upload begins.

      After the logo uploads, it appears as a preview in the appropriate section. Click Remove to remove the file. (You will be able to click Undo Remove before saving to reverse this.)

     

  5. Click Save to save your changes.

Libsyn 5: Managing Your LibsynPro Shows

Add Multiple Libsyn Classic Feeds for Your Pro Show

By default, every Libsyn show is automatically connected to an Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed. However, if you have a LibsynPro show, you can add more Libsyn Classic Feeds and give them each a custom name.

Note

You can only submit one feed per show to Apple Podcasts. See Submit Your Show to Apple Podcasts.

 

To add multiple Libsyn Classic Feeds to your LibsynPro show:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your LibsynPro show account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Destinations to display the Destinations page.  

  3. Click Connect in the row for Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed. 

     

  4. Enter your unique document slug in the field that appears next to your feed URL. (For more details about adding a new destination, see Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to a Destination Feed.)

     

  5. Click Submit.

Your new feed will now display in the following locations:

  • The Destinations table on the show Settings page
  • The Quick Links section on the Dashboard (See Find the URL to Your RSS Feed)
  • The Destinations section on the Episode Details (See View Your Episode Details)
  • The Downloads per Destination section on the Stats page (See Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package)

View the Shows for Your Pro Network

If you have admin access for LibsynPro, you can view all the shows on your network.

To do so:

  1. Open your pro network admin dashboard. See View Your Pro Network Admin Dashboard.

  2. Click the Shows tab to display the Shows page.

The pro network Shows page includes the following:

  • Total Shows:

    Displays the total number of shows on the network.

  • Search:

    You can search for a show by entering text in the Search bar. The system filters the list as you type.

  • Add

    Click to add a new show. See Create a New Podcast.

  • Show Artwork

    The show artwork displays in the first column.

  • Title

    The show title displays next to the show artwork.

  • Episodes

    Displays the number of episodes that have been published for the show.

  • Status

    Displays whether the show is active or inactive. (Shows are inactive if they have a billing status of Closed.)

  • Access

    Displays one of the following:

    • QC - Click to display the Episodes List for the show. See About the QC Process for LibsynPro Episodes.
    • View - Click to display the Episodes List for the show. See View an Episodes List for Your Pro Network.
    • Reactivate - Click to view information on reactivating an inactive show.
      • You do not have access to this show. Click to display information on how to gain access.
      • If you have the right permissions (see Note below), then you are redirected to the Edit User page, where you can add the show to your user profile to grant yourself access.

 

Note

You can grant access to a show if you have the following permissions (which are included in the Admin Roles of Administrator or Director):

  • Assign Users
  • Modify Network
  • Manage Users
  • Admin Modify Shows

See About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions.

Libsyn 5: Managing Your LibsynPro Users

About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions

You can add or edit the roles and permissions for a LibsynPro user on the Manage Users page. (See Add or Edit Users for Your Pro Network.)

The user permissions are divided by Admin Role and Producer Role.

ROLESAdmin Role

DescriptionAdministrator:

All Admin Role permissions are selected.

  • The Modify Network permission grants the ability to change the Network Settings. See Manage Your Network Settings.

Director:

This role is designed for users who should have the ability to manage users, shows, and episodes on the network. All Admin Role permissions are selected except Grant Admin, Modify Billing, Modify Network, and View Billing.

Analyst: This role is designed for users who should only have the ability to manage users and statistics for the network. Only the following Admin Role permissions are selected: Assign Users, View Campaigns, Manage Users, and View Network Stats.

Custom: By default, no Admin Role permissions are selected. You can select any you would like to include.

Producer Role

Description

Producer

All Producer permissions are selected.

Editor

This role is designed for a user who can edit and release content on a network. All Producer permissions are selected except Create Shows.

  • The Release Episodes permission grants the ability to approve an episode. See About the QC Process for LibsynPro Episodes.
Creator

This role is designed for a user who can create and edit content but not approve it for release. Only the following Producer Role permissions are selected: Create Episodes, Edit Episodes, and View Stats.

Custom

By default, no Producer Role permissions are selected. You can select any you would like to include.

 

Note

Admin permissions can supersede Producer permissions. A user’s permissions are determined by their highest role(s).

Add or Edit Users for Your Pro Network

If you have admin access for a LibsynPro network, you can manage users of the network. 

To do so:

  1. Open the Manage Users page to view the users in your pro network. See View Users for Your Pro Network.

  2. Click Add New to add a new user, or click and choose Edit in the row for the user you want to edit.

  3. You can add or edit the following fields:

    • First Name:

      Enter or edit the user’s first name.

    • Last Name: Enter or edit the user’s last name.

    • Display Name
      Enter or edit the user name to be displayed on the Manage Users page.

      See View Users for Your Pro Network.

    • Email: Enter or edit edit the user’s email address.

    • Admin Role:

      Select or edit one of the following roles:

      • Administrator - All Administrator permissions are selected.
      • Director - All Administrator permissions are selected except Grant Admin, Modify Billing, Modify Network, and View Billing.
      • Analyst - The only selected Administrator permissions are Assign Users, View Campaigns, Manage Users, and View Network Stats.
      • Custom - Select any other combination of Administrator permissions.

      For more details, see About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions.

    • Producer Role:

      Select or edit one of the following roles:

      • Producer - All Producer permissions are selected.
      • Editor - All Producer permissions are selected except Create Shows.
      • Creator - The only selected Producer permissions are Create Episodes, Edit Episodes, and View Stats.
      • Custom - Select any other combination of Producer permissions.

      For more details, see About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions. 

    • Shows: Select the check boxes for the shows you want this user to be able access within this network. Deselect the check boxes to remove access.

    • Send Password Reset Email:

      This user will be sent an email with a link to reset their password. The password for this user will only change when they have completed the steps in the email.

     

  4. Click Save to save your changes. The user will receive an email.

    • If the user already has a Libsyn account, they will be invited to log in to your network.
    • If the new user doesn’t have a Libsyn account, they will be invited to get started by setting a password.

View Users for Your Pro Network

If you have admin access for a LibsynPro network, you can view the users of the network. 

To do so:

  1. Open the admin dashboard for your pro network. See View Your Pro Network Admin Dashboard.

  2. Click the icon in the top of your account to display the Settings page.

  3. Click Manage Users.

    The pro network Manage Users page includes the following:

    • Search: You can search for a user by entering text in the Search bar. The system filters the list as you type.

    • Add New:

      Click to add a new user. See Add or Edit Users for Your Pro Network.

    • Name: Shows the Display Name entered for the user. See Add or Edit Users for Your Pro Network.

    • Email Address: Displays the email address entered for the user.

    • Admin: Displays the Admin Role entered for the user (Administrator, Director, Analyst, or Custom). See About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions.

    • Producer: Displays the Producer Role entered for the user (Producer, Editor, Creator, or Custom). See About the LibsynPro User Roles and Permissions.

    • Edit: Click to edit the information for the user. See Add or Edit Users for Your Pro Network.

You can grant access to up to five additional users to help manage your show.

  3. You can add or edit the following fields:

    • Last Name: Enter or edit the user’s last name.

    • Display Name
      Enter or edit the user name to be displayed on the Manage Users page.

      See View Users for Your Show.

    • Email: Enter or edit the user’s email address.

     

  4. Click Save to save your changes. The user will receive an email.

    • If the user already has a Libsyn account, they will be invited to log in to your show.
    • If the new user doesn’t have a Libsyn account, they will be invited to get started by setting a password.

You can grant access to up to five other people to help manage your show.  

To view the people who have access to your show:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Manage Users.

    The Manage Users page includes the following:

    • Email Address: Displays the email address entered for the user.

Libsyn 5: Managing Your Stats Reports

Download Stats Reports for Your Show

You can download reports about your statistics if you have the advanced stats package. This package comes with the Advanced Plan and higher. See Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package.

To download statistics for your show:

    1. Click Stats at the top of your Libsyn account to access the Stats page for your selected show.

    2. Click the Download Report button in the upper-right.

    3. In the Stats Export window, select the check box for one or more of the following statistics for your show. (A separate report will export for each statistic you select.)

      **Total Downloads**

: Lists the total number of IAB and Unique downloads your show had during the specified date range, as well as how many downloads it had on each date.
      • By Episodes

        Lists the number of IAB and Unique downloads your show had for each episode released in the specified date range, as well as the date each was released.

      • By Country

        Lists the number of IAB and Unique downloads your show had for each country during the specified date range.

      • By Destination

        Lists the number of IAB and Unique downloads your show had for each destination during the specified date range.

      • By User Agent

        Lists the number of IAB and Unique downloads your show had for each user application during the specified date range.

       

    4. Select a Date Range for the statistics:

      **Last 30 Days**

:

Include downloads from the past 30 days.
      • Last 60 Days

        Include downloads from the past 60 days.

      • Last 90 Days

        Include downloads from the past 90 days.

      • All Time

        Include all downloads since you created your show in Libsyn.

       

    5. Select a file type for your download:

      **.csv**

: CSV (comma-separated values)
      • .xlsb

        XLSB (Excel Binary)

      • .pdf

        PDF (Portable Document Format)

       

    6. Click Export.

      A .zip file downloads, containing a separate file for each statistic you selected. All the files will have the same date range and file type.

To download statistics for individual stats tables, such as Episode Totals or Downloads by Episode:

    1. Click the button in the upper-right of a table.

    2. Select either CSV or PDF.

      A CSV or PDF file downloads with your selected statistics.

      Note

      Every table below the Statistics by Date Range button is filtered by the date range you select. For more info, see Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package.

Get More Detailed Stats with a Custom Tracking Code

Available with the Advanced Plan and higher, the custom stats tracking code allows you to generate a code using whatever naming convention you want, and then see exactly how many downloads came in using that code in your advanced stats.

Note

If you want to use a custom stats tracking code, you have to create one before you embed a link.

To create and set up your stats tracking codes:

  1. Click Episodes at the top of your Libsyn account to open the Episodes page.  

  2. Find the episode you want to create stats codes for.

    Tip

    To find an episode quickly, click the icon and enter the first few letters of the episode name in the Search field at the top of the Episodes table. The system filters episodes as you type.

  3. Click the name of the episode to display the Episode Details page.  

  4. Click the Quick Links button to display the Quick Links window.

     

  5. In the AssignStats Tracking Code field, select a previously entered code or create a new stats tracking code by typing the name in the field that displays.

    Note

    Use a name that will help you remember which player it’s referring to. For example, if you’re creating a stats code for a player you’re going to embed on your website, you could name it “MyWebsite.” Note that stats code names can’t have special characters or spaces.

     

  6. Press Enter on your keyboard when you’re done.

    Your custom tracking code is now appended to the end of the following links:

    • Direct Download
    • Enclosure Tag
    • Libsyn Directory URL  

  7. Click the icon to copy the embed code for the link you want to use.  

  8. Paste the embed code you copied wherever you want to embed it, such as a website or blog post.   

  9. If necessary, repeat the steps to create more stats codes for this episode by clicking the Assign Stats Tracking Code field again and entering a new name. You should create a different stats code for each player you want to get statistics for.

    Important

    If you created more than one stats code for an episode, make sure to select the right one before you copy an embed link or any other link. To do so, click the Assign Stats Tracking Code field and select the stats code you want to use. If you want to get a link that’s not associated with any stats code, click the AssignStats Tracking Code field and select Clear.  

After you create and embed stats tracking codes, you can review how many downloads each one gets.

Your show stats will tell you how many downloads you’ve received for all your tracking codes, and your episode stats will tell you how many downloads you’ve received for the tracking code on a particular episode. See Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package.

Note

You can also create a custom tracking code for an episode player. See Customize and Share a Media Player for a Specific Episode.

Libsyn 5: Miscellaneous

Set Up Apple Podcasts Subscriptions

With Apple Podcasts Subscriptions, you can charge your Apple listeners a subscription fee to access your premium content. Premium options could include ad-free listening, subscriber-only content, and early access.  

Note

When publishing a subscriber episode to Apple Podcasts through Libsyn, the only compatible file type is MP3.

 

Prerequisites

  • Set up your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed to submit to Apple Podcasts under your own Apple ID. See Submit Your Show to Apple Podcasts.

    Note

    Please set up only one feed to submit to Apple Podcasts. Apple subscriptions are designed to be an integrated experience so listeners can access both free and paid content from the same show page.
