  • If you have previously chosen the option to have Libsyn submit your show to Apple Podcasts directly, you must transfer show ownership in Apple Podcasts Connect to your own Apple ID by generating a token in your Apple Podcasts Connect account. Once the token has been generated, you can enter the token into the Apple Ownership Token field in your Libsyn Podcast Settings.

To set up Apple Podcasts Subscriptions in Libsyn:

  1. Click the icon at the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Apple Podcasts Subscriptions to display the Apple Podcasts Subscriptions page.

     

  3. Click Get an Apple ID to create an Apple ID if you don’t have one.  

  4. Click Create Subscription to create or sign in to your Apple Podcasts Connect account and join the Apple Podcasters Program. 

    • Complete your agreement with Apple Podcasts, including contact, tax, and banking information.
    • Set up subscription information with Apple for your show or channel (for multiple shows).  

  5. On Libsyn’s Apple Podcasts Subscriptions page, click Get API Key to generate and download an API key file from Apple to your computer.   

    Important

    Do not change the file name. The file name includes the provider ID and your key ID, which is needed for Apple authorization. 

     

  6. Click Upload API Key.  

  7. Browse your computer and select your API key file. The file type is .pem.  

  8. Click Open to upload the file.  

    • You are now connected and ready to publish subscriber episodes. See Publish a Subscriber Episode to Apple Podcasts.

    • Click Delete File if you want to delete your API key file.

       

  9. Optionally, you can choose to add your existing episodes to the subscription. This will create subscription versions of all of your past and future episodes. If you have dynamic ads on these episodes, the subscription versions will no longer have dynamic ads, but the free versions will.

Understand the Difference Between IAB and Unique Downloads

When you review your download stats in Libsyn, you can look at the number of IAB downloads as well as the number of Unique downloads to gain a deeper understanding of how your show is doing. This article explains the differences between Unique and IAB downloads in terms of what they measure and how to quickly switch from one to the other and then back again.

Understand Download Stats

Counting how many of your show’s episodes your audience downloaded is a key source of data for determining how well your show is doing. Having accurate download counts is important to build advertiser confidence and to dependably portray a show’s popularity, both of which are crucial when trying to grow your podcast and revenue.

However, what counts as a download can vary depending on several factors. To offer a wide range of significant statistics, Libsyn measures episode downloads using two different definitions of a download: IAB downloads and Unique downloads.

What Is a Unique Download?

A “Unique” download refers to a set of metrics specifically developed by Libsyn. The metrics differentiate between raw requests for downloads and download requests from single users.

The Unique downloads algorithm accounts for two important pieces of information:

  • IP Address
  • User Agent (Apple Podcasts, Chrome, etc.)

If a request or group of requests (such as in a progressive download) comes from a single IP address using the same user agent during a fixed 24-hour calendar window, it is counted as a single Unique download.

What Is an IAB Download?

IAB is an acronym for the “Interactive Advertising Bureau” – an organization that (with the help of Libsyn and others) has developed a series of industry standards to define what counts as a download.

In addition to filters used by Libsyn’s Unique download metrics, our IAB-certified metrics adhere to the IAB’s 2.1 guidelines for calculating statistics. We use a fixed 24-hour calendar window to calculate unique listener sessions. Our definition of uniqueness relies on a combination of the user’s IP address and their full User Agent string, while ensuring that at least one minute of playable content has been downloaded. For IPv6 addresses, we truncate them to the first 64 bits, while IPv4 addresses are kept in their entirety.

To ensure the accuracy of our statistics, we maintain a comprehensive IP exclusion list. This list primarily comprises known datacenter IP ranges from major cloud providers such as Cloudflare, Amazon Web Services (AWS), Google Cloud, and Microsoft Azure. By excluding traffic originating from these sources, we aim to filter out automated bots and non-human activity, providing a more precise representation of genuine human users. This list is regularly updated to account for new and changing datacenter ranges.

Note

IAB statistics are the default view for all users.

How Do I Compare my IAB Numbers with My Unique Numbers?

In general, IAB metrics filter out more downloads than the Unique metric. As explained above, IAB does not count downloads from bots or from other automated sources — such as servers caching content. For this main reason, you will normally see that you have fewer IAB downloads than Unique downloads.

How Do I Use My Download Stats for Advertising or Sponsorship Opportunities?

If you have an opportunity for sponsorship or for advertising on your show — congratulations! To describe your show’s popularity, you can tell the sponsor or advertiser that your download reports are third-party certified by the IAB through Libsyn. Then, you should share your IAB numbers with the sponsor or advertiser.

What Do I Do If I Have More Questions About My Stats?

If you need more help thinking through your show and how your stats are calculated, reach out to our Support team at .

Switch Back and Forth from IAB to Unique

An IAB / Unique toggle appears in the following places:

  • On the Dashboard at the top of the Stats Snapshot section
  • On the Episode Details at the top of the Episode Stats section
  • At the top of the Statistics page (which you can access if you are on the Basic Plan, or higher)

Click the toggle to switch between IAB and Unique.

If you’re scrolling through the Statistics page to review your download stats and you don’t remember if you set the toggle to IAB or Unique, just look to the right of the section title. If the text IAB appears next to the title, the stats are for IAB downloads. If a U appears, you’re viewing Unique downloads. See the image below.

Libsyn 5: Moving an Existing Podcast to Libsyn

Move Your Podcast from Another Host to Libsyn

If you host your podcast with another provider, bringing it over to Libsyn is easy. Once you move to Libsyn, you can start enjoying our features, such as our advanced publishing and stats, our wide distribution to the most popular apps and platforms, and our excellent technical and industry support to ensure you succeed.

This article walks you through how to move your podcast to Libsyn.

Step 1: Sign up for Libsyn

To get started, sign up for a Libsyn account. See Sign Up for a Libsyn Account.

 

Step 2: Create a Show

Enter the billing information for your show. See Create a New Podcast.

 

Step 3: Import Your Content to Your Libsyn Account

Now that you have set up your account, you can import your existing podcast’s content — including all of your episodes and more — from its old location to Libsyn. See Importing Your Feed to Libsyn.

Important

Please do not proceed to Step 4 until after your feed has been imported and you have verified that all of your episodes are available at Libsyn.   

 

Step 4: Create a Tag to Update Your Directories

Now that your new Libsyn RSS feed is ready, you need to generate a new feed URL tag. Creating this tag is part of the process of getting podcast directories to update automatically to your new feed. It works in conjunction with the redirect that you’ll create in step 5. But first, to create your new feed URL tag:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.  

  2. Click Advanced Tools.

     

  3. In the row for Feed & Website Redirects, click Edit to display the Feed & Website Redirects page.  

  4. In the Use New Feed URL Tag field, select Yes.

     

  5. Click Save.

Step 5: Copy the URL for Your Libsyn Feed

Podcast directories will need the URL for your new feed. See Understand Your RSS Feed.

To find the URL for your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed:

  1. Go to your Dashboard page. See View Your Dashboard.  
  2. Scroll to the Quick Links section.  
  3. Click theicon next to Feed. 

The URL will be copied to your clipboard. 

 

Step 6: Create a 301 Redirect at Your Old Host

A 301 redirect at your old host will tell podcast directories that your URL has permanently moved to a new place. When some directories encounter the 301 redirect at your old host in conjunction with the new feed URL tag you created in Step 3, they will automatically update and start using your new Libsyn location.

You’ll need to contact your old host, provide them with the URL for your old feed with them, and instruct them to redirect it to your new Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed URL, which you copied in step 4.  

Important

After you set up the redirect, we recommend that you maintain your account with your old host for up to four weeks so that the redirect remains in place long enough to ensure that all of your directories have enough time to update to your new URL.

For your convenience, here is a list of how to set up the 301 redirect with some popular hosts:

  • Acast: Contact the support team at support@acast.com
  • Audioboom: Contact the support team at 0
  • Blubrry: Log in to your account and follow Blubrry’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect
  • Buzzsprout: Log in to your account and follow Buzzsprout’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect
  • Megaphone: Contact the support team at support@megaphone.fm or 0
  • Omny Studio: Log in to your account and follow Omny Studio’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect
  • Podbean: Log in to your account and follow Podbean’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect
  • Podomatic: Contact the support team at 0
  • Simplecast: Log in to your account and follow Simplecast’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect
  • Spotify for Creators: Log in to your account and follow Spotify’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect
  • Spreaker: Contact Spreaker’s Support Team
  • Squarespace: Log in to your account and follow Squarespace’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect
  • Transistor: Log in to your account and follow Transistor’s instructions for creating a 301 redirect

Step 7: Send Directories to Your New Libsyn Feed

When podcast directories encounter the 301 redirect at your old host (step 5) in conjunction with the new feed URL tag you created at your new host (step 3), many of them will automatically update and use your new Libsyn location going forward.

Podcast directories that automatically update include:

  • Apple Podcasts
  • BeyondPod
  • iHeartRadio
  • Overcast
  • PocketCasts
  • Spotify  

Manual Updates

Some directories won’t follow a 301 redirect to your new feed. In those cases, you’ll need to contact the directory to ask them to update the URL manually.

Podcast directories that must be updated manually include:  

  • Pandora
  • TuneIn

Our Libsyn Support Team can help! If you publish content to a directory that must be manually updated, discuss it with our Libsyn Support Team at the following email: . Libsyn Support can help you configure those directories in Libsyn and get them updated where appropriate.

 

Step 8: Close Your Account at Your Old Host

When you have made sure that all the directories you publish your episodes to are now using your Libsyn feed, you can close your account at your old host.

Congratulations and welcome to Libsyn!

 

Get More Help

If you have any questions or need more help, contact our Support Team at .

Switch from Feedburner to Libsyn

If you use Feedburner to manage your RSS feed, moving to Libsyn’s RSS feed is easy. After you move to Libsyn’s feed, you will continue to retain control over all aspects of it, including the ability to customize it or to redirect it to another feed whenever you want. At Libsyn, you will always have 100% control over your feed.

If you are already using Libsyn, we recommend that you use Libsyn’s feed for two main reasons: it works better with the iOS Podcast app, and it is easier to help you if you ever encounter any issues. If something ever goes wrong with your feed, our Support Team can help you fix it. Unfortunately, we only have a limited ability to support third-party apps.

Switching from Feedburner to Libsyn is a three-step process: first you need to change the URL that Feedburner is pulling your podcast information from. Then, you need to create a new feed URL tag in Libsyn. Finally, you can delete your feed in Feedburner.

Change the URL Feedburner Is Pulling from

Feedburner works by pulling information from an already existing feed. To get started, therefore, you need to get Feedburner to pull your podcast information from your Libsyn feed instead of the feed it’s currently pulling from.

  1. Log in to your Feedburner account.

  2. Under FEED TITLE, click the title of your podcast to open the configuration.

  3. Under the name of your show, click Edit Feed Details to open the section with the fields for your feed.

  4. In the Original Feed field, replace the URL that appears with the URL for your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed.

    Note

    To easily find the URL for your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed, see the “What Is the URL to my RSS feed?” section at the bottom of the Understand Your RSS Feed article.

  5. Click Save Feed Details.

Create a Tag for Your Feed in Libsyn

After you change the URL that Feedburner is pulling information from, you need to create a new feed URL tag in Libsyn to get Feedburner to accept the change.

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click **Advanced Tools.

    **

  3. In the row for Feed & Website Redirects, click Edit to display the Feed & Website Redirects page.

  4. In the Use New Feed URL Tag field, select Yes.

  5. Click Save.

Delete Your Feed in Feedburner

  1. Log in to your Feedburner account.

  2. Under FEED TITLE, click the title of your podcast to open the configuration.

  3. Under the title of your podcast, click Delete Feed.

  4. In the section that opens, select the With permanent redirection check box.

    Important

    Make sure you select the With permanent redirection check box before you delete the feed. If the check box is not selected, the redirection to your Libsyn feed won’t work correctly.

  5. Click Delete Feed.                  

Feedburner confirms that your feed has been deleted. It will then permanently redirect your feed to your new Libsyn feed, which will automatically update your directories (such as Apple Podcasts) as well as your subscribers to start using your new Libsyn feed.

Libsyn 5: Navigating Your Statistics

Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package

The features in the advanced stats package are available with the Advanced Plan and higher. The advanced statistics package includes everything in the basic package and more. 

To view your advanced stats:

  1. Click Stats at the top of your Libsyn 5 account. 

  2. Set your date range. (See Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package.)

  3. Scroll down the page after the Downloads by Episode section to see the sections that are only available with the advanced plan. 

    All tables include the following functionality:

    • a.: Click Previous or Next, or click a page number to scroll through the list of items.
    • b.: Click the icon and enter the first few letters of the name of the item in the Search bar. The system filters the items as you type.

Note

The advanced stats package also includes the ability to download your Unique and IAB stats. For details, see Download Stats Reports.

 

Downloads per Country

In the Downloads per Country section, you can see the countries where your podcast is most popular. The right side of the section lists countries in order from most downloads to least. The download count includes all downloads for all episodes over the time range you selected.

Here are the options you have in the Downloads per Country section for reviewing your downloads:

  • a.: Hover your mouse cursor over a country to see the download numbers for that country.
  • b.: Click the + or - icons to zoom in or out of the map. You can also click and drag the map to change its position.
  • c.: Click any country on the map to see a breakdown of your download numbers in that country by area. For example, if you click the United States, you can see downloads by state. Click the  icon to return to the global map.

Note

If you click the U.S. to see a breakdown by state, you can also click Markets in the upper-right to see your stats broken down by market area. A market area designates a region where people might receive similar radio, TV, and other media offerings, such as newspapers or magazines. The Markets option is only available for the U.S. For other countries, you can see breakdowns by regions, such as provinces.

 

Downloads per User Agent

A user agent refers to any program or app that listeners can use to access your content, such as Chrome, the Apple Podcasts app, Overcast, the Spotify app, and more. These are the programs listeners use to access content. You can review these stats to get accurate download counts and to determine where your listeners are engaging with your episodes most frequently. 

The Downloads per User Agent section gives you all of the stats associated with user agents. The download count includes all downloads for all episodes over the time range you selected.

Downloads per Destination

A destination, also known as a publication destination, is any place that you have set up in your Libsyn account to publish your episodes to, including the Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed, Spotify, Facebook, WordPress, and more.

Important

Note that Apple Podcasts pulls from your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed, which means that Apple Podcasts itself is not a destination. If you have set up your podcast to appear in Apple Podcasts, then you can review the Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed destination to get a sense of how many downloads are coming from Apple Podcasts. However, those numbers might include any other directory that is pulling from your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed.

The Downloads per Destination section gives you your destination stats. The download count includes all downloads for all episodes over the time range you selected.

Click the name of a destination to see more specific stats for that destination. 

Downloads Per Stats Code

If you have generated any custom tracking stats codes, this table shows you the number of downloads associated with each code.

YouTube

The YouTube section displays the number of views you’ve received on YouTube for the episodes you’ve published there. It displays the date the episode was published on YouTube, the number of views it has received, the average duration of each view, and the total estimated minutes that people have spent watching your episode.

Click the name of an episode to watch it on YouTube.

Note

The YouTube section displays only the number of views you’ve received on YouTube. Views are not counted as downloads and are not added into any other statistics.

Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package

Libsyn’s basic stats package lets you keep track of how well your podcast is doing. Basic stats provide information such as the number of downloads for your show over time, as well as the number of times each of your episodes have been downloaded.

Note

Libsyn also offers an advanced package. Advanced stats offer even more detail about your podcast, such as information about what apps your audience is using to download your content, where your downloads are coming from globally, and more. For more information about advanced stats, see Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package.

To access your stats, click Stats at the top of your Libsyn 5 account. 

Overview of Your Podcast Stats

When you first open the Statistics page, it provides an overview of your podcast’s statistics. You can see information such as your show’s all-time downloads, as well as downloads over the past week, month, or downloads over the last 7 days, 30 days, or year. These numbers represent your podcast as a whole, so they count downloads across all of your episodes.

Note

The difference between the metrics for the last seven days versus the last week, or the last 30 days versus the last month, is that one measures downloads over a span of days while the other measures the number of downloads in the current week or month you’re in. For example, if it’s Monday, the This Week stats will only show you the number of downloads over the last two days because the week starts on Sunday. On the other hand, Last 7 Days shows you numbers for the last seven days stretching into the previous week.

In any of the graphs, you can hover your mouse cursor over the line to see more specific numbers, as shown in the image below.

Episode Statistics

The Statistics page also provides more detailed statistics about each of your episodes. You can get a quick view of how your last five episodes are doing in the Last 5 Episodes graph, or you can search for specific episodes in the Episode Totals table.

Last 5 Episodes

The Last 5 Episodes graph provides an easy-to-read overview of your last five episodes’ performance. The bars in the graph are color coded so you can easily see how each of your episodes did in the first 7 days, 30 days, or 90 days since their release, as well as all time.

You can hover your mouse cursor over any of the bars to see a breakdown of the numbers for that episode, as shown below.

Episode Totals

The Episode Totals table lists all of the episodes you have published. The table also lists the dates you published each episode, and gives you download statistics for the first 7, 30, and 90 days after an episode’s release, as well as all-time totals.

In the Episode Totals table, you can:

  • a.: Click Previous or Next, or click a page number to scroll through all of your episodes.
  • b.: Search for a specific episode. To do so, click on the icon and enter the first few letters of the title (or a word in the title) that you’re looking for in the Search bar. The system filters out episodes as you type.
  • c.: Click a specific episode to open a page where you can see more details about that episode’s statistics. See View Statistics for a Specific Episode.

Podcast Statistics over Different Date Ranges

The final section in the basic stats package provides more information about your podcast downloads as a whole. With these features, you can review downloads over a specific date range, and you can see download activity over days, weeks, or months.

Set a Specific Date Range

The date range you set in the calendar affects which days you’ll be able to review in the graph. To change the date range, click the Statistics By Date Range button to open the calendar.

To set a date range in the calendar, you can:

  • a.: Click one of the options on the left.
  • b.: Drag your mouse cursor over the dates you want to select. Selected dates appear in green.
  • c.: Enter a number in the days up to today box to select however many days you want to view up to and including today.
  • d.: Click the boxes at the top and manually enter dates with your keyboard.

When you’re done selecting dates, click Select Dates in the upper-right of the calendar to apply them.

See Download Activity for Your Podcast as a Whole

The graph below the Statistics By Date Range button shows downloads based on the date range you selected for your podcast as a whole. That means that download numbers include all downloads for all episodes of your podcast over the date range you selected.

You can use the graph to look closely at specific dates or smaller date ranges within the larger range you set in the calendar. Here are your options for using the graph:

  • a.: Click the button in the upper-right to switch between daily, weekly, and monthly stats.
  • b.: Hover your mouse cursor over the graph line to see specific download numbers.
  • c.: Click View Table to display a table with downloads organized by date. Click Hide Table to hide it again.
  • d.: Hover over the icon and then push or pull to see more or less detail in the graph. You can push or pull the icon on the left as well as the one on the right.

 

Note

When you view your daily IAB stats, Distinct Listeners displays the number of times the episode was downloaded in a single day, as determined by listener ID. This is different from Unique Downloads, which is a set of metrics developed by Libsyn to display statistics that differentiate between raw requests for downloads and download requests from single users. Unique Downloads consider several factors, primarily IP address and user agent. For more information on Unique Downloads, see Understand the Difference Between IAB and Unique Downloads.

 

Downloads by Episode

The Downloads by Episode table lists all your episodes that received downloads during the selected date range. The table lists the dates you published each episode and how many times the episode was downloaded during the date range. 

In the Downloads by Episode table, you can:

  • a.: Click Previous or Next, or click a page number to scroll through all of your episodes.
  • b.: Search for a specific episode. To do so, click on the icon and enter the first few letters of the title (or a word in the title) that you’re looking for in the Search bar. The system filters out episodes as you type.
  • c.: Click a specific episode to open a page where you can see more details about that episode’s statistics. See View Statistics for a Specific Episode.

See a Snapshot of Your Show’s Statistics

When you log in to Libsyn, the Dashboard displays a Stats Snapshot at the top, showing downloads for your show’s episodes. You can also navigate to the Dashboard from anywhere in your account by clicking Dashboard.

 

Here is an overview of what you can do in the Stats Snapshot section:

  • a.: Click the toggle to switch from IAB download stats to Unique download stats. Click the toggle again to switch back. For more information about IAB and Unique metrics, see Understand the Difference Between IAB and Unique Downloads.

  • b.:

    The graphs show the total number of downloads you had: 

    • Since your podcast began
    • In the last month
    • In the last week

    Depending on your window size, the interface may show all three graphs stacked or only one at a time. Scroll to see additional graphs if they don’t all fit on your screen.

  • c.: Hover your mouse cursor over a point on a graph to see how many downloads you had on a specific day (or during a specific month, if you’re viewing the All Time graph).

 

Note

To see more detailed stats, such as downloads by episode and more, click Stats at the top of your Libsyn account to go to the Statistics page (which you can access if you are on the Basic Plan, or higher). For more information about the Statistics page, see Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package or Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package.

View Spotify Statistics in Libsyn

As soon as you enroll in Spotify Video, you can view Spotify streaming data directly within your Libsyn Stats.

The Spotify Stats section provides an overview of how your episodes are performing on Spotify, including total streams, trends over time, and episode-level performance.   

Note

Spotify Stats only include streaming data from Spotify. They include data from both video and audio files. 

A stream is counted when a listener plays your episode on Spotify for at least 60 seconds. See Understanding engagement metrics on Spotify for more details.

To access Spotify Stats:

  1. Click Stats at the top of your Libsyn account.  

  2. Click the Spotify Stats tab.

     

Spotify Streams Overview

At the top of the page, you’ll see a summary of your Spotify streams across different time ranges:

  • All Time
  • This Week
  • This Month
  • Last 7 Days
  • Last 30 Days
  • This Year

These metrics provide a quick snapshot of your show’s performance on Spotify.  

Total Daily Streams

The Total Daily Streams chart displays how your streams change over time.

You can:

  • View stream trends across a selected date range. (See Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package.)
  • Switch between Daily and Monthly views.
  • Click View Table to see the underlying data.

The table shows each date along with the number of streams recorded.  

Episode Totals

The Episode Totals table displays performance for individual episodes on Spotify.

For each episode, you can view:

  • Title
  • Date
  • Total Streams

Use this section to compare how different episodes are performing.

View Statistics for a Specific Episode

To view your episode-level statistics, click on an episode title while viewing your show-level statistics. See Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package. 

Alternatively, you can click the View Stats button beneath the Downloads for this Episode table while viewing your episode details. See View Your Episode Details.

The episode-level statistics include the same tables as your show-level statistics, but with data for just one episode. See Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package and Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package.

Episode-level statistics also includes a Media File Breakdown section. This section lists the total number of times each media file in the episode was downloaded. You can click on the file name to view the downloads for that file over time.

Libsyn 5: Publishing Your Episodes

About Your Episode Details

After you add your media at the top of the Add Episode form, it’s time to enter your details to prepare your episode for publication. The information you enter includes details like the episode’s title, as well as show notes that will engage your listeners and get them excited about your new episode. Or, if you prefer, you can also import the information from the ID3 tags in your media file. See Publish an Episode.

It’s important to enter the appropriate information to make sure podcast directories display and organize your episode correctly when you publish.

Note

Want to speed up your publishing process? You can create episode defaults that automatically put information into the fields in the Episode Details form so you don’t have to fill each field out every time you publish. For more information about episode defaults, see Speed up Your Publication Process with Defaults.

The sections in this article correspond to the fields in the Episode Details section of the Add Episode form. To find a section quickly, click a link in the list below.

  • Episode Title
  • Description
  • Apple Podcasts Title
  • Season Number
  • Episode Number
  • Episode Type
  • Rating
  • Category
  • Author
  • Permalink Points To
  • Episode Tags

Note

Not done entering your episode’s information but need to go? If you started writing, but you’re not ready to publish, you can save your work as a draft and come back to it later. For more information about how to do so, see Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish It Later.

 

Episode Title

Enter the title of the episode you’re uploading. Your title can include information like the episode number, such as “Episode 1: Welcome to My Awesome Show.”

The title you enter will appear as your episode title in the podcast directories where you publish, such as Apple Podcasts or Spotify. In addition, it will also appear as the blog post title in your Libsyn blog page or in places like WordPress or Blogger.

Note

The title you enter automatically appears in the Apple Podcasts Title field further down the form. If you publish to Apple Podcasts, see the “Apple Podcasts Title” section below to make sure your title meets Apple’s requirements.

 

Description

The episode description, also known as show notes, is the place where you can get your listeners excited about your new episode and offer important details about it. The description appears in the podcast directories you publish to and also serves as the content for your blog post in your Libsyn blog page, or in WordPress or Blogger (if you’re using OnPublish). It’s also included in your RSS feed, so it will appear in places like Apple Podcasts.

If you have enabled the ability to publish episodes to Apple Podcasts directly from Libsyn, the Description is a required field. See Submit Your Show to Apple Podcasts. 

You can refine your description by formatting your text (such as bolding words) and by including links and images. (See Including Images in Your Episode Description.) Note that some podcasting applications don’t always show the full range of formatting.

Notes

  • Apple Podcasts limits the episode description to 4,000 characters, so Libsyn recommends that you keep your description length below this limit.
  • Want to speed up your description writing process? You can save chunks of text — called snippets — to reuse in your descriptions. For more information about snippets, see Speed up Your Episode Description Writing with Snippets.

 

Apple Podcasts Title

If you publish to Apple Podcasts, you can use the Apple Podcasts Title field to enter an episode title that will only be used for Apple Podcasts. If you entered a title in the Episode Title field above, it appears automatically in this field. However, read on to make sure this title meets Apple’s requirements.

The reason there is a second title field for Apple Podcasts is that Apple has different requirements for titles than other podcast directories. In Apple Podcasts, the title of your episode should not contain information such as the name of your podcast. It should also not contain episode or season numbers. Apple Podcasts displays that information in other places, so the episode title should contain just the title and nothing more.

Here are a few examples:

  • Correct: Welcome to My Awesome Show
  • Incorrect: Episode 1: Welcome to My Awesome Show
  • Incorrect: Episode 1 of Alistair’s Great Show: Welcome to My Awesome Show

The title you enter in the Apple Podcasts Title field appears only in Apple Podcasts. You can keep a different title in the Episode Title field if you want it to appear differently in other places. For example, if you want your title to appear as “Episode 1: Welcome to My Awesome Show” in most podcast directories, you can enter that title in the Episode Title field. Then, you can enter “Welcome to My Awesome Show” in the Apple Podcasts Title field to make sure your show appears correctly in Apple Podcasts.

If you don’t publish on Apple Podcasts, you can leave this field blank.

Season Number

Enter the number for the season to which the episode you’re uploading belongs. If you don’t use seasons to organize episodes, you can leave this field blank.

Episode Number

The episode number you enter appears in the iOS Podcast App, and your episode appears in the correct order based on the episode number (and season number, if you’re using one).

Episode Type

Specify the kind of episode you’re uploading so that podcast directories can organize it correctly. For example, if you’re uploading a trailer of your show rather than a full episode, you can specify that detail here.

Click the Episode Type field and select the type from the list that appears. Here are your options:

  • Full: A complete, traditional episode of your show.
  • Trailer: A short promo of your show, usually between 30 and 60 seconds in length.
  • Bonus: An episode that’s not consistent with the traditional length or content of your show. For example, a bonus episode might consist of a short follow-up to a previous episode.

Rating

Use the Rating field to specify whether the episode you’re uploading is clean or explicit.

For example, if you have a clean show, but the episode you’re uploading is an outlier that contains explicit language and/or subject matter, you can use this field to label just this episode as explicit. Leave the field alone if you don’t need to update your rating for this episode.

Note

When you create a new episode, the Rating field will automatically populate with whatever you’ve set the Explicit field in your Episode Defaults to be. See Speed up Your Publication Process with Defaults.

Your episode’s (and show’s) rating is important for establishing parental controls. Also, keep in mind that some countries do not permit explicit content, so marking an episode explicit might result in your episode, and your whole show, becoming unavailable to listeners in those countries.

Category

You can create categories or select one you’ve already created to apply it to the episode. Categories are useful for organizing your episodes. For example, if you host a podcast about sports and some of your episodes are about the NFL, but others are about the NBA, you can create categories to distinguish between them.

Also, you can create a player that only lists the episodes in a certain category. (See Customize and Share the Libsyn Media Player for Your Podcast.) This feature is useful in case you want to embed the player in a webpage, but you only want it to play a certain category of your episodes. To expand on the example of the sports show above, if you’re posting a player to an NFL site, you can use an “NFL” category to make sure only NFL episodes appear in the player. 

Note that this field is different from the Category field when you set up your show. The show-level category is for specifying what your show is about so that podcast directories can organize it appropriately. This field at the episode level is for you to organize your own show in a podcast player.

To create a category:

  1. Click the Category field to place your cursor in it.
  2. Type the name of the category you want to use.
  3. Press Enter on your keyboard.

To select a category you’ve already created, click the Category field and select the category you want in the list that appears.

Author

In the Author field, enter the names of this episode’s authors if they are different from the author names you entered when you configured your show.

Entering new information in this field is useful if you’re hosting a guest that you want to feature. For example, if you are hosting a famous guest in this episode, enter your name as well as your famous guest’s name in the Author field. Doing so helps listeners find your episode if they are searching for your guest author.

The names you enter in this field appear as the authors for this episode only. If you leave the field blank, the information you entered when you configured your show appears as the author.

Note

Enter the names as you want them to appear when you publish. For example, if there are several authors, you can separate the names using either “and” or “&,” depending on your style and preferences.

 

Permalink Points To

This field provides a URL where your listeners will always be able to find the episode you are uploading. For example, your permalink might point to your Blog page, or to a different page where you will always keep the episode. It’s important that the URL you enter here is unique to this episode and that it never changes.

Note

If you want your episode permalink to point to your blog page, your permalink URL will automatically be the URL for your episode post on your Libsyn Podcast Website.

If you are not using the Libsyn blog page to house your episodes, you might use a custom URL, such as whatever blogging platform you prefer. To use the Custom URL option, generate your blog post, get the URL, and enter it in the Custom Permalink URL field.

Episode Tags

Episode Tags function like keywords to help people find your episode. For example, if your episode is about meditation, creating a “meditation” tag makes it more likely that your episode will appear to listeners who are searching for that keyword. Note that tags are most useful if your show is on YouTube because YouTube uses tags to help people find episodes. Most podcast directories, including Apple podcasts, don’t make use of tags.

Note

YouTube limits the episode tags to 400 characters, so Libsyn recommends that you keep your total tag length below this limit. Libsyn also recommends that you limit each episode to 20 tags and only apply one word to each tag.

You can enter a tag by creating a new one. In addition, Libsyn remembers tags you created previously, so you can also quickly find and reuse tags that you created for a previous episode.

To enter a tag:

  1. Click the EpisodeTags field to place the cursor in it.
  2. Select a tag you’ve used before or create a new one.
    • a.: To create a new tag, type the name of the tag and then press Enter on your keyboard.
    • b.: To select a tag you’ve used before, type the first few letters of its name to find it quickly. and then click it in the list that appears beneath the field.
  3. If necessary, repeat the steps to add more tags.

You can also remove any tags you entered by mistake by clicking the X to the right of the tag you want to remove.

Add Your Own Episode Transcript

You can upload a transcript for your episode through Libsyn. Your transcript will be used by publishing destinations (such as Apple Podcasts) that support the transcript tag in your RSS feed.

Prerequisites

  • Your transcript must be an SRT or VTT file.

  • The publishing destination must be instructed to look for your transcript.

    Note

    By default, Apple will automatically create a transcript for your podcast on Apple Podcasts, but you can instruct them to use your own transcript instead. This process differs depending on whether you submitted your show to Apple Podcasts manually or had Libsyn do it.

    • If you have an Apple Podcasts Connect account: Configure your transcript settings to display transcripts that you provide, when available. You can do this at either the show level or the episode level. For help, see Apple Podcasts’ article Transcripts on Apple Podcasts.
    • If Libsyn submitted your show to Apple Podcasts: Contact the Libsyn Support Team at support@libsyn.com to request that they configure your show’s transcript settings to display transcripts that you provide, when available. Apple’s auto-generated transcripts will display if a transcript is not uploaded in Libsyn.

To upload your transcript to Libsyn:

  1. Open the Add or Edit form for your episode. (See Publish an Episode or Edit an Episode.)

  2. Click the Add Episode Transcript area on the Assets tab. In the window that opens, navigate to the SRT or VTT file you want to upload and then double-click it. (Alternatively, you can drag and drop the file into the Add Episode Transcript area.)

  3. After the transcript file uploads, an icon indicates that a transcript is attached to the episode. Destinations can then use the transcript you have uploaded. (You can click Remove to delete the transcript from the episode.)

    • You can use the Libsyn interface to view the URL for your transcript by opening the Quick Links window from the Episode Details. See About the Quick Links Window.

    • A  tag is automatically generated in your feed at the episode level with the proper file URL and type.

      Examples:

Create a Blog Post

While Libsyn is a media host, Libsyn is also a syndication service. If you are using our OnPublish tools and/or using our Libsyn Podcast Website, you may be interested in generating a “text post,” a blog post for your blogging and microblogging channels.

Creating a text post is very similar to creating a podcast episode.

  1. Sign in to Libsyn and select the show for which you want to create a text post.   

  2. Click New in the upper-right corner.  

  3. Click Episode. The Add Episode form displays.  

  4. Upload any artwork you want to use for your blog post on the Episode Details tab:

    • Add Episode Artwork: The episode artwork primarily affects the RSS feed. While text posts aren’t pulled by Apple Podcasts or podcatchers, for anyone who pulls your feed using something like Feedly, having the image can be helpful. We do still recommend following the standard artwork requirements for consistency and the broadest compatibility.
    • Add Widescreen Episode Artwork: Has no effect on a blog post; only used in videos you create for places like YouTube and Facebook.

     

  5. Complete the Episode Details:

    • Episode Title: The episode title will display as the blog post title for your Libsyn Podcast Website, as well as for WordPress, Blogger, or any other blogging platform you release to via Libsyn.

    • Description:

      The episode description will appear as the blog post in your Libsyn blog page as well as for WordPress, Blogger, or any other blogging platform you release to via Libsyn.

      Your description can include URLs, images, and other details.

     

  6. The rest of the Episode Details will not be included in a blog, so you can leave them set to the default. However, you may wish to complete the following Episode Details that will display only on the Episode Details page within Libsyn (especially if you intend to add media in the future to convert this text post to an audio episode).

    • Season Number: You can enter a season number or leave this blank for N/A.
    • Episode Number: You can enter an episode number or leave this blank for N/A.
    • Episode Type: Select Full, Trailer, or Bonus.
    • Rating: Select Clean or Explicit to indicate a rating.
    • Author: You can enter an author name for your blog post.

     

  7. Select an option in the Availability field:

    • Immediately on Publish: Publish your post immediately.

    • Schedule Episode:

      Schedule a time to publish later, with one date for all publication destinations. See Schedule a Date to Publish Your Episode.

      • Start - Select a date and time.

     

  8. Click Publish. (You can also click Save as Draft if you want to save your progress and return to it later. See Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish it Later.)

    The Episode Details page displays for your blog post within Libsyn. See View or Edit a Blog Post.

After your episode is published, it appears on your Libsyn blog page as well as in any destinations you have added that accept text-only posts.

A published blog post displays within Libsyn in the Episodes list with a status of Published Text. You can edit it to add media if you want to convert it to an episode. See Edit an Episode.

Include Images in Your Episode Description

When adding or editing a description for your episode, you can format your text and include links and images. (Note that some podcast player apps don’t always show the full range of formatting.) See About Your Episode Details.

To include an image in your episode description:

  1. Click the Insert/edit image button in the Description area.

  2. Click the Source button in the Insert/Edit Image window.

  3. In the Choose Image window, select Upload New to click or drag a new image. Alternatively, you can select Choose Existing if you want to search for a previously uploaded image. You can use the Search field to filter the list by name, and the Order By field to switch the display order between Most Recent and File Name. Click the image to select it. 

  4. The URL for the image displays in the Source field of the Insert/Edit Image window. You can add an alternative description. 

  5. You can change the number of pixels in the Width and Height fields to resize the image. Changing the number in one field changes the number in the other field to keep the size proportionate unless you click the Constrain proportionsbutton to unlock the aspect ratio.

  6. Click Save.

  7. The image appears in the Description field. You can resize it by dragging on the corners. Click the Insert/edit image button again to make additional edits to the image.

Publish a Subscriber Episode to Apple Podcasts

You can publish subscriber content with Apple Podcasts Subscriptions. See Set Up Apple Podcasts Subscriptions.

Note

When publishing a subscriber episode to Apple Podcasts through Libsyn, the only compatible file type is MP3.

To publish a subscriber episode:

  1. Click New in the upper-right corner and select Episode to display the Add Episode form. See Publish an Episode.

  2. When subscriber content is enabled, additional options display in the Schedule section. 

  3. In the Episode Type field, select one of the following options:

    • Free Only:

      Your main audio will be available free to all listeners. 

      • A Free Episode Availability field displays.
        • Select Immediately on Publish to make the free content always available.
        • Select Schedule Episode to add a start date. You can also add an end date or limit the media availability so it cannot be accessed before the scheduled publication date. See Schedule a Date to Publish Your Episode.

    • Subscriber Only:

      Your main audio will not be submitted on your feed. It will only be submitted through Apple as a subscriber episode, and only available to listeners who have subscribed to your content with the Apple Podcasts app.

      • A Subscriber Episode Availability field replaces the Free Availability field.
        • Select Immediately on Publish to make the subscriber content available right away.
        • Select Schedule Episode to add a start date and (optionally) an end date.

    • Free and Subscriber:

      Your main audio will be served as a free episode via your RSS feed. Optionally, you can add a subscriber audio file that will be served to your paying subscribers. Your subscribers will get the free episode when a subscriber audio file is not available. 

      • A Free Episode Availability field displays.
        • Select Immediately on Publish to make the free content available right away.
        • Select Schedule Episode to add a start date. You can also add an end date or limit the media availability so it cannot be accessed before the scheduled publication date. See Schedule a Date to Publish Your Episode.
      • A Subscriber Episode Availability field displays.
        • Select Immediately on Publish to make the subscriber content always available.
        • Select Schedule Episode to add a start date and (optionally) an end date.
      • Click Add Subscriber Audio to upload your subscriber media file.
        • Your subscriber audio file must have a different name than your main audio file.
        • You can set the release dates so your paying subscribers can listen to your episode earlier. 
        • Your subscriber audio file could include extra content, or the entire episode could be a bonus episode.
        • Subscriber audio will not include Auto Ads or dynamically inserted ads. You should upload a second audio file if the main audio file has baked-in ads that you do not want to serve to subscribers.

     

  4. Click Save & Continue (see Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish It Later) or Publish.

    After you publish, your subscriber episodes are distributed to Apple Podcasts Connect with the availability you specified.

    Note

    If you’ve been using Apple Podcasts Connect to upload subscriber episodes, you won’t see them in the Libsyn dashboard, but they are still available on Apple Podcasts. After you’ve begun publishing subscriber audio with Libsyn, we recommend that you continue to publish only through Libsyn to avoid confusion.

Publish an Episode

When you’ve recorded an episode of your show and you’re ready to share it, sign in to Libsyn to upload and distribute it.

Note

Before you publish an episode, make sure you have configured your show and set up your publications correctly. See Are You Ready to Publish an Episode?

After you upload an episode, Libsyn stores it and makes it available to share with the world. For example, you can have your episode appear on popular podcast directories, like Apple Podcasts or Spotify. Or, you can share it with individual people, or publish it in a blog, tweet, or post, among other options.

Note

Signing in to Libsyn is not the only way to publish an episode. See Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episode.

To sign in to Libsyn and publish an episode:

  1. Sign in to Libsyn and select the show for which you want to publish an episode. 

  2. Click New in the upper-right corner.

  3. Click Episode. The Add Episode form displays.

  4. Add your media file at the top of the page. See Upload a Media File.

  5. Upload any square artwork you want to use just for this episode in the Artwork section, or skip this step if you want your podcast artwork to appear for the episode. See Upload Episode Artwork.

  6. Add your title, description, and any additional details in the fields in the Episode Details section. See About Your Episode Details.

  7. Select an option in the Availability field in the Schedule section:

    • Immediately on Publish:

      Publish your episode immediately. (You must select this if you are publishing your first episode.)

    • Schedule Episode:

      Schedule a time to publish later, with one date for all publication destinations. See Schedule a Date to Publish Your Episode.

      • Start - Select a date and time.
      • Media Availability - Select one of the following:
        • Always available
        • Based on release schedule

     

  8. Select the Update ID3 tags checkbox if you want to export the information you entered on this page to the ID3 tags in the media file that you uploaded. For more information about ID3 tags, see Manage the Episode Information in Your Media Files.

  9. Click Save & Continue to save your progress and move on to the Assets tab, or click Publish to publish your episode.

  10. On the Assets tab:

    • Upload any widescreen artwork you want to use just for this episode. You can skip this step if you don’t want widescreen artwork to appear for the episode. See Upload Episode Artwork.
    • Upload your transcript if you want to provide your own transcript on Apple Podcasts instead of using Apple’s autogenerated transcript. See Add Your Own Episode Transcript.

  11. If your show has automatic ads enabled, click Save & Continue to save your progress and move on to the Advertising tab. If it doesn’t, click Publish.

  12. If advertising is turned on for your show, you will be able to serve ads for this episode. 

    • You will see options to include mid-roll, pre-roll, and post-roll ads in this episode if you have automatic ads enabled. See Manage Automatic Ad Settings for an Episode.
    • You will see a Save and Continue to Libsyn4 Slot Picker link if you joined our advertising program through Libsyn Pro. Click to save the information you have entered so far and log into Libsyn4 to pick your advertising slots. See Working with Advertising Campaigns.

  13. Click Publish. (You can also click Save as Draft if you want to save your progress and return to it later. See Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish it Later.)

Congratulations! Your new episode is now published!

After you publish, your episode appears in all of the publishing destinations – such as podcast directories and social media – to which you’ve connected your show (there might be a delay in certain places while they process your episode). The episode also appears in your Episodes list, where you can view or edit it, and review stats.

Note

You must use the Social Destinations calendar to publish to Facebook or LinkedIn if you are on the Advanced Plan and higher. See Schedule an Episode to Appear on Social Media Using the Social Destinations Calendar.

Publish to Spotify Video

Optimizing Your Video File

While no additional steps are required to publish video episodes to Spotify, optimizing your video files can help improve processing and playback performance.

To ensure the best playback experience on Spotify, consider the following when preparing your video files:

  • Use a standard format such as MP4.
  • Export using a common codec like H.264.
  • Aim for 720p to 1080p resolution for a balance of quality and file size.
  • Use a constant bitrate (CBR):
    • 3 Mbps for 720p
    • 5–10 Mbps for 1080p

Spotify will process and optimize your video after upload, and playback quality may vary depending on the listener’s device and connection.

For full technical specifications, see Spotify’s video guidelines.  

Publishing Workflow

Once Spotify Video is enabled for your show, you can continue using Libsyn’s standard publishing workflow. 

To publish a video episode, upload a video file when creating your episode, and publish as usual. No additional steps are required to send your video to Spotify. See Publish an Episode.  

Delivery to Spotify

When you publish a video episode:

  • Your video is automatically delivered to Spotify.
  • Your audio continues to be distributed via your RSS feed as normal.

You do not need to submit your episode separately to Spotify.  

Availability and Playback

After publishing, your video is processed and delivered to Spotify. Availability may take some time and is not always immediate.

On Spotify:

  • Listeners have the option to watch or listen to your episode.
  • Video playback and the ability to switch to audio-only may vary depending on the listener’s device and app.

Spotify controls how video content is displayed and played within its platform.  

Spotify Stats in Libsyn

When your show is connected to Spotify, playback and streaming data from Spotify is available within your Libsyn Stats.

You can use this data to understand how your episodes are performing on Spotify. See View Spotify Stats in Libsyn.

 

Important Notes About Spotify Video

Spotify Video currently has several platform-specific limitations and behaviors to be aware of.

 

Spotify for Creators Access

Spotify’s claiming workflow currently prioritizes shows distributed through RSS feeds.

As a result, some Spotify Video shows or video-only listings may have limited access to Spotify for Creators features, including certain analytics and creator tools.

 

Artwork Management

After enabling Spotify Video, some artwork management options in Spotify for Creators may become unavailable.

To ensure consistent updates, we recommend managing artwork through Libsyn whenever possible.

 

Episode Management

Some Spotify Video management features may be limited after an episode has been published.

Depending on Spotify’s current platform functionality, certain actions — including removing published video episodes directly through Spotify for Creators — may not always be available.

Save Your Episode as a Draft to Publish It Later

Entering all the information to publish your episode is a lot of work! If you’re not done, you can save your progress and return to it later. To do so:

  1. At the bottom of the Assets and Advertising tabs, click Save AsDraft.

    Note

    If you click Save & Continue on the Episode Details tab and leave the Add Episode form, the most recent updates will get saved to your episode, and your episode will appear as a Draft.

     

  2. When you’re ready to start working on your episode again, sign in to Libsyn and click Episodes in the top of your Libsyn account to display your Episodes list.

  3. Find the episode you want to work on.

    Tip

    There are two ways to find your drafts quickly:

    • Click Filter in the upper-right of the Episodes table and select Draft to see only the episodes in draft status.
    • Enter the first few letters of the episode name in the Search field in the upper-left of the Episodes table. The system filters episodes as you type.

     

  4. Click on the far right of the episode row and click Edit. The Edit Episode form opens and you can continue to work on your episode.

Schedule a Date to Publish Your Episode

Libsyn gives you flexibility and control over when your episodes get published. For an overview of how to publish an episode, see Publish an Episode.

You can schedule a new episode to be released at the same time on all the publication destinations to which you’ve connected your show, including podcast directories, social media, and elsewhere.

Note

For details about individually scheduling posts on sites such as Facebook and LinkedIn, see Schedule an Episode to Appear on Social Media Using the Social Destinations Calendar (available for the Advanced Plan and higher).

To schedule a date for publication:

  1. At the bottom of the Episode Details section on the Add Episode form, click the Availability field and select Schedule Episode.  

  2. In the Start field, click the icon to select the date and time that you want to publish your episode.

     

  3. In the Media Availability field, choose one of the following:

    • a.: Always available: Choose this option if you want to make your episode available to share before the date you’ve scheduled it to be published. This option is useful if you want to share your episode with someone who needs to listen to it before the general public does. (Note that Libsyn stats will start tracking your episode as soon as you make it available.)
    • b.: Based on release schedule: Choose this option if you don’t want your episode to be available to anyone before the scheduled time for publication.

     

  4. Click Publish.

    • If you selected Always available, Libsyn stats will begin to track your episode immediately.
    • Your episode will publish on the day and time you entered in the Release field to all the publishing destinations in which you configured your show to appear.

Schedule an Episode to Appear on Social Media Using the Social Destinations Calendar

After you upload a new episode to Libsyn, Libsyn’s scheduling features make it easy to promote it on on Facebook or LinkedIn. For example, you might want to post your new episode to social media several times over the next few weeks to gain traction with your followers and make sure they see your new content. With Libsyn’s social media promotional tools, you can easily schedule several posts to go out on specific days and times.

After you schedule your posts, you can review the Social Destinations calendar to see when and where they are due to be published so that you can easily keep track of your schedule.

Note

Managing your social media posts through the Social Destinations calendar is only available on the Advanced Plan and higher. If you do not have access to the Social Destinations calendar, you can manage your social media posts when you publish your episodes, or when you schedule an episode for publication.

To schedule an episode to appear on social media:

  1. Publish your episode as you normally would. After it’s uploaded to Libsyn, you can schedule your social media posts.

    Note

    When you have access to the Social Destinations calendar, clicking Publish on the Add Episode page does not publish or schedule your episodes for Facebook or LinkedIn. You must use the Social Destinations calendar to publish to these destinations if you are on the Advanced Plan and higher.

     

  2. Click Social in the top of your Libsyn account. The Social Destinations calendar opens.

  3. Click the icon in the upper-right corner of the page. The New window opens.

  4. In the Destination Name field, select the social media account where you want to schedule a post for your new episode. Note that social media accounts appear in this menu only if you’ve connected your show to them. See Are You Ready to Publish an Episode? for more information.

    Note

    If you select Facebook OnPublish, a Post As Text/Post As Video toggle appears beneath the Episode field. Move the toggle to Post As Video for Libsyn to send a video post to Facebook. If a video episode is selected, the file is sent straight to Facebook. If an audio episode is selected, Libsyn will use your artwork combined with your audio file to generate a video and push that out to Facebook. If you turn the toggle to Post As Video, you don’t have to select a link in step 9 below.

     

  5. In the Publish Date field, click the icon to open a window where you can enter the time and select the date when you want to schedule your post.

  6. In the Message field, enter the content that you want to appear in your post.

  7. In the Episode field, select the episode that you want to post.

  8. Optionally, upload an image if you want to use artwork for this post that is different from your regular show artwork or different from the default artwork displayed. You can upload the image in one of the following two ways:

    • a.: Click the Click or Drag New Image button. In the window that opens, navigate to the file you want to upload and then double-click it.
    • b.: Find the file you want on your computer, and then click and drag it to the Click or Drag New Image button. When you let go of the mouse button, the upload begins.

     

  9. In the Link To field, select the link that you want to provide for your audience to access your episode. If you select Custom URL, enter the link you want to use in the Custom URL field that appears.

  10. Click Save. Your post is scheduled and appears in the Social Destinations calendar.

Review Your Social Media Schedule

After you schedule your posts to appear on your social media channels, you can review your schedule in the Social Destinations calendar. Your scheduled posts appear as “cards” in the calendar, and the title of the episode you’re posting appears in each one so that you can keep track of them.

Here are all of the features available in the calendar:

  • a.: See where your posts are scheduled to appear at a glance with the color-coded cards. Dark blue is Facebook and medium blue is LinkedIn.
  • b.: Click any number on the calendar to see the that day’s schedule in greater detail. You can also click the Today button in the upper-right of the calendar to see today’s schedule. To return to the calendar from the day’s schedule, click the  icon in the upper-right corner.
  • c.: Click the green arrows next to the month to move up a month or down a month.
  • d.: Hover your mouse cursor over any of the cards to see more information about it, such as when the post is scheduled to be released, or when it was released if it was already posted.
  • e.: Click any card to open a window where you can see the information you entered. If the post has not yet been released, you can edit the information and save it.

Speed up Your Episode Description Writing with Snippets

Writing an engaging and detailed episode description (aka, show notes) to prepare your episode for publication can be involved. However, if you repeat certain content in all of your show notes, you can speed up your description writing by using snippets.

A snippet is a chunk of text that you can save in Libsyn to reuse in your show notes. You can create and save as many snippets as you want. For example, you might have some promotional content that you only use from time to time. Saving that content in a snippet lets you forget about it until you want to use it again; then, you can conveniently and quickly add it to your show notes whenever you want.

 

Create a Snippet

Next time you are writing in the Description field in the Add Episode form to prepare an episode for publication, you can create one or more snippets for future use. Here’s how:

  1. In the Description field, click Snippets. The Snippets window opens.

  2. In the Snippets window, click +New.

  3. Enter the name you want to call your snippet in the Title field.

  4. Enter your snippet content in the Content field.

  5. Click Save. 

  6. Repeat the steps above to enter more snippets, or click Done to finish and close the Snippets window. The snippets you created are now ready to insert in your show notes.

Insert a Snippet into Your Show Notes

After you’ve created one or more snippets, you can quickly insert them into your show notes to speed up your writing process. To do so:

  1. In the Description field, place your cursor wherever you want to enter the snippet.

  2. Click Snippets. The Snippets window opens.

  3. Find the snippet you want to insert. If necessary, you can find the snippet by using the Search field. To do so, enter the first few letters of the snippet title and then press Enter on your keyboard.

  4. Click the snippet you want to insert. It now appears in your show notes.

Update or Delete a Snippet

If you want to change the title of a snippet or its content, you can update it. Or, if you don’t need a snippet anymore, you can delete it.

  1. In the Description field, click Snippets.

  2. Find the snippet you want to update or delete. If necessary, you can find the snippet by using the Search field. To do so, enter the first few letters of the snippet title and then press Enter on your keyboard.

  3. Click Edit in the row that contains the snippet you want to update or delete.

  4. Update or delete the snippet.

    • a.: To update the snippet, change the title, content, or both, and then click Save.
    • b.: To delete the snippet, click Delete. Then, in the window that opens, click Delete again to confirm.

Speed up Your Publication Process with Defaults

When you publish an episode, you need to enter information about it so that it appears correctly in your publication destinations. To speed up your publication process, you can enter default information that appears automatically in the Add Episode form.

For example, if you know that your episodes will almost always have a clean rating, you can use defaults to select Clean so that it always appears automatically in the Explicit field when you’re preparing an episode for publication. The default saves you from having to select that option every time you publish an episode.

The information you enter as default is just a starting point to help speed up your publication prep. You can always change any default settings when you publish an episode if they don’t apply.

To set up episode defaults:

  1. If you have more than one show, make sure you select the show for which you want to set defaults before you start. See Add an Another Podcast to Your Libsyn Account.

  2. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

     

  3. Click Episode to display the Episode Defaults form.

  4. Enter the information you want to appear automatically when you prepare an episode for publication. You can leave any field blank if you don’t want to enter a default for it. 

    • Category: Enter the category for your episode. For more information about episode categories, see the “Category” section in About Your Episode Details.

    • Permalink Points To: Enter the URL to which you want your publication to point. For more information about your options, see the “Permalink Points To” section in About Your Episode Details.

    • Update ID3 Tags:

      ID3 tags are the metadata in your MP3 file. Metadata are pieces of info about the file such as title, author name, and description.

      You can select one of the following options as the default for your Add Episode form:

      • Import from MP3 File: Select this option to automatically turn on the Populate Form with ID3 Data option when you publish an episode. This option uses the information in your ID3 tags to import all relevant information to Libsyn. 
      • Export to Episode MP3 File: Select this option to automatically select the Export info from this form to ID3 tags in MP3 file option when you publish an episode. This option takes the information you write in the Add Episode form and uses it to update the ID3 tags in your episode’s media file. 
      • None: Select this option to leave both ID3 options in the Episode Details form turned off when you publish an episode.

      For more information, see Manage the Episode Information in Your Media Files.

    • Explicit: Enter whether your episodes are clean or explicit. For more information about ratings, see the “Explicit” section in About Your Episode Details.

    • Season Number: Enter the season number you’re currently on. When you change to a new season number, remember to come back to this field and change it. If you don’t use seasons for your show, leave this field blank.

    • Episode Number: Enter the episode number that you are currently on. For example, if your next publication is episode 6, enter 6. Libsyn automatically tracks your publications and increases your default number by one when you publish to help with episode numbering and to speed up publication. For example, if you enter 6, the Episode Number field will automatically be labeled with a 6 in the Episode Details form when you prepare your next publication, and the publication after that will automatically be labeled 7, and so on.

    • Schedule Release:

      Select one of the following options:

      • None: Select this option if you don’t want to set up a default schedule because you need to set up a different schedule every time you publish.
      • Set Schedule for All Destinations: Select this option if you want to set up a default schedule. See Schedule One Date to Publish Your Episode to All Your Destinations below.

     

  5. Click Save. Next time you publish an episode, the information you entered above appears automatically in the Add Episode form for your new episode.

Schedule One Date to Publish Your Episode to All Your Destinations

If you have a set schedule for preparing episodes and publishing them, you can set up a default schedule for your episodes. For example, if you usually prepare your episodes for publication on a Friday and release them on the following Monday, you can set up Mondays as your default publishing day.

This option lets you set up one default time to publish your episode to all of the publication destinations you’ve connected your show to, including podcast directories, websites, and social media sites. To do so:

  1. In the Episode Defaults form, in the Schedule Release field, select Set Schedule for All Destinations.

  2. In the Media Availability field that displays, select one of the following options:

    • Media files are always available: Select to make your episode available to share before the date you’ve scheduled it to be published. This option is useful if you want to share your episode with someone who needs to listen to it before the general public does. (Note: Libsyn stats will begin to track your episode as soon as you make it available to share.)
    • Media files are available based on release schedule: Select if you don’t want your episode to be available to anyone before the scheduled time for publication.

     

  3. In the Release Type field that displays, select one of the following options:

    • Release Immediately on Publish:

      Select if you want your episode to be published immediately to all of your publication destinations when you click Publish.

    • Delay Release by Number ofDays: Select  if you want your episode to be published to all of your publication destinations a specific number of days after you click Publish. Enter the number of days you want to wait for your episode to be published in the Release Delay field, and enter the time you want to publish the episode in the field to the right of it.

    • Delay Until Next Day of Week: Select this option if you want to publish your episode on a specific day of the upcoming week after you click Publish. Enter the day of the upcoming week you want to publish your episode in the Release Day field, and enter the time you want to publish the episode in the field to the right of it.

     

  4. Click Save.

Upload a File for Download Only

If you want to upload a file and simply get a URL to share with people so they can download that file directly, you can upload a File for Download Only. This is different than a podcast episode because the podcast episode releases out to all of your destinations (such as your feed, your blog page, and your social destinations).

To upload a file for download only:

  1. Open the File Manager page. See Manage Your Files.

  2. In the Active Files section, click the Add New button.

  3. In the window that opens, select the file you want to upload.

When the upload is complete, a notification appears in the upper-right and the file displays in the Active Files list. You can click a file name to open the File Details window.

  • Click Convert to Episode to convert an audio or video file to an episode. See Upload Your New Episode.
  • After you have converted the file to an episode, the Convert to Episode button changes to a View Episode button and a Permalink URL button displays. 
  • For more details about this window, see About the File Details.

Upload a Media File

A key part of publishing an episode is adding the media file. You can upload an audio, video, or PDF file on the Add Episode form. See Publish an Episode.

Compatible file types include:  

  • mp3
  • m4a
  • mp4
  • m4v
  • ogg
  • pdf

Note

For recommended audio encoding, see Some Tips on Content Creation and File Preparation.

 

When uploading or replacing a media file in Libsyn, the file name must be unique for your show. If the name matches a file you have previously uploaded (even if the file is not attached to an episode but exists in your active files) the upload will fail. If this occurs, rename the file (for example, by adding “_v2” to the end of the file name) and upload it again.

 

To upload a new media file on the Add Episode form:

  • Click the Media File Uploader at the top of the Add Episode form to open a window where you can search for and select the file you want to upload.

     

  • Alternatively, you can search for and select the file you want to upload and then drag and drop it onto the Media File Uploader.

After you perform one of these options, a gear icon along with the text, “Processing” appears at the top of the Add Episode form to indicate that the file is being uploaded. When the upload is complete, a notification appears in the upper-right.

If you previously uploaded a file to Libsyn or recorded a Connect call (that you have not already used in a Libsyn episode), you can select it and enter information for it. To do so, click the Media Library button. For details, see Use Your Media Library.

After you have selected or uploaded a file, it appears on the Add Episode form as a player.  

  • Click to download the file.
  • Click to preview the file.
  • Click Remove to remove the file. (You will be able to click Undo Remove to reverse this.)

You can now fill in the details for your episode. See About Your Episode Details.

 

Note

In addition to uploading files using the New Episode section as outlined above, you also have other ways of uploading an episode, such as by using FTP.

Upload Episode Artwork

When you publish a new episode, you should also upload specific artwork for that episode. For example, you may want to include the episode number in your artwork.

Note

If you do not upload episode artwork, your Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed will not display any artwork for that episode. Other destinations will display your show artwork for the episode.
