You can upload new episode artwork and new episode widescreen artwork. Episode artwork must be square and will appear in most places you publish, like Apple Podcasts, the Libsyn HTML5 player, and more. The widescreen artwork, on the other hand, appears in videos you create for places like YouTube and Facebook.

Note that there are some requirements for the square and the widescreen artwork.

  • Episode Artwork Requirements: Episode Widescreen Artwork Requirements
    • 1400 by 1400 px (minimum)
    • 3000 by 3000 px (maximum)
    • Must be square
    • Under 500 kb
    • Use sRGB color space
    • JPG or PNG files only
    :
    • Must measure 1920 by 1080 px
    • At least 72 dpi

Note

If you’re looking for some tips on how to create great episode artwork, you can check out our Libsyn blog post about artwork FAQs.

To upload your artwork in the Add Episode form, do one of the following:

  • a.: Click the Add Episode Artwork area on the Episode Details tab, or click the Add Widescreen Episode Artwork area on the Assets tab. In the window that opens, navigate to the file you want to upload and then double-click it.
  • b.: Find the file you want on your computer, and then click and drag it to the Add Episode Artwork area on the Episode Details tab, or click and drag it to the Add Widescreen Episode Artwork area on the Assets tab. When you let go of the mouse button, the upload begins.

After the artwork uploads, it appears as a preview in the appropriate section. Click Remove to remove the file. (You will be able to click Undo Remove to reverse this.)

Use Your Media Library

Your Media Library can be used to manage the files that you have previously uploaded. This can include files you uploaded through the Add Episode form, (whether published or unpublished), files you uploaded through FTP, or files you recorded with Connect.

To access your Media Library:

  1. Click New in the upper-right corner of your Libsyn account.

  2. Click Episode. The Add Episode form displays. See Publish an Episode.

  3. Under the Audio File Uploader, click Media Library.

  4. In the Media Library window, click in the row for the file you want to add to an episode and click Select. There are separate tabs for unreleased files, published files, and files you recorded in Connect.

    Note

    Connect files do not display in the list if they are already being used in an episode.

After you have selected a file, it appears on the Add Episode form as a player. See Upload a Media File.

Libsyn 5: Recording a Call with Connect

Join a Connect Call as a Guest

To join a call in Connect:

  1. In your email invitation, click the Join Event button or follow the link.

  2. Fill out the Name field. The name you choose will display in the Connect call. 

  3. Click **Join Call as Guest

    **

  4. Select your options on the Join Call page. See Use the Join Call Page in Connect.

  5. Click Join Call.

If the call was scheduled for the future, you will enter the lobby.

You will enter the call when the host admits you. If browser notifications are enabled, a desktop notification may appear when the host admits you. 

In the call session, your options include the following:

  • Call Notes:

    • If the host has shared the call notes with you, the previously entered call notes display in the box.
    •  - If the host gives you edit privileges, you can click the pencil icon to edit the notes. Click Save to save your changes or Discard to close without saving. Only one participant can edit the call notes at a time.
  • Live Chat
    Type something in the field below the window and hit Enter to communicate with your guests during the call.

  • Your Mic:

      • (Not available while the call is being recorded.) Click to display a dropdown menu of your available microphones. Select the microphone you want to use for the call. 
    • Microphone Name - The name of your selected microphone displays above the view meter.
    • View Meter - The view meter displays your audio levels.
      • Click to mute yourself.

  • Guests:

    This area displays a list of email addresses and statuses for the guests. The statuses are:

    • Not Here Yet - Displays when the guest is neither in the call nor waiting in the lobby.
    • Ready to Join - Displays when the guest is waiting in the lobby. 
    • On the Call - Displays when the guest has joined the call.

  • Record:

    • The recording status displays as either Not Recording or Recording.
    • A counter displays the length of a recording in progress.

  • Leave Call:

    Click to leave the call.

After the call has ended, you can view its details and any recordings. See View the Details of a Connect Call.

 

Note

If a guest loses connection during the call, the host might need to readmit them once they reconnect.

Join and Record a Connect Call as a Host

To join a call in Connect as a host:

  1. Click Join Call on the Join Call page. See Use the Join Call Page in Connect. The call session screen displays:

  2. In the call session, your options include the following:

    • Call Notes:

      • Your previously entered call notes display in the box.
      •  - Click to edit the call notes. Click Save to save your changes or Discard to close without saving. Only one participant can edit the call notes at a time.

    • Guest Edit: As the host, you can toggle this option on to allow guests to edit the call notes.

    • Live Chat
      Type something in the field below the window and hit Enter to communicate with your guests during the call. You will have the option to export the chat at the end of the call.

    • Your Mic:

        • (Not available while the call is being recorded.) Click to display a dropdown menu of your available microphones. Select the microphone you want to use for the call. 
      • Microphone Name - The name of your selected microphone displays above the view meter.
      • View Meter - The view meter displays your audio levels.
        • Click to mute yourself.
      • Invite Guests: As the host, you can click this button to send more email invitations to people you want to join the active call.

    • Guests:

      This area displays a list of email addresses and statuses for the guests. The statuses are:

      • Not Here Yet - Displays when the guest is neither in the call nor waiting in the lobby.

      • Ready to Join - Displays when the guest is waiting in the lobby. 

        • Admit - As the host, you can click the Admit button next to the guest’s name to allow them to join the call.
        • Deny - As the host, you can click the Deny button next to the guest’s name to deny them access to the call. Denied guests receive a notification stating that the host has denied them entry.

      • On the Call - Displays when the guest has joined the call. 

        • End - As the host, you can click an End button next to a guest’s name to remove them from the call.
          • As the host, you can click the microphone next to a guest’s name to mute them. 

    • Record:

      • As the host, you can click a Record button to begin recording. Click Stop when you are finished.
      • Guests will see either Not Recording or Recording.
      • A counter displays the length of a recording in progress.
      • We recommend that you record a local backup of the interview so that you have another copy in case of any difficulties. See Record a Local Backup for Connect.

    • End Call:

      Click to end the call for everyone.

    Note

    If a guest loses connection during the call, the host might need to readmit them once they reconnect. 

  3. After the call has ended, your recordings may need a few minutes to process. You will receive an email when the recordings finish processing. You can view any recordings on the call’s Detail page. See View the Details of a Connect Call.

    If the call’s Detail page does not display any recordings, your recordings may still be processing.

  4. Click Push to Libsyn to enter your episode details and proceed to publishing your episode in Libsyn. If you have multiple recordings, select the one you want to add to Libsyn and press Go.  See About Your Episode Details.

 

Record a Local Backup for Connect

Connect is a great option for recording remote interviews for your podcast. It gives you crystal clear audio while recording multiple callers at the same volume, with the option to record separate audio tracks. See Join and Record a Connect Call as a Host 

Your Connect recordings are saved on Libsyn’s servers, and then you can download them to your own computer. We highly recommend creating and saving your own backup recording during a call as an extra precaution. While our platform strives for reliability, it’s always a good idea to have a backup plan in case of unexpected issues. There are several ways to create and save a backup.

  • Audio Recorder/Interface: The best way to record a local backup of a Connect call is by using an external audio recorder/interface such as the Zoom Podtrak P4 or a RODECaster Pro. You can plug your headphones into the recorder/interface and then connect it to your computer with a USB cable. The recorder/interface will remove your voice from the mix you hear in your headphones, so you won’t hear yourself on a delay in your headphones as you talk. It will create a recording which you can later load onto your computer for editing.

  • Audio Recorder Connected to an Interface: Another way you can record a local backup is by using a portable audio recorder such as a Zoom H1n or your smartphone. For best results, connect the recorder to your computer using an audio interface and compatible cables.

  • Local Audio or Video Software - You can use software on your computer that’s designed for recording multiple tracks. This includes audio software such as Hindenburg or video software such as Camtasia.

  • Online Screen Recorder: You can also use online software designed to capture your screen and audio while you are running Connect. Some screen recorders include Movavi Screen Recorder, HitPaw Screen Recorder, and ScreenRec. 

  • External Audio Recorder: For the simplest setup, just place a portable audio recorder next to your computer, or use a recording app on your smartphone. For this option, you will need to set the audio output on your computer to use the computer’s speakers instead of your headphones so that the recording device can pick up the sound from the other callers. (This option should be a last resort, as it can result in poor quality.)

Set Up a Recording in Connect

You have access to Libsyn Connect if you are on the Advanced Plan and higher. See About the Plan Levels.

Note

Creating a show is a prerequisite for accessing the Connect feature. See Set Up Your Podcast.

You can use Connect to record a high-quality call between multiple participants through your computer. Audio files recorded in Libsyn Connect are .WAV format and recorded in 44100khz sample rate — perfect for podcast post production. You can also download master tracks in MP3 format for smaller file sizes. 

Note

The current version of Connect is best-suited for Mac and Windows laptops or desktop computers using a Chrome, Firefox, Safari, Microsoft Edge, or Brave browser. It is not fully supported on mobile devices. 

 

To start or schedule a call in Connect:

  1. Click New in the upper-right of your Libsyn account and select Connect Call to display the Call List.

    Alternatively, you can log in directly to Connect at connect.libsyn.com.

  2. Click New in the upper-right of your Connect account to display the Set Call Options page.

  3. Complete the following:

    • Call Title:

      Enter a title for the call.

    • Invitee Email: Type the email addresses of your guests. You can separate each email address by pressing Enter, pressing Tab, or typing a comma. Each participant will receive an email with a link to join the call.

    • Host Name
      Your name automatically displays.

    • Start Now: Toggle this option on if you want to start the call now instead of scheduling it for a future time.

    • Date/Time: (This field does not display if you selected Start Now.) Select the date and time for which you would like to schedule your call.

    • Call Duration: Select a call duration between 5 minutes and 5 hours. You can select in 5-minute increments.

    • Call Notes: You must type your call notes. The call notes section can be used to include a biography of your guest(s), an agenda, or anything you’d like to share with the attendees.

    • Share Call Notes with Guests: Toggle this option on if you want to share your call notes with your guests.

    • Create Individual Tracks: Toggle this option on to enable individual track recording. Each participant will be recorded on their own track for easy editing.

     

  4. Click one of the following:

    • Start:

      This button displays if you selected Start Now. Click to display the Join Call page. See Use the Join Call Page in Connect. 

    • Schedule: This button displays if you did not select Start Now. Click to schedule the call and display it in your call list. See View Your Connect Call List.

After you click Start or Schedule, invitations are sent to you and your guests to connect to the call. The invitation informs you that you will need:

  • Mac or Windows laptop or desktop computer
  • Chrome, Firefox, Safari, Microsoft Edge, or Brave browser
  • Microphone
  • Headphones (recommended) or speakers

Before you or your guests can join the call, you must first visit the Join Call page. See Use the Join Call Page in Connect.

Use the Join Call Page in Connect

The first step in using Connect is for the host to schedule a call, either immediately or for a future time. See Set Up a Recording in Connect. Whether you are the host or an invited guest, you are then directed to the Join Call page.

To navigate to the Join Call page in Connect:

  1. Do one of the following:

    • Click on the Join Event link in an email invitation, or
    • If you are the host, you can click on the scheduled call on your call dashboard. and then click Start Now on the Call Detail page. 

  2. If you scheduled the call for the future, you will enter the lobby. Click Start Now.

    The Join Call page displays.

  3. If a popup displays to ask you to permit Connect to use your microphone, click Allow.

  4. If you are on a mobile device, a popup will display Connect mobile guidelines. Click I understand. 

  5. Under the  menu in the upper right, you can select the following options:

    • Language  - Click to change the language in which the text on your own screen displays. (This will not change the display language for anyone else on the call.)
    • Sign Out - Click to sign out of Connect.

  6. Click the icon to display the following recording options:

    • Microphone dropdown field - Select the microphone you want to use for the call.

    • Automatic Gain Control - (Available in Chrome and Firefox browsers.) Click to toggle this feature off if you want to control your audio gain manually instead of having Connect control it automatically.

      Warning

      You cannot change the microphone or gain control while recording.

  7. Speak into your microphone to test your volume. The meter displays your audio levels.

    Note

    Our recommendations for recording:

    1. Stay between a finger and fist’s distance from the mic.
    2. Keep the mic slightly off-center, rather than directly in front of you.
    3. If you are using your laptop’s in-built microphone, lean close to your laptop and avoid typing while recording, so you can eliminate noise caused by keystrokes.
    4. Use headphones while recording.
    5. Turn off notifications from other applications to reduce distractions.

  8. Click Join Call when you are satisfied with your settings. See Join and Record a Connect Call as a Host or Join a Connect Call as a Guest.

View the Details of a Connect Call

To view the detail page for a call, go to your Connect Call List and click on the row for a call. See View Your Connect Call List.

About the Media Files

The left side of the page contains the media files, which include the following elements:

  • Download All Files:

    Click to download all the individual tracks into a zipped file. Once clicked, a progress bar will appear. Cancel the download at any point by clicking the Cancel button.

    Downloaded files are in WAV format. 

  • Recording # : The first recording you make during a call will be in a folder called Recording 1. If you make additional recordings during the same call, they will be in folders called Recording 2, Recording 3, etc. Expand a folder to view its contents.

  • Master Track: By default, the first track is a combined master track recording of all attendees in the call.

  • Individual Participant Tracks:

    If the host selected individual tracks when setting up the recording, you’ll find separate tracks for each participant in the call, for easier editing. See Set Up a Recording in Connect.

  • Waveforms:

    The height of the waveforms in each track indicates the volume of the recording at the time indicated by the numbers at the bottom of the track. You can use them to help you visually identify when someone was speaking, which helps you determine where to trim or make adjustments when editing.

About the Session Info

The right side of the page contains the session info, which includes the following:

  • Push to Libsyn

    Click Push to Libsyn to enter your episode details and proceed to publishing your episode in Libsyn. If you have multiple recordings, select the one you want to add to Libsyn and press Go.  See About Your Episode Details.

    This button is only visible to the call’s host.

  • Join Now: Click to navigate to the Join Call page. See Use the Join Call Page in Connect. (Disabled for calls that have ended.)

  • Edit: Click to edit the call details. (Disabled for calls that have ended.)

  • Delete: Click to delete the call. (Disabled if you were not the host)  You will be asked to confirm. Deleted calls are moved to the trash can, which you can access by clicking Deleted Calls under the icon in the upper right.

  • Host: Displays the name of the call host.

  • Guests: Displays the email addresses for all invited guests.

  • Preference Settings: Indicates whether the following options were selected when the call was set up:

    • Create Individual Tracks
    • Share Call Notes

  • Copy Link to Session: Click to copy a direct link to the session to your clipboard.

  • Call Start: Displays the time the call started (and whether it was different from the scheduled time). See Set Up a Recording in Connect.

  • Duration: Lists the total length of the call.

  • Recordings: Lists the total length of the master recording made during the call.

  • Attendance: Displays the names of the people who joined the call.

  • Call Notes: Click to display a popup with the call notes. Click Download to download the call notes to your computer.

  • View Chat History: Click to display a popup with the chat history.

 

Use a Connect Recording in Libsyn

Connect Calls are automatically saved in Libsyn so you can publish them as podcast episodes without editing them. The combined master track for each recording you make during a call is saved as a separate MP3 file in your Media Library. See Use Your Media Library. 

 

Get Help with Connect

Click the ConnectSupport link at the bottom of a Call Details page if you have questions or want to leave feedback specifically about Connect.

View Your Connect Call List

To view your Connect Call List, click New in the upper-right of your Libsyn account and select Connect Call.

Alternatively, you can log in directly to Connect at connect.libsyn.com.

If you don’t have any calls yet, you can click Start a New Call to set one up. See Set Up a Recording in Connect.

 

If you have previously recorded or scheduled calls, you will see them listed. 

Under the icon in the upper right, you can select from the following:

  • Account Settings: Click to change your Libsyn account settings. See Change Your Account Settings.
  • Language
    Click to change the language in which the text on your own Connect screen displays. (This will not change the display language for anyone else on the call.)
  • Billing: Click to manage your Libsyn billing. See Manage Your Payment Profiles.
  • Deleted Calls: Click to view your Deleted Calls page for Connect. Calls you have deleted from your dashboard display here until you click Empty Trash.
  • Sign Out: Click to sign out of Connect.

You also have the following options:

  • Total Calls: Displays the total number of calls in which you have participated.

  • Call Title: The first column displays the title entered for the call.

  • Call Date: The second column displays the date and time scheduled for the call.

  • Guests: The third column lists the names of all the guests invited to the call.

  • Status:

    The fourth column displays the status of the call, which include the following:

    • Scheduled - The call has been scheduled. 
    • Missed - The call was not started at its scheduled time.
    • Live Now - The call is currently in progress.
    • Ended - The call has ended but is not processed.
    • Ready - The call recording has been processed. 

Click on the row for a call to view its details. See View the Details of a Connect Recording.

Note

Connect Calls are also automatically saved in Libsyn so you can publish them as podcast episodes without editing them. The combined master track for each recording you make during a call is saved as a separate MP3 file in your Media Library. See Use Your Media Library.

Get Help with Connect

Click the ConnectSupport link at the bottom of a Call List page if you have questions or want to leave feedback specifically about Connect.

Libsyn 5: Set Up a New Podcast

About the Libsyn Podcast Directory

The Libsyn Podcast Directory is an internal podcast directory that only displays shows actively being hosted by Libsyn. When you navigate to your show on the Libsyn Directory, you will see your most recent episode’s player, your show description, a list of published episodes, and your theme color.

Note

Your directory listing’s theme color will automatically be configured based on your show artwork.

When you open a new show, the Include in Directory checkbox in your podcast settings is automatically selected. If you would prefer to remove your show from the Libsyn Directory, you can deselect that box at any time. See About Your Podcast Settings.

Libsyn recommends that you keep your show available on the Directory because it can be a valuable tool for attracting new listeners. Since the Libsyn Directory has been around for so long, your Directory listing appears near the top of the page when a person searches the internet using terms that are relevant to your podcast.

 

Directory Theme Color

If you don’t like the color that is automatically selected for your directory show page, you can manually set the color in your Podcast Settings to a color that better fits your brand.

About Your Podcast Settings

When setting up your show, you must enter key information in the Podcast Settings form, like your show’s title, description, categories, and more. The sections below describe all of the available fields, and what kind of information you should enter to make sure your show will appear in all of your publishing destinations the way you expect it to.

Note

The information you enter in the fields below will form part of your RSS feed and will also be used as the starting point for publications to podcast directories, websites, or social media sites. However, when you set up your publication destinations, you can customize some information to be different. For example, if you want the description for your show to be different on Spotify than in other directories, you can update that field when you set up your connection to Spotify.

 

Title

Enter the title of your show. The show title appears everywhere that you publish, including your feed, in podcast directories, on your Libsyn blog page, on your media player, and more.

If you ever decide to change your show title, just update this field and the change will be reflected everywhere.

 

Description

Your show description is meant to explain what your show is about and get your audience excited to subscribe to it. The description should only be a few sentences long.

The description appears in podcast directories, as well as on your Libsyn blog page, the Libsyn Podcast Directory, and other places you publish your show. Note that it isn’t searchable in Apple Podcasts, and it should not include a list of keywords.

Category One and Two

Most podcast directories organize podcasts by category so that listeners can find podcasts by the topics they are interested in. You are required to enter at least one category. However, if your show falls into more than one, you can enter two.

Category One is the most important because it will be the main way your show is categorized. However, if you enter a second category, your show will appear under that one as well.

Owner Name

Enter the name of the person or entity who owns the show. The person or entity whose name you enter should be authorized to make or request changes on the show’s behalf.

Owner Email

Enter the email of the person who you entered in the Owner Name field. The person who receives emails at the address you enter should be able to answer questions about the show and be authorized to make decisions about it.

Usually, this email address is used by podcast directories in case they ever need to contact you about your show. For that reason, it’s important that you enter an email address that the owner checks somewhat regularly. However, this email will be publicly available in your RSS feed, so don’t enter an email that you prefer to keep private.

Author

Enter the name or names of the person or people who run the podcast and appear in each episode. Or, you can also enter the company that produces (and therefore “owns”) the podcast.

Authors are searchable in many podcast directories, including Apple Podcasts. Although it is a good idea to enter names that listeners will search for to increase your show’s visibility, you need to enter valid information in this field. If you enter names that are not actually associated with your podcast, or information that resembles spam, Apple Podcasts will pull your show from their store.

If you leave the field blank, some directories, including Apple Podcasts, will list the author as “unknown.”

This field is also sometimes referred to as “provider” or “artist” in certain directories.

Website URL

Your website URL is a place you manage where you can make all of your show’s episodes available to your audience, as well as other information about yourself and your show. The URL gets sent to your RSS feed, which some directories — like Apple Podcasts — pick up and make available to your listeners.

Libsyn automatically enters your Libsyn blog page into this field when your show is created. However, you can enter a different URL (or update it later) if you want to use a different site, like your own website or one that’s hosted elsewhere.

 

Release Frequency

This lets your listeners know how often you plan to update your show with new episodes. By default, this field will show No Set Schedule, but you can select a different option to indicate whether you expect to release an episode daily, weekly, etc. 

You can also select Serial Show (Complete) to indicate that all episodes of a serial show have been published and no additional episodes are expected. (If you select this option, your show page on Apple Podcasts will display “Complete Series” next to the category and the first season by default.)

 

Third Party Content

If your show includes content created by someone else, you must have all the necessary rights to use that content in your podcast under the laws of each country or region in which your show is available.  

Choose either No Third Party Content or Has Rights to Third Party Content.

No Third Party Content: Your show does not have any content that was created by someone else.

Has Rights to Third Party Content: Your show has content that was created by someone else and you have the rights to use it.

 

Type

Choose either Episodic or Serial.

Episodic: this term refers to a podcast that releases episodes over time, but the order in which your audience listens to those episodes doesn’t matter. Most podcasts are episodic.

Serial: this term refers to a podcast that releases podcasts in a certain order, and audiences must listen to them in that order. Usually, a serial podcast follows a storyline, like true-crime podcasts, which you must listen to in a certain order or you won’t be able to follow the story.

Explicit

Use the Explicit field to specify whether your show is clean or explicit.

Your show’s rating is important for establishing parental controls. Also, keep in mind that some countries do not permit explicit content, so marking your show explicit might result in your show becoming unavailable to listeners in those countries.

Note

Not sure whether your show is clean or explicit? Explicit can mean a show uses language that is inappropriate for young listeners (such as some famous “four-letter words”), but it can also mean that the subject matter is inappropriate even if the language is “clean.” If you’re not sure whether your subject matter qualifies as explicit, conduct the following thought experiment: “If I were a parent of young children, would I be comfortable with them listening to this episode?”

 

Language

Select the language that is used in most of the episodes of your show.

 

Episode Display Limit

This field displays the number of episodes available on podcast directories for your show. By default, the limit is 300, but you can change it. For example, if you enter 100, but you have 150 episodes, podcast apps will only list the 100 most recent episodes for your show. If you want to list all of your episodes with no limit, enter 0. 

 

Copyright

Enter a copyright notice and the current year with the copyright symbol (such as © 2022) to indicate that your content is under copyright protection. If you include the year, remember to update it when necessary.

 

Apple Ownership Token

If you already have a show on Apple Podcasts but it’s not in your Apple Podcasts Connect account, you can claim your existing show by generating a token in your Apple Podcasts Connect account and then entering the token in this field.

 

Directory Theme Color

If you include your show in the Libsyn Podcast Directory, your theme color will automatically be configured based on your show artwork.

If you don’t like the color that is automatically selected for your directory show page, you can manually set the color in your Podcast Settings to a color that better fits your brand.

 

Feed Unlocked/Feed Locked

Click the Feed Unlocked toggle to lock your RSS feed. When you click the Feed Unlocked toggle, it changes to Feed Locked.

When you lock your feed, a “feed locked” tag appears on your RSS feed. When other hosts see that tag, they will block attempts to import your feed from its current location. This feature is useful to prevent someone from taking your RSS feed (which is publicly available) and importing it to another host to try to make money on your show without you knowing about it.

When you lock your feed, other hosts won’t allow anyone to import it, including you. If you lock your feed and ever want to import it to another host, remember to unlock it first by clicking the toggle again.

Important

Locking your feed prevents it from being imported to many podcast hosts, but there are some hosts that don’t use the feed lock feature. This feature gives you some protection, but it won’t work with hosts that don’t read the “feed locked” tag.

Include in Directory

When this checkbox selected, your show is added to the Libsyn Directory for listeners to find. Deselect this checkbox if you do not want your show to be added to the Libsyn Directory. See About the Libsyn Podcast Directory.

 

External Media Links

In the External Media Links section, you can enter the direct URLs to listen to your show on different platforms. The links will be available to display on your Libsyn podcast website and in your Libsyn media player.

You can enter an external media link in the following fields:

  • Apple Podcasts URL
  • Spotify URL
  • YouTube URL
  • X Handle
  • Facebook URL
  • Instagram URL
  • LinkedIn URL
  • iHeartRadio URL
  • Amazon Music URL
  • Audible URL
  • Deezer URL
  • Gaana URL
  • Player FM URL
  • JioSaavn URL
  • Boomplay URL

Add Another Podcast to Your Libsyn Account

You can have more than one podcast under the same login.

  1. Click on the show selector dropdown arrow in the upper-left and then click Add New Show.

  2. Complete the fields on the Add New Show page. See Create a New Podcast.

Create a New Podcast

Before you can host and distribute a podcast with Libsyn, you must sign up for a hosting account.

To create a new podcast:

  1. Log in to Libsyn 5.  

  2. Navigate to the Add New Show page.

    • If you do not have a Libsyn podcast yet, you’ll be automatically redirected to the Add New Show page.
    • If you already have at least one Libsyn podcast and want to add another for the same login, see Add Another Podcast to Your Libsyn Account.  

  3. On the Add New Show page, select one of the following for your initial hosting plan:

    • Basic Plan:

      $12 billed monthly. You’ll be able to:

      • Upload 3 hours of new audio content per month.
      • Upload approximately 50 hours of new video content per month.
      • Access basic statistics.
      • Publish video episodes to YouTube.

    • Advanced Plan:

      $25 billed monthly. You’ll be able to:

      • Upload 10 hours of new audio content per month.
      • Upload approximately 50 hours of new video content per month.
      •  Access advanced statistics, social promotion tools, and Libsyn Connect.
      • Publish and monetize video episodes on Spotify.

    • Max Plan:

      $150 billed monthly. You’ll be able to:

      • Upload 55 hours of new audio content per month.
      • Upload approximately 50 hours of new video content per month.
      • Access all of the features available in the Advanced Plan.

       This plan is optimized for high-volume podcast publishing.

  4. On the Checkout page, complete the following fields:

    • First Name: You must enter a first name.

    • Last Name: You must enter a last name.

    • Company: Optionally, you can enter a company name.

    • Billing Location Lookup: Start typing an address, and select the correct address from the list that displays. This will autocomplete the address fields below.

    • Billing Address: You must enter a billing street address.

    • Suite/Apt: Enter a suite or apartment number if applicable.

    • City: You must enter a billing city.

    • State: You must enter a billing state.

    • Postal Code: You must enter a billing postal code. If you do not have a billing postal code, enter 00000 (five zeros).

    • Country:

      You must select a billing country.

    • VAT/GST Number:

      (Field displays when you select an applicable country.) You can enter a Value-Added Tax or Goods and Services Tax number, if you have one.

    • Payment Method Nickname: Give a nickname to the payment method you entered.

    • Payment Method:

      Select one of the following:

      • New Credit Card - Libsyn accepts Visa, MasterCard, American Express, Discover, JCB, UnionPay, and UCB. Enter your 16-digit credit card number, the expiration date in MM/YY format, and the CVV number found on the back of your card.
      • New ACH (US Only) - Enter your US bank account name, account type, routing number, and account number.
      • New PayPal - You will be prompted to validate your PayPal information after you click Pay Now.

    • Make This My ‘Primary’ Payment Method: Select this check box if you want this to be your default payment method.

    • Make This My ‘Backup’ Payment Method: Select this check box if you want this to be your backup payment method. If your primary payment method fails to process a charge, your backup payment method will then be used to attempt to process the charge.

    • Promo Code:

      You can enter a promo code if you have one.

      • If the code is valid, the details of the offer display below the field.
      • If the code is only valid for specific plans, an error displays informing you which plans can be used with this promo.

    • Cloudflare Verification Box:

      Libsyn uses Cloudflare Turnstile as a verification tool.

      • Select the Verify you are human check box if it displays.
      • After Cloudflare has finished verifying that you are a real web visitor and not a bot, the Cloudflare verification box displays: Success! 

     

  5. Click Pay Now. If you are a first time creator, your first month will be free. If you are not, you will be immediately billed the full cost of your subscription.

    Note

    When you open a new podcast, you will get billed monthly on the same day your show was initially activated. For example, if you create a podcast on April 19th, that show’s billing will renew on the 19th day of each month.

     

  6. Next, you’ll be prompted to either set up your podcast or import your existing podcast.

  • Note

    You can upgrade to even more storage on the Subscriptions page. See Manage Your Payment Profiles.

Optimize Show Discoverability

Optimizing your show’s distribution channels can help you reach as many listeners as possible. Even if you decide to start podcasting as a hobby, you give yourself the best chance at growing your audience and encouraging listener engagement by broadcasting your show to a wide array of locations.

If you want to take basic steps to increase the discoverability of your podcast, you could employ any of the following strategies:

  • Weaving keywords into episode titles.
  • Giving your podcast consistent branding.
  • Releasing episodes on a frequent/regular basis.

You can also use resources that we offer to ensure that your show is widely accessible.

Podcast Destinations

The best way to get potential listeners to notice your show is by sending it to podcast destinations. After you brand your podcast and upload an episode, you should immediately submit your show to Apple Podcasts and Spotify since those are considered to be the most popular podcast directories.

Note

When you send your show to Apple Podcasts, it will also appear on smaller podcast directories like Overcast, Podcast Addict, and Podchaser because those clients automatically pull shows from Apple Podcasts’ database.

You could also connect your podcast to the other destinations that we offer since listeners use more than just Apple Podcasts and Spotify to ingest podcasts. This will allow you to maximize the amount of people that your podcast reaches. See Connect Your Show to Publish Episodes to a Destination Feed.

Social Destinations

Another great way to promote your podcast is to share it with your friends and followers on your social media pages. By posting your episodes on social media, you could make your show available to individuals who may not be familiar with your podcast, or podcasts in general.

You can use Libsyn to connect your Facebook, YouTube, and LinkedIn accounts directly to your show. Depending on your show’s plan level, you would be able to customize your episode releases to Facebook and LinkedIn using the social destinations calendar. See Schedule an Episode to Appear on Social Media Using the Social Destinations Calendar.

Your Libsyn Podcast Website

When you host a podcast with Libsyn, you get access to a customizable Libsyn Podcast Website where all of your episodes will appear. You can send listeners to this site to listen to your show and get more information about you. Your website can also help you promote your show since it will appear for people who search the internet for related topics. See Set Up Your Libsyn Podcast Website.

The Libsyn Podcast Directory

The Libsyn Podcast Directory is an internal podcast directory that only displays shows actively being hosted by Libsyn. Listeners can use the Libsyn Podcast Directory to listen to your podcast, but we recommend making your show available on the Directory because it can be a valuable tool for promoting your show and attracting new listeners.

Since the Libsyn Directory has been around for so long, your Directory listing appears near the top of the page when a person searches the internet using terms that are relevant to your podcast. This allows for better discoverability of your show.

Set Up Your Podcast

When you first create a show, you will be asked to enter your Podcast Title and Description, or Import an Existing RSS Feed. Once that is complete, you will be taken to your show Dashboard where you can begin the Onboarding process.

Libsyn uses the information you enter in your podcast settings to create your RSS feed and to provide information to all the publication destinations to which you want to connect. The information you enter determines how your show will appear in podcast directories, as well as other places, such as your Podcast Page, the Libsyn Podcast Directory, and other websites and social media accounts.

To finish setting up your show:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Podcast. The Podcast Settings form opens.

  3. Enter your show’s information in the fields. For more details about these fields, see About Your Podcast Settings.

  4. Use the Update Show Artwork and Update Widescreen Show Artwork sections on the right to upload your show’s artwork.

  5. Click Save.

Now that your show is set up, you can publish your episodes and connect to podcast directories, websites, and social media accounts.

To sign up for a Libsyn hosting account:

    • Email
      You must enter a valid email address. Use the basic format local-part@hostname.

    • Password: You must create a password between 8 and 75 characters that contains at least one upper case letter, one lower case letter, and one number.

    • I agree to Libsyn’s Terms of Service and Privacy Policy: You must select this check box to agree to Libsyn’s terms and policies in order to create an account. You can read the terms and policies here:

      • Libsyn’s Terms of Service
      • Privacy Policy

    • Cloudflare Verification Box:

      Libsyn uses Cloudflare Turnstile as a verification tool.

      • Select the Verify you are human check box if it displays.
      • After Cloudflare has finished verifying that you are a real web visitor and not a bot, the Cloudflare verification box displays:  Success! 

     

  2. Click **Sign Up.

    **

  3. Click on the verification link in the email we sent. 

  4. On the Success page, click Login to Libsyn.

  5. You’ll be redirected to the Add New Show page to create your podcast. See Create a New Podcast.

Upload Your Show’s Artwork

When you set up your show, you must upload your show’s artwork.

Your show’s artwork is the image your listeners see when they find your show in podcast directories, apps, social media, and more. The image below shows how the artwork for She Podcasts appears in Spotify.

To represent your podcast show, you must upload new show artwork. You can also upload new widescreen show artwork. Show artwork must be square and will appear in most places you publish, such as Apple Podcasts, the Libsyn HTML5 player, and more. The widescreen artwork, on the other hand, appears in videos you create for places like YouTube and Facebook.

There are some requirements for the square and the widescreen artwork:

  • Show Artwork Requirements: Widescreen Show Artwork Requirements
    • 1400 by 1400 px (minimum)
    • 3000 by 3000 px (maximum)
    • Must be square
    • Under 500 kb
    • Use sRGB color space
    • JPG or PNG files only
    :
    • Must measure 1920 by 1080 px
    • At least 72 dpi

 

Note

If you’re looking for some tips on how to create great show artwork, you can check out our Libsyn blog posts about artwork FAQs.

When your artwork is ready, there are two quick ways to upload it.

  • 1.: Click the Add Artwork or Add Widescreen Artwork area in the Podcast Settings section. In the window that opens, navigate to the file you want to upload and then double-click it.
  • 2.: Find the file you want on your computer, and then click and drag it to the Add Artwork or Add Widescreen Artwork area in the Podcast Settings section. When you let go of the mouse button, the upload begins.

After the artwork uploads, it appears as a preview in the appropriate section.

View Your Dashboard

Your Show Dashboard is the landing page for your podcast. When you log in to Libsyn or switch to another show on your account, you can add a new episode, see a snapshot of your show downloads, access your most recent episodes, copy your RSS feed, embed a single episode, and manage your show’s storage.

Your Show Dashboard displays:

Your Show Dashboard includes the following:

  • Show/Network Selector:

    You can use the Show/Network selector in the upper left of your Libsyn 5 account to switch between shows. You can also add shows to your account and switch between networks if you have access to more than one LibsynPro network. See Switch Between Networks.

    • Dashboard: Click to view your show’s main dashboard.

    • Episodes: Click to view your show’s episodes. See View Your Episodes List.
    • Social: Click to view your show’s social destinations calendar. See Schedule an Episode to Appear on Social Media Using the Social Destinations Calendar.
    • Advertising: Click to view your show’s Automatic Ads and PodRoll earnings reports. See View Revenue for Your Show.

  • New: Click New in the upper right of your Libsyn account to publish an episode or set up a recording in connect.

    • Account Settings: Click to view your account settings page.
    • Billing: Click to view your billing page. See Manage Your Payment Profiles.
    • Support: Click for Support information.
    • Logout: Click to log out of Libsyn.

  • Stats Snapshot: Displays the following:

    • All Time Downloads: Represents the total number of episode downloads since your show began.
    • Downloads This Month: Represents the total number of episode downloads since the current month began.
    • Downloads This Week: Represents the total number of episode downloads since the current week began.

    For more information, view See a Snapshot of Your Show’s Statistics.

  • Announcements: Displays announcements containing promotional material for Libsyn customers.

  • Quick Links: The Quick Links panel contains the following:

    • Apple Podcasts / Libsyn Classic Feed: Click the Copy icon to copy your RSS Feed URL to your clipboard. See Find the URL to Your RSS Feed.
    • Site: Click the Copy icon to copy your podcast website URL to your clipboard.

  • Embed Single Episode: You can choose an episode using the selector and click Get Code to open the Link/Embed episode player panel. See Customize and Share the Libsyn Media Player for a Specific Episode.

  • Monthly Storage: Displays the following:

    • The percentage of storage used in the current month.
    • A progress bar that details how many megabytes have been used and how many megabytes are in your current plan.
    • A counter that tells you how many days you have left until your storage resets.
    • A Manage button that you can click to get more details about your monthly storage and uploaded files.

    See Understand How Your Monthly Storage Works.

Libsyn 5: Troubleshooting

Get Help When You Need It

At Libsyn, we offer several ways for you find information or to get a hold of our Support Team if you ever need help with your podcast. Review the different options in the sections below and choose whichever is most convenient for you.

Search Our Knowledge Base

This article that you’re reading forms part of Libsyn’s Knowledge Base, which contains useful information about managing your account, your podcast, and your content. If you want to find information about a topic or about how to get something done, use the Search field in the main Knowledge Base page or in the top right corner of any article (including this one).

Send Us a Help Request from Your Account

If you run into an issue, or if you want to send us feedback to request a new feature, you can conveniently send us a help request from your account. To do so:

  1. Click the  icon in the lower-right of your account. The Resource Center panel opens.

  2. Click Contact Us.                          

  3. Click Create New Ticket. The Submit a request form opens.

  4. Fill out all necessary fields in the form.

  5. Click Submit.

Our Support Team will review your help request and someone will reach out to you.

Send Us an Email

If you have any questions or concerns, you can reach out to our Support Team at the following email: . When you reach out to us, make sure to give us information about your podcast, such as your show title, so we can find your podcast and account easily.

Watch Our Libsyn Webinars

Libsyn offers monthly webinars that cover everything from podcasting topics, to recommendations, and best industry practices. We also provide information about stats, WordPress, and upcoming features.

Our webinars provide even experienced podcasters with important updates about what’s happening in Libsyn as well as the podcasting industry at large.

To stay connected with our latest webinars, follow our Libsyn Crowdcast channel.

Stay up to Date on Libsyn Updates and the Podcasting Industry

Libsyn offers several resources to keep you up to date about the podcasting industry and about upcoming Libsyn features. For more information, see Keep Informed about the World of Podcasting.

Reset Your Password

There are two ways to reset your password. If you are able to sign in your account, you can change your password in your Account Settings page. Or, if you forgot your password and can’t sign in, you can request that Libsyn send you a password reset link to your email.

Reset Your Password in Your Account

You can update your password from within your Libsyn account. To do so:

  1. Sign in to your account using your current password.

  2. Click the  icon and select Account Settings. The Account Settings form opens.

  3. Click Password to expand the Password section.

  4. Enter your new password in the New Password field, and then enter it again in the Retype Password field to confirm it. Make sure the password meets the following guidelines: It must be at least eight characters long and contain at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, and one number.

    Tip

    To see the characters you entered for your password, click the  icon to the right of the field.

  5. Click Save.

Request a Reset Link if You Forgot Your Password

If you forgot your password and can’t sign in to your account, you can request a reset link from Libsyn. To do so:

  1. Click Forgot Password on the main sign-in page.

  2. Enter the email you usually use to sign in to your account in the email field.

    Note

    The email you enter must be the same email you use to sign in to Libsyn because Libsyn checks to make sure it recognizes your email before it sends a reset link. Checking to make sure your email is associated with an account is a security requirement.

     

  3. Click Send Reset Link.

  4. When you receive the email from Libsyn, click the link it contains to reset your password.

  5. In the window that opens, enter a new password and retype it in the second field to confirm it.

  6. Click Save.

    Your password is reset and you can use your new password to sign in to your Libsyn account.

Libsyn 5: Uploading and Publishing Your Episodes Using FTP

Set up Cyberduck to Connect to Libsyn

Cyberduck is an open source application that Libsyn recommends for using FTP. This article walks you through how to set up Cyberduck to connect to Libsyn so you can use it to upload and publish your episodes.

Note

Before you can set up Cyberduck, you need to create an FTP password that is different from the password you use to sign in to Libsyn. For more information about setting up your FTP password, see Create or Change an FTP Password.

Set up Cyberduck

  1. Download and install Cyberduck and then open the program.
  2. Click Bookmark and then select New Bookmark in the menu that appears.
  3. In the field at the top of the window that opens, select FTP-SSL.
  4. Enter the following information in the rest of the fields:
    • Nickname: Enter Libsyn FTP, or whatever you want to call your connection.
    • Server: Enter ftp-lax.libsyn.com
    • Port: Cyberduck might automatically fill this information in for you. If it doesn’t, enter either 21 or 990.
    • Username: Enter the email address you use to sign in to Libsyn.
    • Password: Enter the FTP password that you created. Note that this password is not the same password you use to log in to Libsyn.
  5. Click the red X.

You are now ready to connect to your Libsyn account use FTP to upload and publish your episodes.

Connect Cyberduck to Your Libsyn Account

To connect to your Libsyn account whenever you open Cyberduck, double-click the Libsyn FTP bookmark (or whatever you named your connection) in the main Cyberduck window, as shown in the image below.

When you’re connected, Cyberduck provides access to the folders and subfolders you can use to upload and publish episodes, or to upload other materials. For more information about how to use those folders, see Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episodes.

Set up FileZilla to Connect to Libsyn

FileZilla is a free and open source application that Libsyn recommends for using FTP. This article provides detailed steps about how to set up FileZilla to connect to your Libsyn account so you can upload and publish episodes using FTP.

Note

Before you can set up FileZilla, you need to create an FTP password that is different from the password you use to sign in to Libsyn. For more information about setting up your FTP password, see Create or Change an FTP Password.

Set up FileZilla

  1. Download and install FileZilla and then open the program.

  2. Click File and then select Site Manager from the menu that appears. The Site Manager window opens.

  3. Click New site.

  4. In the Select entry pane, change the name of the new site to Libsyn FTP, or to whatever name you want to call your connection.

  5. In the General tab on the right, enter the following information:

    • Protocol: Select FTP - File Transfer Protocol. Do not select SFTP - SSH File Transfer Protocol, as Libsyn does not support SFTP connections.
    • Host: Enter ftp-lax.libsyn.com
    • Port: FileZilla will likely enter this information for you automatically. If it doesn’t, enter one of the following: 21 (if you’re using non-SSL) or 990 (if you’re using SSL).
    • Encryption: Select Use explicit FTP over TLS if available.
    • Logon Type: Select Normal.
    • User: Enter the email address you use to log in to Libsyn.
    • Password: Enter the FTP password that you created. Note that this password is not the same password you use to log in to Libsyn.

  6. Click OK.

  7. If the Remember passwords? window opens, we recommend you select Save passwords and click OK.

You’re now ready to connect to your Libsyn account and start using FTP for uploading and publishing episodes. 

Connect FileZilla to Your Libsyn Account

You can now connect to your Libsyn account whenever you open FileZilla. To do so:

  1. Click the icon in the upper-left of FileZilla to open the Site Manager window.
  2. In the Select entry pane, click Libsyn FTP (or whatever you named your connection).
  3. Click Connect.

Now that you’re connected, you can use the folders in FileZilla to upload and publish episodes, or to upload other materials. For more information about how to use those folders, see Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episodes.

Set up WinSCP to Connect to Libsyn

WinSCP is a free, open source application for using FTP. This article provides details about how to set WinSCP up to connect to your Libsyn account so you can use FTP to upload and publish your episodes.

Note

Before you can set up WinSCP, you need to create an FTP password that is different from the password you use to sign in to Libsyn. For more information about setting up your FTP password, see Create or Change an FTP Password.

Set up WinSCP

  1. Download and install WinSCP and then open the program. The Login window opens.
  2. In the Login window, enter the following information:
    • File Protocol: Select FTP. After you do so, the Encryption field appears.
    • Encryption: Select TLS/SSL Explicit encryption.
    • Host name: Enter ftp-lax.libsyn.com
    • Port number: WinSCP might provide this information for you. If it doesn’t, enter either 21 (for non-SSL) or 990 (SSL).
    • User name: Enter the email address you use to log in to Libsyn.
    • Password: Enter the FTP password you created. Note that this password is not the same password you use to log in to Libsyn.
  3. Click Save. The Save session as site window opens.
  4. In the Site name field of the Save session as site window, enter Libsyn FTP, or whatever name you want to call your connection.
  5. Optionally, we recommend that you select the Save password check box. Doing so is useful because it saves you from having to log in with your FTP password every time you want to connect to your Libsyn account.
  6. Click OK.

You can close the Login window, and you are now ready to connect to your Libsyn account to upload and publish episodes using FTP.

Connect WinSCP to Your Libsyn Account

Whenever you open WinSCP, you can connect to your Libsyn account by going to Session > Sites, and then selecting Libsyn FTP (or whatever you named your connection), as show in the image below.

When you connect, your show folders appear in the pane on the right of the WinSCP window. For more information about how to use the subfolders in your show folders to upload and publish episodes, or to upload other materials, see Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episodes.

Use FTP to Upload or Publish Your Episodes

FTP is an acronym for “file transfer protocol,” which is an easy and convenient way to share files from your device to others. Using FTP gives you several helpful options for getting your episodes and other materials into Libsyn. For example, you can use FTP to upload more than one file to Libsyn at the same time, or to upload accompanying image or PDF files that you can link from your show notes.

If you don’t want to sign in to your Libsyn account at all, you also have the option of publishing directly from FTP.

Note

Are you ready to publish your episode? Make sure you have configured your show and set up your publications correctly, including connecting your show to the podcast directories where you want your show to appear. For an overview of everything you should prepare, see Are You Ready to Publish an Episode?

This article walks you through how to get started using FTP, and all of the options you have for uploading and publishing when you’re up and running.

Set up FTP Software to Connect to Libsyn

To get started with FTP, you first need to create an FTP password in Libsyn. See Create or Change an FTP Password.

Next, download the FTP software you want to use. There are many software programs available, but we have several recommendations for Windows and macOS. 

  • Windows: MacOS
    • FileZilla
    • WinSCP
    • Cyberduck
    :
    • Transmit
    • Cyberduck

After you install your FTP software, you need to enter the correct information to make sure it connects to your Libsyn account. Here is an overview of the information you need to enter:

  • Hostname (also sometimes referred to as Server): ftp-lax.libsyn.com
  • Username: Enter the email address you use to access your Libsyn account.
  • Password: Enter the FTP password you created in Libsyn. Note that this password is not the same as your Libsyn account password; see Create or Change an FTP Password for more information.
  • Port Numbers:
    • FTP: 21
    • FTPS: 990
  • FTP Protocol: If your FTP software requires that you enter an FTP protocol, select either FTP (port 21) or FTPS (port 990). SFTP (SSH File Transfer Protocol) is not supported and will not connect to Libsyn. Note that not all FTP software asks you to enter an FTP protocol.

After you enter all required information, you can connect your FTP software to your Libsyn account, and then you are ready to upload and publish your episodes using FTP.

Note

For more specific information about setting up a particular FTP application and connecting it to Libsyn, you can refer to our articles about how to set up FileZilla, Cyberduck, or WinSCP.

Upload Files and Publish Episodes with FTP

After you enter all required information and connect your FTP software to Libsyn, the FTP application provides access to a list of folders that you can use to upload and publish episodes.

The main folder you see is your podcast folder, which is named after your show ID. If you have more than one podcast, you’ll see a folder for each one. For information on finding which show ID relates to each show in our system, see How to Find Your Show ID.

When you open a podcast folder, you will see the following subfolders:

  • assets:

    Use this folder to upload images or PDF files to which you want to link. For example, if you have an important PDF file that you want to distribute with your episode, such as a transcription of the episode, you can upload it here and then include a link to it in your episode description (also known as show notes).

    When you upload an image file or a PDF file to this folder, you can link to it using the following URL structure (note that you must replace the sections in red with the appropriate information):

    0 show ID/file name

    To find your show ID, see How to Find Your Show ID.

  • dropbox:

    Use this folder to upload any media files that contain episodes that you want to publish later. This folder gives you the option of uploading more than one file at the same time.

    After you upload episodes, they are available in Libsyn. From your Libsyn account, you can enter all of the necessary information for the episode to prepare it for publication, including show notes, artwork, and scheduling information that specifies when you want the episode to become available in your publication destinations.

    For more information about how to publish an episode that you’ve uploaded using the dropbox folder, see Publish an Episode.

     

    Note: Although the name is the same, the dropbox folder has no connection to the Dropbox cloud storage service.

  • public:

    Use the public folder to upload media that you want to share for “download only.” “Files for download only” are files that you can make publicly accessible, but do not get published to any publication destinations — like podcast directories, social media, or web pages — through Libsyn.

    This option is useful if you’re using Libsyn to store your episodes, but you’re updating your RSS feed using different software. See Working with a File for Download Only.

  • quickcast:

    Use the quickcast folder to publish content to all of the publication destinations to which you’ve connected your show, including podcast directories, social media, and more. Episodes you place in this folder publish right away as soon as the upload finishes and your publication destinations process it.

    The information needed to publish your episode — such as the title, artwork, description, and more — come from the information stored in the media file you upload. This information is stored in containers called ID3 tags, which you need to update before you publish using the quickcast folder. For more information about ID3 tags and how to use them, see Manage the Episode Information in Your Media Files.

When you upload a file to Libsyn using any of the folders above, the file disappears from your FTP software. This behavior is normal. Libsyn pulls the file from the FTP server, processes it, and then stores it. You can find all of your uploaded files in your Libsyn account.

Libsyn 5: Using Advanced Tools for Your Show

Add a URL Prefix (or Prepend) to Your Show

A URL prefix (or prepend) is the addition of a URL in front of a URL. This enables you to use third-party analytics services.

For example, if your file URL hosted at Libsyn normally looks like this:

`https://traffic.libsyn.com/showname/somefile.mp3`

…and you want to have an external service track stats for your show. That company may give you a URL Prefix you can add to your normal file URLs. Let’s say your URL Prefix looks like this:

`https://thisisa.com/url/prefix`

If the URL Prefix gets added to your regular media file URL, your media file URL will suddenly look like this:

`https://thisisa.com/url/prefix/traffic.libsyn.com/showname/somefile.mp3`

The service must be whitelisted. Keep in mind that sending your listener to another service to track things means that you need to care a lot about that third party service protecting the privacy of you and your listeners. Libsyn’s prefix system whitelists services with prefixes that have already been vetted to be GDPR and CCPA compliant. If the service is not part of Libsyn’s whitelist, it cannot be used for a prefix. Currently, the whitelisted services providing prefixes are:

  • AdBarker
  • Artsai
  • BackBeat Media
  • Blubrry
  • Charisma Media
  • Chartable
  • Claritas
  • CoHost
  • Magellan
  • OP3
  • Podcorn
  • PodRoll
  • Podscribe
  • Podtrac
  • Spotify Ad Analytics/Podsights
  • Up.Audio
  • Veritonic

There is a limit of seven prefixes. Redirects can be very useful. They can be used for a number of important processes performed for URLs across the internet. However, the internet will only tolerate so many changes of direction before it complains about too many redirects. Libsyn’s system, therefore, will limit you to adding 7 URL prefixes to your show’s file URLs.

Don’t add multiple redirects pointing back to the same service. What we mean by that is, even if you have more than one assigned prefix URLs from a company, it doesn’t mean that company supports having a single show sent to their service multiple times. In other words, if you have two Chartable prefixes (for example), only add one in Libsyn. Adding both will actually break your file delivery to listeners as it isn’t supported by most third-party services.

So, I have a URL Prefix. How do I add it to my show?

To add a prefix to your show:

  1. Click the icon at the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Advanced Tools.

  3. In the row for URL Prefixes, click Edit.

  4. Add up to seven Prefix URLs.

  5. Click Save.

  • If you enter a URL that is not whitelisted, the system will give you an error stating that the URL couldn’t be saved as it doesn’t meet specifications. If you still want to add the prefix, please reach out to support@libsyn.com for consideration.
  • Once the URL prefixes are added, you will see that your file URLs will all be generating with the prefix attached automatically.

Add Feed and Website Redirects for Your Show

If you decide to move on from Libsyn, you will need to move your content to your new host and then point podcast directories from Libsyn to your new feed. This is done by configuring 301 redirects. (You must be the show owner to edit Feed & Website Redirects.)

To add redirects:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Advanced Tools.

  3. In the row for Feed & Website Redirects, click Edit to display the Feed & Website Redirects form.

  4. Complete the fields:

    • Feed Redirect URL
      Paste the URL for your new feed here to update directories to go to your new feed instead of your Libsyn feed.
    • Website Redirect URL: If you are using the Libsyn Podcast Website and want to redirect to a new website URL, you can enter the new URL here. See Set up Your Libsyn Podcast Website.
    • Use New Feed URL Tag: Not applicable. This field is only referenced when moving to Libsyn from another host. For more information, see Moving an Existing Podcast to Libsyn.

     

  5. Click Save to save your changes.

Enable Extra RSS Tags for Your Episodes

If you are an advanced user, you may want to add custom tags to your RSS feed. Some creators use custom tags to add more content and organization to their feed and make it easier for listeners to find.

Warning

Custom tags are inserted directly into your RSS feed, so if done incorrectly, it could cause issues with your feed and show listings.

Before adding extra RSS tags, we recommend you research which custom tags are supported by the listening platforms you want to target and add only those tags.  

This article explains how to enable the ability to add extra RSS tags at the episode level.

Note

You can also add extra RSS tags at the destination level.  See About the Advanced Options for a Destination Feed. 

To enable the ability to add extra RSS tags in your Libsyn episodes:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Advanced Tools.

  3. In the row for Extra RSS Tags, click Edit to display the Extra RSS Tags page.

  4. In the Allow Extra RSS Tags field, select Yes.

  5. Click Save to save your changes.

You will now see an Extra RSS Tags field in your Add Episode form when you create a new episode. See Publish an Episode.

  • You can type the XML for your custom tags in this field.
  • If your XML isn’t formatted correctly, the form will display an error after you click Save.
  • After you publish your episode, check to make sure that listening platforms are interpreting the tags as you expected.

Importing Your Feed to Libsyn

If you already have a podcast when you sign up for a Libsyn hosting account, you can enter your billing information and then import your existing podcast’s content to Libsyn using the “Let’s Get Started!” window that appears before the Onboarding guide.

You can also import another podcast to your account by adding a show to your account and migrating your podcast’s content to Libsyn using the Advanced Tools.

To import your content:

  1. Click theicon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click **Advanced Tools.

    **

  3. In the row for Feed Sync & Import, click Edit to display the Feed Sync & Import page.

  4. Complete the fields:

    • Action: Select One Time Import.

    • Feed URL:

      Paste the URL for your old RSS feed.

    • Ignore changes to metadata:

      Select if you want to pull in episode information without changing show settings.

    • Delete items not found in feed:

      Select if you want to delete items in your Libsyn feed that don’t match those in the feed you’re importing.

  5. Click Submit.

If your old feed has:

  • No lock tag - We’ll automatically begin to import your content.
  • Lock tag set to Yes - We’ll send an email to the address associated with your old feed, asking you to set the lock tag to No to allow your feed to be imported.
  • Lock tag set to No - We’ll send an email to the address associated with your old feed, asking you to verify that you are granting permission for your feed to be imported.

When your feed has been imported, we’ll send an email to the address associated with your old feed to let you know.

Manage Episode Categories

When you create an episode, you can create a category to help with organization. These categories can be used when customizing your Libsyn Podcast Player.

To manage your episode categories:

  1. Click the icon in the top of your Libsyn account to display the Settings page.

  2. Click Advanced Tools.

  3. In the row for Episode Categories, click Edit. Your Episode Category List displays:

     

    You have the following options:

    • Name: Displays the name of the category.

    • Episodes: Displays the number of episodes using this category.

    • Edit: Click to edit the name of the category.

    • View: Click to view a list of the episodes that use this category.

    • Delete:

      Click to delete the category.

      • You will be asked to confirm.
      • When the category is deleted, the episodes that were in this category will be moved to the general category.

Libsyn 5: Viewing Your LibsynPro Stats

Review the Network-Level Statistics That Come with LibsynPro

If you have admin access for a LibsynPro network, you can review download stats across the entire network, as well as per show.

To view your LibsynPro network-level stats:

  1. Open your pro network admin dashboard. See View Your Pro Network Admin Dashboard.

  2. Click the Stats tab to display the Stats page.

Overview of Your Network Stats

When you first open the Stats page, it provides an overview of your network’s statistics. You can see information such as your network’s all-time downloads, as well as downloads this week, month, and over the last 7 days, 30 days, and year. These numbers represent your network as a whole, including downloads across all your network’s shows.

Note

The difference between the metrics for the last seven days versus the last week, or the last 30 days versus the last month, is that one measures downloads over a span of days while the other measures the number of downloads in the current week or month you’re in. For example, if it’s Monday, the This Week stats will only show you the number of downloads over the last two days because the week starts on Sunday. On the other hand, Last 7 Days shows you numbers for the last seven days stretching into the previous week.

In any of the graphs, you can hover your mouse cursor over the line to see more specific numbers, as shown in the image below.

**

**

Show Totals

The Show Totals table lists all of the network’s shows and displays download statistics for the last three months of each show, as well as all-time totals.

In the Show Totals table, you can:

  • a.: Click Previous or Next, or click a page number to scroll through all of your shows.

  • b.: Search for a specific show. To do so, click on the icon and enter the first few letters of the title (or a word in the title) that you’re looking for in the Search bar. The system filters out shows as you type.

  • c.:

    Click to download stats for this table as a CSV or PDF file.

Network Statistics over Different Date Ranges

This section provides more information about your network downloads as a whole. With these features, you can review downloads over a specific date range, and you can see download activity over days, weeks, or months.

Set a Specific Date Range

The date range you set in the calendar affects which days you’ll be able to review in the graph. To change the date range, click the Statistics By Date Range button to open the calendar. For info on setting a date range in the calendar, see Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package.

Total Daily Downloads

The Total Daily Downloads graph shows downloads based on the date range you selected for your network as a whole. This includes download numbers for all shows in your network over the date range you selected.

You can use the graph to look closely at specific dates or smaller date ranges within the larger range you set in the calendar. For info on your options for using the graph, see Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package.

Downloads by Show

The Downloads by Show table lists all your shows that received downloads during the selected date range. The table lists the title of each show and its downloads during that date range. 

In the Downloads by Show table, you can:

  • a.: Click Previous or Next, or click a page number to scroll through all of your shows.

  • b.: Search for a specific show. To do so, click on the icon and enter the first few letters of the title (or a word in the title) that you’re looking for in the Search bar. The system filters out shows as you type.

  • c.:

    Click to download stats for this table as a CSV or PDF file.

 

Downloads per Country

In the Downloads per Country section, you can see the countries where the shows in your network are most popular. The right side of the section lists countries in order from most downloads to least. The download count includes all downloads for your network over the time range you selected.

For info on your options for reviewing your downloads in the Downloads per Country section, see Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package. 

Downloads per User Agent

A user agent refers to any program or app that listeners can use to access your content, such as Chrome, the Apple Podcasts app, Overcast, the Spotify app, and more. These are the programs listeners use to access content. You can review these stats to get accurate download counts and to determine where your listeners are engaging with your episodes most frequently across your network.

The Downloads per User Agent section gives you all of your network’s stats associated with user agents. The download count includes all downloads for your network over the time range you selected.

For info on your options for reviewing your downloads in the Downloads per User Agent section, see Review the Statistics That Come in the Advanced Package.

Review the Show-Level Statistics That Come with LibsynPro

The features in the LibsynPro stats package are available to customers who have signed up for LibsynPro. The LibsynPro show-level stats package includes everything in the basic and advanced stats packages and more.  

To view your LibsynPro show-level stats:

  1. Click Stats at the top of your Libsyn account.   

  2. Set your date range. (See Review the Statistics That Come in the Basic Package.)  

  3. Scroll down to the bottom of the page to see the tables that are only available with LibsynPro. 

    All tables include the following functionality:

    • a.: Click Previous or Next, or click a page number to scroll through the list of items.
    • b.: Click the icon and enter the first few letters of the name of the item in the Search bar. The system filters the items as you type.
    • c.: Click to download stats for this table. See Download Stats Reports.

If you have admin access for a LibsynPro network, you can review your bandwidth data – that is, how much data your audience downloaded from your network’s shows. LibsynPro networks have unlimited upload space, but bandwidth usage can be factored into the price you pay for your network. You can view the bandwidth usage across your entire network, as well as for each show, displayed in gigabytes.

When you first open the Bandwidth page, you can see an overview of your network bandwidth usage for the current month as well as the previous two months.

The GB Per Show table lists all of the shows on your network. The table lists the bandwidth usage in gigabytes transferred for the three most recent months, as well as all-time totals.

In the GB Per Show table, you can:

When you’re done selecting dates, click Select Dates in the upper-right of the calendar to apply them.
